# Linux Syscall Compliance Matrix

Primary source: `https://raw.githubusercontent.com/torvalds/linux/master/arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` (`arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`, native `common`/`64` rows only; `x32` rows excluded).
Local cross-check: `/usr/src/kernels/6.19.6-100.fc42.x86_64/arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl`.

Generation rule: syscall numbers, ABI tags, names, and Linux entry points come from Linux source. Oxide source is used only for current-route annotation and subsystem impact mapping.

Generated rows: 385. Current main annotation: `2048296e0` (`B1245-sioc-ifmetric`).
Local cross-check delta: missing-from-local=471:rseq_slice_yield; extra-local=none.

### Completeness invariant (`F789`, retracting `F784`)

**`F784`'s finding was wrong and its rows are withdrawn.** It reported that the
table held 319 of 385 native syscalls and appended 66 `NEEDS-AUDIT` rows for the
"missing" ones. The matrix was never incomplete: it tracked all 385 the whole
time. The 66 were an artefact of the checker, which split rows on `|` without
honouring the markdown `\|` escape and so silently dropped every row whose notes
contained a pipe. `F789` removed the 65 duplicate rows this created (a 66th, row
319, was removed by the `F783` lane) and escaped the 46 rows whose notes really
did carry a bare `|`.

Two lessons, both encoded in `tools/matrix-lint.py` so they cannot recur:

- **A parser that drops rows it cannot understand reports absence, not error.**
  The naive split did not fail on a pipe-bearing row; it skipped it. Absence then
  reads as "untracked". The lint counts rows it *rejects* and fails on them.
- **`F784` verified with the parser that produced its own error**, then reported
  "insertions only, zero deletions, so nothing was disturbed". The diffstat was
  true and the conclusion false: a duplicate row is an insertion. A check must be
  able to observe the failure mode it is chosen to rule out (`docs/40`).

`make matrix-gate` (in `make ci`) enforces: exactly one row per syscall number,
14 escape-aware columns on every row, and a status drawn from the legend. Its
`--self-test` fixtures inject each defect alone and require that defect's sole
diagnostic, so one rule cannot masquerade as another rule's positive control.

Derivation rules for the route column, which `F784` did get right and are worth
keeping:

- **nr / ABI / name** — the `common` and `64` rows of `syscall_64.tbl`; `x32`
  excluded.
- **Route** — a match arm whose left side names the `NR_*` constant, preferring
  an arm under `syscalls/src/dispatch/`. Three exclusions each cost a false
  positive: `nrs.rs` (a declaration, not a route); `sched/src/diag/syscall_names.rs`
  (maps a constant to a *string*); and arms whose right side is a bare string
  literal. Scanning only `syscalls/src/dispatch/` reports five spurious
  `DISPATCH-GAP`s — `94:lchown`, `117:setresuid`, `126:capset`, `248:add_key`,
  `249:request_key` — which are routed from `syscalls/src/perms.rs`,
  `sched/src/cred/dispatch.rs` and `fs/src/keyring.rs`.

## Status Legend

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `NEEDS-AUDIT` | Linux has a real entry; Oxide has a visible route, but semantic parity is not proven in this matrix. |
| `PARTIAL` | A committed fix/audit exists for part of the Linux contract, but the row still has remaining semantic or harness gaps. |
| `IMPL` | Full Linux-row semantics are implemented and backed by the required harness evidence. |
| `DISPATCH-GAP` | Linux has a real entry; Oxide lacks a visible `NR_*` constant or dispatch route. |
| `LINUX-ENOSYS` | Linux source intentionally has no real native implementation for this slot. Oxide must return `-ENOSYS`, not a stub success. |
| `IN-PROGRESS` | Local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall family. |
| `NEEDS-REWORK` | Committed work landed, but the row is deliberately NOT closed: a known defect, unverified claim, or architectural constraint recorded in the notes must be resolved before it can be `IMPL`. Deliberately chosen over `IMPL` so the findings stay visible and the rework starts from full context instead of re-deriving it. |

## Audit Rules

| Rule | Requirement |
|---|---|
| Row authority | Add/remove/renumber rows only by re-reading Linux `syscall_64.tbl`. |
| Semantic authority | Verify behavior against Linux implementation source named in `Linux refs`, not against local notes. |
| Harness authority | A row is not complete until its required harness covers success, error ordering, permissions, ownership, namespace, symlink/hardlink, tmpfs/dev/proc/sysfs, and user-copy fault cases relevant to its systems. |
| No fallback truth | Oxide route presence only means code is reachable; it is not evidence of Linux compatibility. |

| 0 | common | `read` | `sys_read` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, procfs, sched, socket, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:23` | `IMPL` | `B719-syscall-000-read-complete; B853-vsock-final-file-release` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(read), fs/read_write.c:ksys_read, fs/read_write.c:vfs_read, fs/read_write.c:rw_verify_area, include/linux/fs.h:MAX_RW_COUNT, net/socket.c:sock_read_iter, net/socket.c:sock_recvmsg`; B854-socket-file-ownership; B868-network-blocked-io-close | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_read_shape.rs; vfs::file_read; net hosted suite; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `read(2)` audit against local Linux source. Syscall fd lookup now precedes user-buffer validation; no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd return `EBADF` before `EFAULT`; `FMODE_READ` rejects write-only fds before touching the user buffer; zero-length reads still validate fd/mode and call the backend; original count is user-access validated before Linux `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp; backend sees the clamped count; negative-count-shaped u64 counts fault through access validation before clamp, matching Linux access_ok-before-rw_verify ordering on x86_64. VSOCK read now stays on the originally pinned File through VFS dispatch, so fd close/reuse cannot redirect the operation. Netlink read stays on the netlink receive queue path. Socket VFS read no longer returns blanket `EINVAL`: stream sockets use stream readers, datagram/packet sockets consume one message through the same receive core as `recvfrom(..., src=NULL)`, and fresh/listening TCP returns `ENOTCONN` like Linux. VFS read hooks still emit access notification for positive reads; syscall-level task I/O accounting now updates `rchar`/`syscr`, and procfs exposes dynamic `/proc/<pid>/io` plus `/proc/self/io`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_read_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs file_read`; `cargo test -p net`; `cargo check -p fs`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. B854 keeps NETLINK read on the same pinned File through queue dispatch and proves close/reuse cannot retarget the operation. B868 adds lock-coupled terminal receive waits for VSOCK, NETLINK, and AF_UNIX while retaining the original File across close/reuse. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL (x86): **DIVERGES**. Blocking `read` on a pipe RESUMES correctly under SA_RESTART (`wdiff\|fd\|pipe_read_sarestart\|outcome=ok\|payload=1`, MATCHES) but returns **ENOSYS** without it, where Linux returns EINTR (`pipe_read_norestart`). This pair is the DISCRIMINATOR that exonerated the interrupter and implicated the syscall-return tail for the whole ENOSYS family. Separately, tty job control does NOT resume: a backgrounded pgrp read that takes SIGTTIN never returns after `tcsetpgrp`+SIGCONT (`wdiff\|jobctl\|read_resumes_after_fg\|outcome=timeout` vs oracle `data rc=3`) — F749's headline fix, unverified until now. Plan §17 D1/D4. |
| 1 | common | `write` | `sys_write` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `B720-syscall-001-write-complete` | `fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(write), fs/read_write.c:ksys_write, fs/read_write.c:vfs_write, fs/read_write.c:rw_verify_area, fs/read_write.c:generic_write_check_limits, fs/read_write.c:generic_write_checks; fs/super.c:freeze_super, fs/super.c:sb_start_write, fs/super.c:thaw_super; include/asm-generic/access_ok.h:__access_ok; net/socket.c:sock_write_iter; net/ipv4/udp.c:udp_sendmsg; net/unix/af_unix.c:unix_dgram_sendmsg`; B868-network-blocked-io-close | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_write_shape.rs; vfs::sb_freeze_write; vfs::file_write_limits; net hosted suite; netlink VFS write harness; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `write(2)` audit against local Linux source. Syscall fd lookup precedes user-buffer validation; no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd return `EBADF` before `EFAULT`; `FMODE_WRITE` rejects read-only/O_PATH fds before touching the buffer; zero-length writes validate fd/mode and call the backend; original count is user-access validated before Linux `MAX_RW_COUNT` clamp; backend sees the clamped count. Source buffer now prevalidates a readable VMA to avoid kernel page faults until extable copy-from-user recovery exists; target checks compile the real path. Task I/O accounting updates `wchar`/`syscw`; existing VFS hooks update mtime/ctime and inotify for positive writes. Prior B715 work completed RLIMIT_FSIZE, `s_maxbytes`, socket, datagram, IPv6 UDP, AF_UNIX, and netlink write parity. Freeze is now Linux-shaped: new regular-file writers sleep while frozen; thaw wakes them; `freeze_super` drains existing writers before `freeze_fs`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_write_shape`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_read_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs --test sb_freeze_write`; `cargo test -p vfs --test superblock_freeze`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_write_limits`; `cargo test -p net`; `cargo test -p netlink vfs_write_dispatches_netlink_request_and_queues_reply`; `cargo check -p fs`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. B868 adds bounded AF_UNIX send queues and lock-coupled TCP/UNIX writer waits with terminal state published before wake. |
| 2 | common | `open` | `sys_open` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:25` | `IMPL` | `B721-syscall-002-open-complete` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/open.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(open), fs/open.c:do_sys_open, fs/open.c:build_open_how, fs/open.c:build_open_flags, include/linux/file.h:FD_ADD, fs/file.c:get_unused_fd_flags, fs/file.c:fd_install, fs/namei.c:do_open, fs/namei.c:may_open, fs/namei.c:path_openat` | `vfs::install_open_hooks; vfs::fdtable_reserve_install; vfs::file_opath_ops; vfs::file_mnt_readonly; vfs::path_total_max; vfs::path_name_max; fs_syscall_model::syscall_shape_covers_udev_runtime_and_user_permissions; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | Completed Linux `open(2)` audit against upstream Linux source. Legacy `open` delegates to shared `openat(AT_FDCWD, path, flags, mode)` like Linux `do_sys_open`; prior B716 work completed `build_open_how` legacy masking, `O_PATH` stripping, `O_TMPFILE`, `O_CREAT\|O_DIRECTORY`, `O_DIRECTORY` lookup, sync flag normalization, existing `O_CREAT` directory `EISDIR`, `OPENAT2_REGULAR`, and sticky-dir existing-target checks. This branch fixed the remaining fd-install path to match Linux `FD_ADD`: reserve fd with `get_unused_fd_flags` before open side effects, publish with `fd_install`, rollback with `put_unused_fd` on every failure, and return `EMFILE` before `f_op->open`/truncate side effects. It also removed the syscall-layer pre-call to `inode->i_fop->open`; `install_open_at` now owns the single Linux `do_dentry_open` open hook, skips it for `O_PATH`, exposes the final `File` to `on_open_file`, and propagates `O_TRUNC` backend errors before fd publication. Shared pathname fetch preserves Linux `EFAULT`/`ENOENT`/`ENAMETOOLONG` and byte-count semantics through `read_user_path` and VFS path gates. Evidence: `cargo test -p vfs --test install_open_hooks`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fdtable_reserve_install`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_opath_ops`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_mnt_readonly`; `cargo test -p vfs --test path_total_max`; `cargo test -p vfs --test path_name_max`; `cargo test -p fs --test fs_syscall_model`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. |
| 3 | common | `close` | `sys_close` | fd-table | fd-table, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:78` | `IMPL` | `B718-syscall-003-close-linux-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/open.c:1496, fs/open.c:1460, fs/file.c:878, fs/file.c:733` | `fs::sys_close_shape::{sys_close_uses_current_fdtable_and_removes_before_return, sys_close_matches_linux_unsigned_int_fd_truncation, sys_close_without_current_or_fdtable_is_ebadf}; vfs::tests::file_fd::{fdtable_close_invalid_fd, fdtable_close_then_realloc_fills_hole, fdtable_flush_fires_on_close, fdtable_close_returns_flush_error_after_removing_fd, fdtable_close_flushes_snapshotted_file_ops}; vfs::file_fput_release` | Audited Linux `close`/`filp_flush`/`file_close_fd`/`close_fd`; fixed close so flush errno returns after the fd table entry is already removed, fixed `File::flush` to dispatch through the open file description's snapshotted `file->f_op` instead of the inode's current ops, and added a syscall-context harness proving current-task fd-table use, no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd `EBADF`, Linux unsigned-int fd truncation, and remove-before-return behavior. Last-ref release remains separate from per-close flush. No active restart-internal errno gap exists because Oxide has no internal `ERESTART*` errno variants; backend-visible `EINTR` propagates as `EINTR`. | 7 | common | `poll` | `sys_poll` | vfs | fd-table, fs-special, poll, rlimit, signal, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs, waitqueue | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:42` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/select.c:860, fs/select.c:872, fs/select.c:974, fs/select.c:1060, include/uapi/asm-generic/poll.h:11`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `fd-vfs-io-model; crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_poll_shape.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/poll_syscall_shape.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/poll_subs_mask.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/poll_subs_exclusive.rs` | Audited against Linux `do_pollfd`/`do_sys_poll`/`SYSCALL_DEFINE3(poll)`: `nfds` is bounded by the current task's `RLIMIT_NOFILE`, user pollfds are copied in before fd-table lookup, only `revents` are copied out after polling, copyout faults surface after readiness evaluation, negative fds are ignored, non-negative bad fds report `POLLNVAL`, requested events are filtered while `POLLERR/HUP/NVAL` remain always-reportable, and `poll(NULL, 0, -1)` parks until a deliverable signal instead of returning immediately. Direct hosted syscall harness covers no-current/rlimit ordering, copyin-before-fdtable, missing fd-table `EBADF`, delayed copyout, copyout `EFAULT`, bad/negative fd behavior, timeout-zero, and zero-fd indefinite wait interrupted by signal; VFS harnesses cover readiness masks and subscriber wake semantics. B854 snapshots every initially resolved File once; subscriptions, readiness rescans, and source deadlines use only retained Files, preserving negative, duplicate, and `POLLNVAL` entries across close/reuse. F737 moves the shared poll engine onto an absolute monotonic end_time and onto `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict`, Linux `do_poll`'s break order: readiness, then a deliverable signal, then the expired deadline, so `poll(fds, n, 0)` with a pending signal is EINTR as `count = -ERESTARTNOHAND` makes it. `signal_pending` is `Task::deliverable_signals`, the shared `sig_ignored`-filtered rule. `revents` are copied out unconditionally after `do_poll` returns, so an interrupted poll zeroes them as Linux does. F743 completes the restart half. `do_poll` now ends an interrupted scan with Linux's `-ERESTARTNOHAND` (`fs/select.c:936-940`) instead of folding it to `-EINTR` inside the engine, and `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(poll)`'s tail (`fs/select.c:1074-1087`) converts that into an armed `do_restart_poll` block plus `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK`. poll(2) is the one member of this family that needs a block: `int timeout_msecs` cannot carry the residual timeout back the way ppoll's timespec does, so a restart without one would sleep the full duration again. The block stores ufds, nfds, has_timeout and the ABSOLUTE end_time, and the continuation re-arms itself exactly as `do_restart_poll` does. Slot 219 dispatches it. Hosted: `fs::sys_poll_shape` covers the armed payload for both the infinite and the finite timeout. |
| 8 | common | `lseek` | `sys_lseek` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:46` | `IMPL` | `B726-syscall-008-lseek-complete` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:122, fs/read_write.c:145, fs/read_write.c:161, fs/read_write.c:387, fs/read_write.c:395, fs/read_write.c:412` | `fd-vfs-io-model; crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_lseek_shape.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/lseek_syscall_shape.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/file_seek_negative.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/tests/file_seek_espipe.rs` | Completed Linux `lseek(2)` audit against upstream `ksys_lseek`/`vfs_llseek`/`generic_file_llseek_size`: fd lookup precedes whence validation, no-current/no-fd-table/bad-fd paths return `EBADF`, syscall fd argument truncates as Linux `unsigned int`, live bad-whence returns `EINVAL` before seekability, nonseekable valid whence returns `ESPIPE`, rejected negative/overflow seeks preserve `f_pos`, and generic non-sparse `SEEK_SET/CUR/END/DATA/HOLE` behavior matches Linux. No usercopy is involved in `lseek(2)`, and 64-bit x86_64/aarch64 `off_t`/return width does not hit Linux's 32-bit-only `EOVERFLOW` branch. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_lseek_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs --test lseek_syscall_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_seek_negative`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_seek_espipe`; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks. |

The syscall restores the arch `rt_sigframe` register image through HAL, prevalidates the user frame range against readable VMAs before kernel reads, restores the signal mask through Linux-shaped `set_current_blocked` so `SIGKILL`/`SIGSTOP` remain unblockable, and maps malformed frames to forced `SIGSEGV` instead of a returned errno. aarch64 `sigcontext.__reserved` now matches the 4096-byte Linux UAPI field, x86_64/aarch64 frame-range helpers keep layout authority in HAL, and aarch64 restore uses the same per-task SVC-frame slot as delivery. B1257 resolves `__kernel_rt_sigreturn` from the mapped AArch64 vDSO and stores it in the mm; delivery uses that kernel-owned entry rather than glibc's intentionally zero `sa_restorer`. B1459 closed three signal-frame privilege holes reported by the sched audit. (1) aarch64 `restore_signal_frame` stored the user `sigcontext.pstate` straight into the SVC frame's SPSR_EL1 slot, which the exit path `msr spsr_el1`s before `eret` — a forged `M[3:0]=0b0101` ran the process's own code at EL1; it now goes through `hal::uregs::aarch64::sanitize_native_pstate`, a port of Linux `valid_user_regs`/`valid_native_regs` (`SPSR_EL1_AARCH64_RES0_BITS` mask + EL0t/AArch64/DAIF-clear check + `user_regs_reset_single_step`), and a rejected PSTATE takes the existing badframe path to forced SIGSEGV exactly as Linux's `err \|= !valid_user_regs()` → `goto badframe`. (2) x86_64 wrote the user `sigcontext.eflags` verbatim into the r11 slot `sysretq` reloads; SYSRET's own mask (`R11 & 3C7FD7H`, Intel SDM) passes IOPL, IF, NT and TF, so any process could grant itself IOPL=3 (port I/O + `cli`) or return with interrupts disabled. Now `(cur & ~FIX_EFLAGS) \| (user & FIX_EFLAGS)` per `arch/x86/kernel/signal_64.c:84`. (3) `build_signal_frame` had no `access_ok`: the frame address derives from the user's own SP (or `sigaltstack` `ss_sp`), so `mov rsp,<kernel VA>; syscall` made the kernel write a 560 B / 4.6 KB attacker-shaped frame at an arbitrary kernel address. Placement is now the pure `sigframe_range()` that `fs::sig_dispatch` validates with `validate_user_buf` (range+alignment, i.e. `access_ok` — deliberately NOT the VMA-walking variant, since a frame legitimately lands below a `MAP_GROWSDOWN` stack VMA's current start) BEFORE any mask/altstack mutation (Linux's `setup_rt_frame`-then-`signal_delivered` order), with `build_signal_frame` returning `#[must_use] bool` and re-checking; failure force-SIGSEGVs. Both EFLAGS and PSTATE rules now have ONE owner, the ungated host-tested `crates/arch/hal/src/uregs.rs`, shared with `ptrace(2)` so the two cannot drift. Also mirrored `handle_signal`'s `regs->flags &= ~(DF \| RF \| TF)` for handler entry (a handler was being entered with the interrupted DF, against the SysV ABI), Linux `setup_return`'s TCO clear on arm64, and `frame_uc_flags()`'s `UC_SIGCONTEXT_SS \| UC_STRICT_RESTORE_SS`. |

B769 fixes Linux queue-count ioctl parity: regular-file `FIONREAD` stays `i_size - f_pos`, while non-char `FIONREAD`/`SIOCOUTQ` now routes through `vfs::IoctlIntCmd` and `FileOps::ioctl_int` instead of a generic `poll()` boolean/zero fallback. Pipe/FIFO `FIONREAD` reports queued bytes; inet/unix/packet sockets, netlink, and vsock report backend queue counts where the local model has them; unsupported backends return `ENOTTY` before copyout. B768 fixes Linux `fs/ioctl.c` `FIOASYNC` parity: `ioctl_fioasync` reads the user int, treats same-state requests as success, and changes `FASYNC` only through the open file's snapshotted `file_operations::fasync`; unsupported state changes now return `ENOTTY` without changing flags or generic-registering async delivery. VFS now owns a real `FileOps::fasync_file` hook, with pipe/FIFO and socket/netlink/vsock backends opting into the generic fasync registry. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_fasync_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_fowner -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs pipe -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B767 fixes Linux `fs/ioctl.c` default-arm regular-file gate for `FIBMAP` and legacy `FS_IOC_*SP` preallocation ioctls: only regular files now take the regular-file `file_ioctl` path; directories, symlinks, and other non-regular files fall through without usercopy or backend `bmap`/`fallocate` side effects. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_regular_gate_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B766 fixes Linux `FIOQSIZE` parity from `fs/ioctl.c`: accepted directories/regular files/symlinks now return allocated inode bytes (`i_blocks * 512`, matching local `inode_get_bytes` shape) rather than logical size, with hosted coverage for regular sparse-shaped files, directories, symlinks, and special-file `ENOTTY` without user copyout. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_fioqsize_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p fs --test sys_ioctl_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p vfs --test fileattr_prepare -- --nocapture`; x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; aarch64 target check remains blocked before this patch by missing `vdso-aarch64.so` because `vendor/cross/aarch64-linux-musl-cross/bin/aarch64-linux-musl-gcc` is absent. B765 fixes ext4 fileattr admission from Linux `ext4_fileattr_set`: unsupported translated flags are rejected before the backend masks to user-modifiable bits, so unsupported DAX through `FS_IOC_SETFLAGS`/`FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` no longer succeeds silently or mutates nothing. B764 fixes ext4 fileattr parity from Linux `ext4_ioctl_check_immutable` and `EXT4_FL_USER_VISIBLE`: immutable ext4 inodes now reject other flag/project-id changes while immutable remains set, `EXT4_EXTENTS_FL` is visible through `FS_IOC_GETFLAGS`, normal flag updates preserve it, and unsupported extent-layout toggles fail instead of clearing the on-disk extent bit. B763 adds Linux-shaped ext4 PROJECT RO_COMPAT admission plus journaled `i_projid` and ctime persistence for `FS_IOC_FSSETXATTR` on PROJECT filesystems, with in-memory PROJECT-bit image harness coverage. Prior B734-B744 covered common VFS dispatch, fileattr, remap admission, ext4 no-remap proof, and tty exclusive ioctls. Remaining before `IMPL`: filesystem-specific DAX/casefold support where supported by the backing filesystem, filesystem-specific remap backends only for filesystems that actually advertise Linux remap/reflink support, and remaining driver/file-specific ioctl families. STATUS CORRECTED IN-PROGRESS->PARTIAL (D394): the branch named in the Branch column is merged and deleted, so the work landed long ago. IN-PROGRESS means 'local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall' -- leaving it set on merged work tells the next agent a live lane owns this row, which is exactly how duplicate lanes get opened. PARTIAL is the honest reading: a committed fix exists, full Linux parity is not proven. B1583 restructures ioctl dispatch to Linux's order after finding a second non-Linux-shaped path: `handle_file_ioctl` (the `ext4_ioctl` set) ran as stage two ahead of every per-fd-kind handler, returning ENOTTY for its command numbers on ANY fd, so e.g. `FS_IOC_GETVERSION` on an epoll fd got ENOTTY where Linux returns EINVAL. There was also no `IS_ANON_FILE` analogue, so `FIONREAD` on userfaultfd/perf/io_uring fds (tagged Regular) was answered by the generic stage with a fabricated `i_size - f_pos` of 0 before the fd's own handler ran. New ungated `syscalls::ioctl_owner` is a single table consulted once, returning `None` (Linux `-ENOIOCTLCMD`) for anything the generic stage does not own; `handle_file_ioctl` moved after the per-fd-kind handlers and gated on non-anon; `S_ANON_INODE` added to vfs, which required collapsing three byte-identical per-crate copies of the anon-inodefs `d_op` static into one VFS-owned static since the marker is pointer identity. `FIOQSIZE` is deliberately asymmetric with `FIONREAD` — Linux's generic stage owns it for every type and answers ENOTTY itself; an existing shape test caught that. B1972 re-verified the three items this row held open and closes all three as vacuous rather than outstanding. Closed in B1972: the DAX item -- the reference's ext4 accepts the DAX file attribute and reports success even when the backing device has no DAX capability, because the device capability gates only whether the inode is wired for DAX, never the errno; oxide reports success and leaves the bit unpersisted, the same observable outcome, and oxide has no DAX-capable block device to differ over (`ext4::fs_ioc_flags_image ext4_rejects_unsupported_dax_flag_without_corruption`). The remap item -- the reference's ext4 carries no remap callback, so a clone ioctl on ext4 is EOPNOTSUPP upstream, and oxide answers EOPNOTSUPP by the same absent-callback route (`ext4::remap_unsupported_image`, `vfs::FileOps::supports_remap_file_range` defaulting false with no real backend overriding it); no filesystem oxide has advertises remap upstream either, so the item names work no filesystem here can owe. The driver/file-specific item -- `016_ioctl/core.rs` is a single dispatcher covering every fd kind this kernel has, whose fallback ENOTTY matches the reference's answer for an fd kind carrying no ioctl callback, and inspection produced no fd kind answering a wrong errno. One owner table confirmed: a single `sys_ioctl`, with the per-fd-kind command matches reached FROM it rather than competing with it. Casefold is fileattr flag translation only for both ext4 and tmpfs, which this row's prose implied more of; filed as its own row in `scratch/issues.d/B1972-syscall-abi-partial-rows.md` rather than left implied here. |

VFS positional read now matches Linux `generic_read_dir` by returning `EISDIR` for directories. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_pread64_shape`; `cargo test -p vfs --test file_positional_io`; `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zbuild-std-features=compiler-builtins-mem -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls --target targets/x86_64-unknown-oxide-kernel.json`; same aarch64 target check. |

B854 keeps writev on its original File, gives NETLINK exactly one datagram without flattening, and gives pipes one ring-lock operation so writes at or below `PIPE_BUF` are atomic and `O_DIRECT` packet boundaries follow the original iovecs. B868 applies the same pinned-File and blocked-send guarantees to vectored TCP/AF_UNIX writes. |

B854 snapshots each selected File once; subscriptions, readiness rescans, and deadlines cannot be redirected by concurrent close/reuse. `pselect6` uses the same corrected engine. F737 moves the shared select engine onto `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict`, Linux `do_select` plus `core_sys_select` break order, and onto `Task::deliverable_signals` for `signal_pending`; the timeval writeback now goes through the shared `poll_select_finish` rule, honouring the `STICKY_TIMEOUTS` persona and the zero-timeout no-update case. F743: `do_select`/`core_sys_select` end an interrupted wait with `-ERESTARTNOHAND` (`fs/select.c:681-686`) rather than a folded `-EINTR`, so the no-handler cases Linux restarts (SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, a job-control stop) restart here too; the timeval writeback that makes that restart correct already runs. select(2) arms no restart block, matching `kern_select`. The `sticky:` fold (`fs/select.c:361-363`) is now applied: `-ERESTARTNOHAND` becomes `-EINTR` exactly when the residual timeout could not reach userspace (STICKY_TIMEOUTS persona or a faulting copyout), because a call whose timeout was not updated cannot be restarted. Owned by the shared `pselect_ppoll::{wait_verdict, timeout_writeback_plan, finish_return}` rules. |

Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_pause_shape -- --nocapture`; `cargo test -p syscall restart -- --nocapture`; x86_64/aarch64 `cargo check -Zbuild-std=core,alloc,compiler_builtins -Zjson-target-spec -p syscalls`; `git diff --check`. F743 removes the private signal triage: `pause_wake` was one of three divergent copies of the same rule. All three now call `Task::sleep_wake`, the single owner beside `Task::deliverable_signals`, so pause, nanosleep and clock_nanosleep cannot drift. Behaviour is unchanged for pause; the shared rule additionally fixes a masked-SIGKILL hole (below, row 35). B1456 removed pause's private in-loop `stop_until_cont_sig(); continue;` (both park-loop arms): it consumed the stop signal the tail must dequeue and resumed in place, so the ERESTARTNOHAND restart decision never ran. `SleepWake::Stop` is gone from the enum, which makes the shape unrepresentable. |

The private `sleep_wake` copy is gone in favour of `Task::sleep_wake`. Hosted: `fs::sys_nanosleep_shape` grew coverage for the ABS/REL split, the remainder-resume across two interruptions, and the ignored-signal rule; `sched::task::sigwake` adds 8 triage tests. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL (x86, `userspace/wait_diff`): **DIVERGES**. Interrupted relative sleep returns `rc=0` where the host Linux oracle returns `-1/EINTR` (`wdiff\|sleep\|rel_norestart`, `rel_sarestart`, raw `syscall(SYS_clock_nanosleep)` — not the glibc wrapper, which returns the errno instead of setting it). Remainder writeback itself is correct (`rem_lt_req=1`, `rem_gt_zero=1`); the return value is not. Also pins the semantic the campaign was commissioned against: `SA_RESTART` changes NOTHING for the sleep family (`signal(7)` never-restarted list), so the two records are deliberately identical. Linux `do_nanosleep` loops on `t->task && !signal_pending(current)` (`kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2404`) and a stop signal satisfies `signal_pending`, so the loop EXITS and the interrupted tail runs — `nanosleep_copyout` to `rmtp`, `set_restart_fn` with the absolute expiry, `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` — with the stop taken afterwards in `get_signal` and SIGCONT resuming via `restart_syscall(2)`. Stopping in-loop reached neither `write_remaining` nor `arm_restart_block`. `wdiff\|sleep\|stopcont_restart_block` now MATCHES the oracle on x86 AND aarch64 (`rem_written=1`), taking the whole differential to 29/29 identical records on both arches. |

Remaining: remaining family/error behavior, packet-observation parity, and syscall differential coverage. B854 resolves one send target and derives nonblocking state from its retained File, including INET, NETLINK, UNIX, and VSOCK dispatch. B868 adds bounded atomic AF_UNIX datagram/seqpacket sends, partial stream progress, and shutdown/final-release writer wakes. B1624: a netlink framing error FAILED THE SEND, so `ip addr`/`ip route` reported "Cannot send dump request: Invalid argument" — iproute2 sends `nlmsg_len` bytes of request followed by its zeroed unused buffer, and `msg_len > consumed - off` rejected the padding after the dump had already been answered and its reply built. Linux's netlink core discards the receive path's verdict entirely (`netlink_unicast_kernel` returns `skb->len` regardless), so no framing condition may ever fail the send. New ungated `netlink::rcv_skb` owns the walk: a short tail, an undersized `nlmsg_len`, or one past the datagram ends the walk silently; a final message whose ALIGNED length overruns clamps the advance per `netlink_rcv_skb`; reserved types below `NLMSG_MIN_TYPE` and non-`NLM_F_REQUEST` messages never reach a handler and are acked only under `NLM_F_ACK` (NETLINK_AUDIT exempted, as it walks the datagram itself). New ungated `netlink::sockaddr` replaces three `sockaddr_nl` decoders that disagreed — one returned EAFNOSUPPORT for a foreign family where Linux gives EINVAL, one never checked the family, and none reduced `nl_groups` to the single group a send reaches; a latent port/group destructuring swap at three call sites is now unrepresentable via a typed `NlDest`. Harness: `cargo test -p netlink` 166/0 (was 147); guest-verified on BOTH arches — `ip addr`, `ip link`, `ip route`, `ip -6 addr` all exit 0 with full output. B1641: see rows 54/55. AF_UNIX `MSG_OOB` IS implemented — the earlier absent-feature note was recorded from a grep against a path that does not exist (`net/src/unix/`; the subsystem is `net/src/unix_sock/`), and a nonexistent-path grep reads exactly like a missing feature. B1943: out-of-band arrival now signals the receiving description's `f_owner` with `SIGURG`, unconditionally — neither `O_ASYNC` nor a fasync registration is required, so the canonical `fcntl(F_SETOWN)` + `MSG_OOB` flow, which previously signalled NOTHING, now delivers. One delivery owner (`vfs::File::send_sigurg`, reusing the installed SIGIO hook) reached through one shared socket-layer trigger (`net::sock::oob_notify::sk_send_sigurg`) that both AF_UNIX and TCP call; the fasync half keeps its separate owner, the `POLL_PRI` readiness wake, and its rule that a plain `SIGURG` is suppressed without `F_SETSIG`. Evidence: `vfs::tests::file_send_sigurg` 7, `net::unix_sock::tests::sigurg` 4 (real ring send, end to end), `net::sock::oob_notify::tests` 5, `net::stack::tcp::urgent_tests` 5; positive controls RED then green for each. F793 audited this row for promotion while building the mmsg host-oracle corpus and did NOT promote it: the AF_UNIX MSG_OOB gap above is a missing implementation, not a coverage gap, and the send security hook plus per-family error behavior still have no evidence. Nothing in F793 touches the sendto path. B1952 closes the send/receive security-hook gap named on rows 44/46/47/299/307. There was no single decision: the generic per-operation verdict was evaluated in nine places at four depths and the sandbox's address verdict in a tenth, and they disagreed about ordering — a send was refused only AFTER its family had answered ENODATA/EOPNOTSUPP/EINVAL, and a blocking receive re-asked its verdict once per wakeup rather than once per transaction. One boundary (`net::socket_security`) now composes sandbox then module registry and is the only place either is consulted for a message; `socket::security::admit` is the one send call site (top of `send::prepare`, ahead of every family validation, plus the two out-of-band envelope paths) and `syscalls::recv_admit::admit_and_route` the one receive call site — the protocol owner is obtainable ONLY from that call, so no receive can reach a protocol unadmitted. Per-family and per-transport copies deleted from `net::sock::sendto`, `net::sock_io::recvfrom_opts`, `recvmsg::{inet,netlink,vsock}`, `unix_recv`, and `socket::send`'s four inline sites. Two behaviour fixes fall out: a STREAM send naming an address no longer asks for the connect right (it settles no new port; asking denied sends a sandboxed program may make), and an out-of-band send with no urgent channel imports its envelope before the hook so unreadable address memory reports EFAULT, not a permission answer. The sandbox snapshot now travels in `SendContext` so one batch cannot be judged against two policies. Harness: `net::socket_security::tests` 6, `syscalls::recv_admit::tests` 4, `socket::security_hooks_tests` 5 (which replace three tests that asserted on the SOURCE TEXT of `send.rs`/`address.rs` and could not fail for any behavioural regression). Positive controls, each RED then green: deleting the call from `send::prepare` reddens all five send tests; deleting it from `admit_and_route` reddens two receive tests; restoring the transport-derived send right reddens the stream test; swapping the two modules' order reddens the ordering test. Both arch target checks pass; smoke-x86 PASS 129s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. One consequence needed its own fix: removing the transport's duplicate evaluation let the raw IPv4 branch inline into `net::sock::sendto`, whose frame every other protocol's send then carried — the aarch64 worst-case send path grew 480 B and crossed the 13000 B stack-depth ceiling. The branch and `socket::send::send_inet` are `#[inline(never)]` now and the deepest path is 12960 B (main: 12880 B). B1960 closes this row's per-family error behaviour and corrects its packet-observation claim. The observation claim was STALE: the outgoing tap, the `PACKET_OUTGOING` marking, the own-socket and fanout-group exclusions, `PACKET_IGNORE_OUTGOING`, the `sll_pkttype` classification, `PACKET_ORIGDEV`, the `PACKET_AUXDATA` record with its VLAN fields and outgoing checksum-validity suppression, and the vnet header are all implemented and carry 96 hosted tests. What was NOT implemented is the per-family answer set. An IPv4 datagram send accepted `MSG_OOB` silently where the reference refuses it before it looks at the destination (the IPv6 sender carries no such check, so an AF_INET6 socket reaches it only for a v4-mapped destination, which is the same condition that hands the message to the IPv4 sender). An AF_UNIX byte stream ignored a supplied destination where the reference answers EISCONN when connected and EOPNOTSUPP otherwise, while a seqpacket discards `msg_namelen` and never looks. An AF_UNIX socket with no peer reported EDESTADDRREQ where the reference reports ENOTCONN. An AF_VSOCK destination was judged by the outer socket variant rather than by connection STATE, so a connect still in flight or one the peer had already reset reported "already connected"; a vsock datagram destination is now shape-checked (EINVAL for an unbound one) instead of refused outright, and vsock runs no ancillary rule at all, which is what the reference does. AF_PACKET reports ENXIO for a destination no device answers to — `NetError::Enodev` was mapped to ENXIO for EVERY family, so the packet answer was right only by conflation and an IPv6 send naming a missing interface got ENXIO where the reference gives ENODEV; the two are separate variants now. The device-sized `sockaddr_ll` check is asked the moment the device is known and outranks the interface being down. TCP `MSG_OOB` of more than one byte was EINVAL; the reference sends every byte and marks the last urgent, which is the AF_UNIX division reached differently and now expressed through the same plan owner (`socket::oob`), with a zero-length urgent send reporting zero rather than refusing. Differential evidence: new guest probe `t_cmsgfam` runs sixteen per-family sends against the host oracle and PASSES on BOTH arches; the SAME probe against the pre-branch kernel FAILS on nine of sixteen lines, each one a behaviour above. Harness: `socket::tests::family_ancillary` 9, `socket::vsock_addr::tests` 2, `socket::oob::tests` 6, `net::sock::packet_tests` +1, `net::sock::packet_ring_tx_tests` extended; positive control RED-then-green for each. Both arch target checks pass; stack-gate PASS with the aarch64 worst case at 12720 B against 13000 B (origin/main measured 12960 B on the same gate); smoke-x86 PASS 78s, smoke-arm PASS 155s. B1962 closes both of this row's remaining items. An AF_UNIX SOCK_DGRAM **socketpair** end ignored a supplied destination and delivered to its peer wherever the message was addressed: the whole message-pair family was treated as the stream kind for the name rule, so the datagram half inherited the seqpacket answer. It runs the datagram send, so a supplied name is resolved by the ordinary lookup and outranks the peer the pair was created with; the sending end owns no queue, so it publishes no source address and charges no write memory of its own, and the destination's receive queue is the only bound. The same branch carries the shutdown check that lived on the path-bound datagram arm alone, so a send on a shut-down write side reports EPIPE where it previously reported success. The NETLINK privileged-destination gate was not merely unconfirmed, it was ABSENT: a caller-supplied `sockaddr_nl` naming any port ID or any multicast group bit was decoded and handed straight to delivery on every protocol, by all three send entry points, with no capability consulted anywhere on the path. The reference refuses it with EPERM unless the protocol declares its sends unprivileged — exactly ONE does — or the sender holds CAP_NET_ADMIN in the socket's own namespace; there is no separate group-only capability check, which is what this row's planning assumed. The gate lives with the decode (`netlink::sockaddr::parse_supplied_dest`), so no send path can take one without the other, and `connect` asks the same question because the pair it settles becomes the destination every later destination-less send uses unasked. A destination naming neither a port nor a group stays unprivileged on every protocol, which is the destination every ordinary request uses. Harness: `socket::tests::unix_dgram_pair` 5, `netlink::sockaddr::tests` +4 (`cargo test -p netlink` 290, was 286; `-p socket` 79, was 74); positive controls RED then green for each — keying the datagram-pair branch off `false` reddens 3, neutering the gate condition reddens 2. Both arch target builds pass; stack-gate PASS with the aarch64 worst case at 12864 B against 13000 B; smoke-x86 PASS 58s, smoke-arm PASS 110s (`scratch/issues.d/B1962-unix-dgram-name-and-mark-consumers.md`). |

Linux import order, `MAX_RW_COUNT`, late source-length read/write, null and negative length errors, true-length truncation, payload/address copy-fault queue side effects, `O_NONBLOCK`, `MSG_DONTWAIT`, `MSG_PEEK`, `MSG_TRUNC`, `MSG_WAITALL`, family-specific `MSG_OOB`, and `MSG_ERRQUEUE` interaction are shared across INET/INET6, UNIX, PACKET, NETLINK, and VSOCK. AF_PACKET source copyout uses the same late value-result transaction, preserving `sockaddr_ll` metadata. Four new GNU/glibc records make the 99-record x86 Linux/Oxide differential byte-identical. Hosted syscalls pass 128/128, focused packet receive passes 102/102, both GNU targets compile, and both kernel targets build. The system-wide receive security boundary remains explicitly owned by N19; true TCP urgent-data transport remains N20 and recvmsg control exposure remains N10. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL (x86): **DIVERGES**. Blocking `recv` under SA_RESTART reports EINTR where Linux RESUMES and delivers the payload, on both AF_UNIX and loopback TCP (`wdiff\|fd\|unix_recv_sarestart`, `tcp_recv_sarestart`) — the shape says these sites return a real `-EINTR` rather than `-ERESTARTSYS`, i.e. F748's `sock_intr` conversion is not reaching them at runtime. The no-SA_RESTART and `SO_RCVTIMEO`-timed cases MATCH, but that is not corroboration: EINTR is the right answer there for the wrong reason. Plan §17 D2. |

B1624: a netlink framing error FAILED THE SEND, so `ip addr`/`ip route` reported "Cannot send dump request: Invalid argument" — iproute2 sends `nlmsg_len` bytes of request followed by its zeroed unused buffer, and `msg_len > consumed - off` rejected the padding after the dump had already been answered and its reply built. Linux's netlink core discards the receive path's verdict entirely (`netlink_unicast_kernel` returns `skb->len` regardless), so no framing condition may ever fail the send. New ungated `netlink::rcv_skb` owns the walk: a short tail, an undersized `nlmsg_len`, or one past the datagram ends the walk silently; a final message whose ALIGNED length overruns clamps the advance per `netlink_rcv_skb`; reserved types below `NLMSG_MIN_TYPE` and non-`NLM_F_REQUEST` messages never reach a handler and are acked only under `NLM_F_ACK` (NETLINK_AUDIT exempted, as it walks the datagram itself). New ungated `netlink::sockaddr` replaces three `sockaddr_nl` decoders that disagreed — one returned EAFNOSUPPORT for a foreign family where Linux gives EINVAL, one never checked the family, and none reduced `nl_groups` to the single group a send reaches; a latent port/group destructuring swap at three call sites is now unrepresentable via a typed `NlDest`. Harness: `cargo test -p netlink` 166/0 (was 147); guest-verified on BOTH arches — `ip addr`, `ip link`, `ip route`, `ip -6 addr` all exit 0 with full output. B1641: sendmmsg masked MSG_CMSG_COMPAT off its batch spec AND used it to select a 32-bit header decoder - the canonical guard was unreachable while a caller could change the parsed layout. F793 audited this row for promotion and declined at the time: the named N19 security hooks were then absent, and the ancillary/control differential this row needs is per-family sendmsg coverage, which the mmsg batch corpus does not reach. B1938 audited AF_UNIX `MSG_OOB` on this row and found the send path implemented — see row 44 for the one-byte split rule, the EOPNOTSUPP cases, the ancillary-before-EOPNOTSUPP ordering, the SIGPIPE suppression on the urgent tail, and the harness. The one send-side divergence found is the absent `SIGURG` to the receiving description's `f_owner` on arrival, recorded in `scratch/issues.d/B1938-af-unix-msg-oob.md`. This row's own gaps (N19 security hooks, per-family sendmsg differential) are untouched by B1938. B1942: the compat-layout question moved to ONE owner (`syscalls::msg_layout`), asked once per call from (flags, entry ABI) and answered with a typed `MsgLayout` the importer is driven by — the guard and the layout selector can no longer disagree, which was B1641's shape. The 32-bit ABI behind it is now real: `SendBatchIo`'s `compat` flag was initialised false and never set, so three compat importers were dead code. Completing it fixed a 32-bit sender's ancillary stream being handed to the protocols VERBATIM (12-byte headers on a 4-byte grid parsed as 16-byte headers on an 8-byte grid, so `cmsg_level`/`cmsg_type` came out of the payload); `msg_layout::cmsg::compat_to_native` rebuilds it once. Harness: `syscalls::msg_layout::tests` 17, plus compat coverage in `send_user::tests` (13) and `recv_user::tests` (19); every new test given a positive control (removing the rebuild, collapsing the compat header size, or making the compat entry select the native layout each turns it red). Both arch target checks pass. B1943: out-of-band arrival now signals the receiving description's `f_owner` with `SIGURG`, unconditionally — neither `O_ASYNC` nor a fasync registration is required, so the canonical `fcntl(F_SETOWN)` + `MSG_OOB` flow, which previously signalled NOTHING, now delivers. One delivery owner (`vfs::File::send_sigurg`, reusing the installed SIGIO hook) reached through one shared socket-layer trigger (`net::sock::oob_notify::sk_send_sigurg`) that both AF_UNIX and TCP call; the fasync half keeps its separate owner, the `POLL_PRI` readiness wake, and its rule that a plain `SIGURG` is suppressed without `F_SETSIG`. Evidence: `vfs::tests::file_send_sigurg` 7, `net::unix_sock::tests::sigurg` 4 (real ring send, end to end), `net::sock::oob_notify::tests` 5, `net::stack::tcp::urgent_tests` 5; positive controls RED then green for each. F793 audited this row for promotion and declined at the time: the named N19 security hooks were then absent, and the ancillary/control differential this row needs is per-family sendmsg coverage, which the mmsg batch corpus does not reach. B1952 closes the send/receive security-hook gap named on rows 44/46/47/299/307. There was no single decision: the generic per-operation verdict was evaluated in nine places at four depths and the sandbox's address verdict in a tenth, and they disagreed about ordering — a send was refused only AFTER its family had answered ENODATA/EOPNOTSUPP/EINVAL, and a blocking receive re-asked its verdict once per wakeup rather than once per transaction. One boundary (`net::socket_security`) now composes sandbox then module registry and is the only place either is consulted for a message; `socket::security::admit` is the one send call site (top of `send::prepare`, ahead of every family validation, plus the two out-of-band envelope paths) and `syscalls::recv_admit::admit_and_route` the one receive call site — the protocol owner is obtainable ONLY from that call, so no receive can reach a protocol unadmitted. Per-family and per-transport copies deleted from `net::sock::sendto`, `net::sock_io::recvfrom_opts`, `recvmsg::{inet,netlink,vsock}`, `unix_recv`, and `socket::send`'s four inline sites. Two behaviour fixes fall out: a STREAM send naming an address no longer asks for the connect right (it settles no new port; asking denied sends a sandboxed program may make), and an out-of-band send with no urgent channel imports its envelope before the hook so unreadable address memory reports EFAULT, not a permission answer. The sandbox snapshot now travels in `SendContext` so one batch cannot be judged against two policies. Harness: `net::socket_security::tests` 6, `syscalls::recv_admit::tests` 4, `socket::security_hooks_tests` 5 (which replace three tests that asserted on the SOURCE TEXT of `send.rs`/`address.rs` and could not fail for any behavioural regression). Positive controls, each RED then green: deleting the call from `send::prepare` reddens all five send tests; deleting it from `admit_and_route` reddens two receive tests; restoring the transport-derived send right reddens the stream test; swapping the two modules' order reddens the ordering test. Both arch target checks pass; smoke-x86 PASS 129s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. One consequence needed its own fix: removing the transport's duplicate evaluation let the raw IPv4 branch inline into `net::sock::sendto`, whose frame every other protocol's send then carried — the aarch64 worst-case send path grew 480 B and crossed the 13000 B stack-depth ceiling. The branch and `socket::send::send_inet` are `#[inline(never)]` now and the deepest path is 12960 B (main: 12880 B). B1960 closes this row's per-family ancillary/control differential. The tree ran ONE rule — the SCM one — for every family that is not AF_UNIX, where the reference has three. A UDP sender's `IP_PKTINFO`, `IP_TTL`, `IP_TOS`, `IP_PROTOCOL` and `IP_RETOPTS` were therefore stepped over and DROPPED, while its `SCM_RIGHTS` was refused with EINVAL and its `SCM_CREDENTIALS` was validated against the sender's identity — the reference's answer inverted in both directions — and `SO_MARK`, `SO_PRIORITY`, `SCM_TXTIME`, `SO_TIMESTAMPING_OLD/NEW`, `SCM_TS_OPT_ID` and `SCM_DEVMEM_DMABUF` were EINVAL everywhere. Three owners now: one cursor (`socket::cmsg_walk`) states the framing rules the SCM and IP parsers previously stated separately and were free to disagree about — the minimum header, the `controllen` bound, the alignment step, and the difference between a stream that FAILS the walk and one that merely ENDS it; one generic rule (`socket::sockcm`) owns SOL_SOCKET for every family that does not carry descriptors, including the capability gate on `SO_MARK`, the interactive-band gate on `SO_PRIORITY` and the exact widths; and one selector (`socket::control_family`) decides WHICH rule a socket speaks, keyed on its transport and — for an AF_INET6 datagram socket — on whether its destination is v4-mapped, since that is the same condition that hands the message to the IPv4 sender and therefore to the IPv4 rule. The parsed overrides now reach the wire: `socket_sendto` and `sendto_v6` resolve them against the socket's own choices through named helpers on `Raw4Control`/`Raw6Control`, so each replaces exactly the `setsockopt` counterpart it mirrors for one datagram. Differential evidence: `t_cmsgfam` (see row 44) covers the ancillary half — an unknown type at the transport's own level, a level the transport does not own, an unknown SOL_SOCKET type, `IP_TTL`/`IP_TOS` and their exact widths, and `SCM_RIGHTS` stepped over by a family that cannot carry it. Harness: `socket::cmsg_walk::tests` 6, `socket::sockcm::tests` 7, `socket::tests::family_ancillary` 9, `net::send_control::tests` +5; positive controls RED-then-green for each. B1962 gives the admitted values their consumers and produces the missing differential frame. `SO_MARK`, `SO_PRIORITY`, `SCM_TXTIME` and `SO_TIMESTAMPING` were admitted, screened for width and capability, and then DROPPED — an admitted value with no consumer is a lie told to userspace — and the socket-level `SO_MARK` was in the same state. The overrides now have one holder (`net::send_control::SockCm`, beside the IP-level ones the same walk settles) and one resolver (`net::sock::txmeta::tx_meta`), which answers each question ONCE per message from the socket's own choice and the override settled over it; nothing downstream re-reads the option and no value is mirrored into a second place. Each then reaches the path that acts on it. The mark reaches the transmit route lookup, which already took one and was being asked with zero from every socket send, so a mark-selecting policy rule could not fire for a socket's own packets; the IPv6 policy lookup was worse — it ignored the rule's mark selector outright, and the rule table is shared, so a mark-selecting rule matched EVERY IPv6 packet whatever its mark. The priority selects a transmit band: the default discipline is three FIFO bands drained in order, where the tree had one FIFO and therefore nothing the value could select, and a band fills on its own so a low-priority flood cannot take the slots a higher-priority one would use. The departure time and the mark ride the packet. The timestamping bits publish the transmit record they ask for on the sending socket's error queue — that publisher had NO caller anywhere in the tree — under the key `SCM_TS_OPT_ID` named or the socket's own counter. Two lookups stay deliberately unmarked and say so at the call site through a named constant: a route-metrics or path-MTU query answers about the route rather than about one packet, and TCP output re-resolves per segment from an entry with no access to the socket's option table — the second is a real gap and is recorded as one, because closing it wants the connect-time route cache the reference has, not a mirrored field on the entry, which is the split source of truth this lane removed elsewhere. The differential blocker was not only that the probe's uid was unpinned: a manifest row could DECLARE a uid and have it silently discarded, because both runners parsed the row with an all-or-nothing field-count guard and fell back to `unprivileged` — row 42 had been declaring `root` and running unprivileged the whole time. Short rows now fail the run by name instead of defaulting, and the host oracle runs under the same declared identity as the guest instead of whatever invoked the script. Four new probes carry the two uncovered areas at both privilege levels: `t_cmsgcap`/`t_cmsgcap_priv` for the capability-gated SOL_SOCKET types (the capability is consulted before `SO_MARK`'s width but after `SO_PRIORITY`'s, and the interactive band is the unprivileged one) and `t_cmsgv6`/`t_cmsgv6_priv` for the whole SOL_IPV6 level including its 2292 aliases and the capability-gated extension headers. All four PASS on BOTH arches — guest stdout byte-identical to the host oracle, 15/15/37/37 lines — and the privileged frames differ from the unprivileged ones in exactly the capability-gated lines on host and guest alike, so no kernel divergence is left in either area. Two harness defects fell out and are fixed: the host oracle was written to one arch-independent path, so two concurrent arch lanes raced and one lane's linker truncated the binary the other was executing (aarch64 recorded 1 of 5 oracles on the first concurrent run), and an unpinned host uid would have reported the capability difference itself as a kernel defect on 6 of the 15 SOL_SOCKET cases. Harness: `net::sock::txmeta::tests` 2, `net::sock::tx_tstamp::tests` 2, `net::netdev::tx_band::tests` 2, `net::netdev::tests::tx_dispatch::a_packet_priority_selects_the_band_it_drains_in`, `net::route::policy_tests` +2, `net::raw4::tests::transmit::a_sends_mark_band_and_departure_time_reach_the_packet` (`cargo test -p net` 2198); positive control RED then green for each — collapsing the band map reddens 3, removing the IPv6 mark selector reddens 1, making the override accessor return the socket value reddens 1, dropping the generation-method half of the timestamp condition reddens 1. Both arch target builds pass; stack-gate PASS at 12864 B against 13000 B; smoke-x86 PASS 58s, smoke-arm PASS 110s (`scratch/issues.d/B1962-unix-dgram-name-and-mark-consumers.md`). |

B854 removes remaining NETLINK/VSOCK fd-taking receive wrappers and retains the original File through family dispatch and receive. B855 moves SCM_RIGHTS batch publication into the socket work layer, atomically publishes CLOEXEC descriptors, preserves an installed prefix while rolling back the current reservation and discarding the suffix on EMFILE or copy fault, reports MSG_CTRUNC, leaves MSG_PEEK rights queued while installing duplicate descriptors, drops unread rights outside AF_UNIX queue locks, and immediately runs reentrant lost-wakeup-safe cycle collection. B868 closes NETLINK/VSOCK receive lost-wake windows and adds AF_UNIX shutdown-generation and blocked-receive evidence. `B1637`: `SCM_CREDENTIALS` pins the sender's identity into each message and renders it in the receiver's namespace (Linux `UNIXCB(skb).pid`). `B1637` built the per-namespace pid map: `numbers.resize(depth, parent_visible_tid)` gave EVERY ancestor level a copy of ONE number, there was a single global `NEXT_VPID` counter so a nested namespace never numbered its own tasks, and the whole tree had exactly THREE namespace-aware sites — all input paths. Now `PidNumberSpace` per namespace (cyclic allocation, RESERVED_PIDS floor, per-ns `pid_max`), `alloc_mappings` walking innermost-to-outermost with full unwind on failure at any level, `pid_nr_ns` returning 0 for a non-ancestor, and the global counter deleted so numbers only ever come from the namespace that owns them. Every reporting site now answers in the READER's namespace. Harness: 3665 passed / 0 failed across sched 1055, syscalls 1295, procfs 136, net 1163, namespace-identity 16; 14 new tests over a 3-level nest prove a distinct number per level, 0 for a non-ancestor, and an intermediate reader seeing the INTERMEDIATE number; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 79s. B1641: recvmmsg had sendmmsg's UIO_MAXIOV clamp, no pending-error check, and kept receiving past an OOB message. B1676 closes the recvmmsg batch-rule test gap: the decisions moved to ungated `syscalls::mmsg_batch` and are covered by 16 hosted tests. F793 audited this row for promotion and did NOT promote it: its Remaining list then named behavior absent from the tree (extended-error origins, compat-msghdr ABI, security hooks), which coverage alone could not close. All three have since been built and are evidenced above. F793's host-oracle corpus covers the recvmmsg BATCH rules only; single-message receive semantics are untouched. B1938 RETRACTS "true OOB delivery" from this row's Remaining list: AF_UNIX out-of-band receive is implemented in ungated `net::unix_sock::stream::oob` and driven by `syscalls::unix_recv::recv_urgent`. A stream carries at most one byte awaiting `recv(MSG_OOB)`; the byte is reported with `MSG_OOB` set in the returned flags and never blocks, and both "nothing pending" and "`SO_OOBINLINE` put it in-band" report the same EINVAL. `MSG_PEEK` leaves it queued. A normal receive that already copied bytes HALTS at the boundary rather than gluing across it (including under `MSG_WAITALL`); a receive that copied nothing steps over the record, discarding a non-inline pending byte and delivering it as ordinary data under `SO_OOBINLINE`; a `MSG_PEEK` step retires nothing. A consumed byte leaves a spent record that bounds exactly one further receive, still reports readable while reporting zero readable bytes, and carries the descriptors that rode it. `SIOCATMARK` answers true at the pending byte and at a spent record that only the next urgent byte follows, and is EOPNOTSUPP on the datagram/seqpacket/listener/unbound flavours; `MSG_OOB` on a datagram or seqpacket receive is EOPNOTSUPP and on an unconnected stream is EINVAL, which is checked first. `EPOLLPRI` is raised while a byte is pending and is NOT suppressed by `SO_OOBINLINE`. Harness: `net::unix_sock::tests::oob` (20 tests, including the queue-model rules `limit`/`step`/`at_mark` in isolation), `cargo test -p net --features hosted --lib` 2083/0. Remaining OOB divergences are recorded in `scratch/issues.d/B1938-af-unix-msg-oob.md`: no `SIGURG` to `f_owner` on arrival, and a zero-length `recv(MSG_OOB)` reporting 0 where the reference reports 1. B1942: a compat receive would have written `msg_controllen`, `msg_flags` and `msg_namelen` at NATIVE offsets (40/48/8) into a 28-byte header — past the end of the structure — and emitted 16-byte `cmsghdr`s into a 32-bit receiver's control buffer. `RecvUser::finish`/`write_namelen` and `recv_control::encode_entry` now take the layout the entry settled, and `MSG_CMSG_COMPAT` is stripped from the published `msg_flags` (kernel bookkeeping, never part of the answer). Harness: `recv_user::tests` 19 (was 13), `recv_control::tests` 9 (was 7), each with a positive control. The compat-msghdr ABI item is closed; the rest of this row's Remaining list is untouched. B1952 closes the send/receive security-hook gap named on rows 44/46/47/299/307. There was no single decision: the generic per-operation verdict was evaluated in nine places at four depths and the sandbox's address verdict in a tenth, and they disagreed about ordering — a send was refused only AFTER its family had answered ENODATA/EOPNOTSUPP/EINVAL, and a blocking receive re-asked its verdict once per wakeup rather than once per transaction. One boundary (`net::socket_security`) now composes sandbox then module registry and is the only place either is consulted for a message; `socket::security::admit` is the one send call site (top of `send::prepare`, ahead of every family validation, plus the two out-of-band envelope paths) and `syscalls::recv_admit::admit_and_route` the one receive call site — the protocol owner is obtainable ONLY from that call, so no receive can reach a protocol unadmitted. Per-family and per-transport copies deleted from `net::sock::sendto`, `net::sock_io::recvfrom_opts`, `recvmsg::{inet,netlink,vsock}`, `unix_recv`, and `socket::send`'s four inline sites. Two behaviour fixes fall out: a STREAM send naming an address no longer asks for the connect right (it settles no new port; asking denied sends a sandboxed program may make), and an out-of-band send with no urgent channel imports its envelope before the hook so unreadable address memory reports EFAULT, not a permission answer. The sandbox snapshot now travels in `SendContext` so one batch cannot be judged against two policies. Harness: `net::socket_security::tests` 6, `syscalls::recv_admit::tests` 4, `socket::security_hooks_tests` 5 (which replace three tests that asserted on the SOURCE TEXT of `send.rs`/`address.rs` and could not fail for any behavioural regression). Positive controls, each RED then green: deleting the call from `send::prepare` reddens all five send tests; deleting it from `admit_and_route` reddens two receive tests; restoring the transport-derived send right reddens the stream test; swapping the two modules' order reddens the ordering test. Both arch target checks pass; smoke-x86 PASS 129s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. One consequence needed its own fix: removing the transport's duplicate evaluation let the raw IPv4 branch inline into `net::sock::sendto`, whose frame every other protocol's send then carried — the aarch64 worst-case send path grew 480 B and crossed the 13000 B stack-depth ceiling. The branch and `socket::send::send_inet` are `#[inline(never)]` now and the deepest path is 12960 B (main: 12880 B). B1959 completes the extended-error queue. `net::socket_error` becomes a module owning the record ABI, every origin (none, local, ICMP, ICMPv6, txstatus/timestamping, zero-copy, txtime), the queue and its pending-errno rule, the `MSG_ERRQUEUE` encoding, the readiness contribution and local-origin reporting — none of it target-gated, so the receive slot moved from open-coding the record to reading one tested decision. Corrected: dequeue re-derives the pending errno only from ICMP-origin records and clears it when no ICMP record follows (a transmit completion no longer clobbers `SO_ERROR`, and a consumed ICMP error no longer stays readable); disabling `IP_RECVERR`/`IPV6_RECVERR` drops every queued error record of either family, keeps the transmit-completion origins and leaves the pending errno alone; the offender sockaddr is emitted only for records that name one (local errors report zeroes, transmit completions need an egress interface and, on IPv4, an explicit ancillary request); `msg_namelen` stays zero for records with no addressable origin; `IPV6_RECVERR_RFC4884` and `SO_SELECT_ERR_QUEUE` were stored-but-unconsumed and are now wired. The queue is bounded by a receive-memory budget. AF_VSOCK's second completion queue is gone — one owner, one identifier space, one coalescing rule shared with AF_INET, whose `MSG_ZEROCOPY` sends previously produced no completion at all. Harness: `net::socket_error` 5 tests -> 39. Positive controls, each RED then green: restoring the old any-origin republish, the family-only purge, the unconditional offender, the unconditional priority bit and the option-blind completion each redden exactly their own test. Both arch builds pass, `stack-gate` PASS both (deepest 12928 B against the 13000 B ceiling), smoke-x86 PASS 83s and smoke-arm PASS 148s, first attempt. B1960 closes this row's VSOCK address/control parity item. Receive-side parity was already correct and directly tested: a connectible receive never fills `msg_name`, the datagram receive is refused by the transport before any address code runs, `MSG_EOR` comes from the wire end-of-message flag, `MSG_TRUNC` reports the record length, and no ordinary control is ever built because the family has none. The send side diverged twice: a destination was judged by the outer socket variant rather than by connection STATE, so a connect still in flight or one the peer had already reset reported EISCONN instead of EOPNOTSUPP; and a datagram destination was refused outright instead of shape-checked, giving EOPNOTSUPP where the reference gives EINVAL for an unbound one. Both are fixed in `socket::vsock_addr`, which is also the point that records that vsock consults NO ancillary data on send — it previously ran the SCM rule, so a control buffer could fail a vsock send. Harness: `socket::vsock_addr::tests` 2, `socket::tests::family_ancillary::a_vsock_destination_is_judged_by_state_not_by_the_socket_variant`; positive control RED on the variant-keyed form. This row does NOT promote on that alone: the extended-error origins item belongs to B1959. B1963 closes this row's last items and it promotes. The copy-fault transaction had no owner: a receive publishes payload, ancillary stream, source address and the two `msghdr` writebacks into four buffers the caller supplies, each of which can fault, and every family decided the order and the per-step answer for itself — the error queue and TCP published the ADDRESS before the control stream while every other family published control first, so a name fault lost control bytes the reference had already written. `syscalls::recv_txn` is now the one owner of that order (payload, control, name, `msg_flags`, `msg_controllen`) and of what each faulting step does, and every family publishes through it: AF_INET/AF_INET6 datagram, raw, packet, TCP, TCP out-of-band, the error queue, AF_UNIX in all four flavours, NETLINK, VSOCK stream, VSOCK seqpacket and the VSOCK error queue. Two behaviour fixes fall out. A STREAM receive whose destination faulted partway through one queued fragment reported the landed prefix as delivered and RETIRED those bytes; a stream consumes fragments whole, so a fragment that cannot be copied is consumed by nothing, ends the receive, and the call answers with what earlier fragments delivered or EFAULT when none did — reporting the prefix retires bytes the transport still holds and tells the caller it received data it can never read. A control-stream copyout that faulted mid-stream discarded the ENTIRE cursor, publishing `msg_controllen` zero for a buffer that already held complete control messages; the entries that landed now keep their space and the faulting entry and everything after it contribute nothing. The row's IP ancillary item was one option short rather than absent — `net::cmsg::plan` already generated and the real receive already emitted IP_PKTINFO/TTL/TOS/RECVOPTS/RETOPTS/PASSSEC/ORIGDSTADDR/CHECKSUM/RECVFRAGSIZE, the whole RFC3542 IPv6 set, the RFC2292 legacy twins and UDP_GRO in the reference's frequency order — and the gap was `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU`, stored and readable and consumed by nothing. It is a SECOND mechanism beside the extended-error queue, with its own one-slot storage, its own replacement rule and its own consumption point (an ordinary receive answers with it ahead of anything queued); both publications now come from the one host-detected-size-failure owner, so a socket with both switched on cannot be told two different numbers. The VSOCK address/control item is RETRACTED: B1960 closed it and it verifies — a destination is judged by connection STATE, a datagram destination is shape-checked with EINVAL rather than refused with EOPNOTSUPP, and the vsock send arm consults no ancillary rule, with both tests ungated and running. The differential item closes with `userspace/glibc_conformance/t_recvfault.c`: 22 lines through the real syscall entry over real mappings — unmapped pages, and pages mapped up to a `PROT_NONE` boundary so a copy faults PARTWAY — covering what each fault CONSUMES as well as what it reports. Harness: `syscalls::recv_txn::tests` 9, `syscalls::recv_control::tests` 11 (was 9), `syscalls::recv_user::tests` 20 (was 19), `net::socket_error::pathmtu::tests` 3, `net::socket_error::tests::origins` +1; `cargo test -p net -p socket -p syscalls` 2191/74/1327 all 0-failed. Positive controls, each RED then green: discarding the control prefix on a fault, restoring the byte-granular fragment copy, swapping name and control in `publish`, and dropping the `recvpathmtu` guard each redden exactly their own tests. The guest differential's own control is the pre-branch kernel, which returns `stream_payload_split rc=1024` where the oracle returns EFAULT — the only diverging line of 22. `tools/oxide-conformance-serial.sh t_recvfault` PASS on x86_64 and aarch64. Both arch kernel checks pass, `make stack-gate` PASS (aarch64 deepest 12736 B against the 13000 B ceiling), smoke-x86 PASS 60s and smoke-arm PASS 118s. |

Remaining: direct `listen` syscall fd/type/backlog/error-order coverage, Linux-exact backlog/SYN queue and reuseport listener-group behavior, security hooks, complete AF_UNIX behavior, and differential runtime coverage. B1598 gives the SOL_SOCKET table one ungated owner (`net::sock_opts::sol_socket`); it was inline in two kernel-gated shims and therefore untestable. B1641: TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT now actually defers. DEVIATION: we complete the handshake and withhold from accept; Linux drops the bare ACK and leaves the peer retransmitting. Needs a request-sock minisock. F792: left `NEEDS-REWORK` deliberately. The TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT deviation above is unresolved behaviour, not missing evidence: closing it needs a request-sock minisock so the bare ACK can be dropped and the peer left retransmitting. F795 re-audit: **the B1641/F792 TCP_DEFER_ACCEPT blocker above is RETRACTED — it is fixed and the note was stale.** `B1656` built the request-sock minisock and it is WIRED into the production ingress, not dead code: `net::stack::tcp_reqsk::defers_segment` is called from `deliver_tcp_packet_hop` BEFORE any state machine sees the segment, so a bare ACK (ACK set, no SYN/FIN/RST, zero payload) on a deferring listener is DROPPED, the request stays SYN-RECV holding its SYN-backlog slot, and the peer is left with an Established socket the server never sees -- Linux's asymmetry. `ReqSock::recalc` suppresses SYN-ACKs while waiting for data, emits one final SYN-ACK at the end of the deferring period, and expires an acked request only once the defer period is spent; `tcp_reqsk_tick` runs off the registered 100ms periodic timer. Data arrival falls through to `input_completing_request`, which applies the segment twice so the payload riding the ACK survives promotion. Evidence: `cargo test -p net --lib defer` 25/0, ungated (`stack::tcp_listener::defer_accept_tests`, `tcp_conn::reqsk::tests`), including `a_deferring_listener_leaves_the_bare_acknowledgement_uncompleted` and `data_promotes_a_deferred_request_and_the_bytes_it_carried_survive`. **CURRENT blocker (different in kind from the retracted one): the row is short of syscall-level harness evidence, not behaviour.** `050_listen.rs` carries `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]` and `net::sock::ops` (holding the `Operation::Listen` security-hook EACCES, the `somaxconn` lookup ENODEV, and the family branch) is kernel-gated too, so none of it is reachable from `cargo test`. Missing: INET/UNIX listen permission-deny coverage (only VSOCK's deny path is tested, `vsock_socket::listen_tests`), the ENODEV namespace-sysctl failure, listen-on-connected EINVAL, netlink EOPNOTSUPP, and re-listen backlog change -- `syscalls::socket_control_tests` "covers" `050_listen.rs` only by `include_str!` source-text grep, which asserts nothing about behaviour. Ungated shape coverage that DOES exist: `net::listen_admit` (datagram/raw EOPNOTSUPP, unnameable-UNIX EINVAL), `net::sysctl::normalize_listen_backlog`, `net::stack::tcp_listener_tests` backlog mechanics, `net::net_ns::tests::listener_in_one_ns_invisible_to_another`. Closing this needs the listen admission ladder extracted to an ungated owner the way `F792` did for `socket(2)`; it is NOT a net-stack behaviour gap. Structural note recorded, not a promotion blocker: the deferred request is a full heap `TcpEntry` allocated at SYN time, not Linux's slim `request_sock`, and there are no SYN cookies -- externally observable behaviour matches, per-half-open memory economics do not. B1850 closes the stated direct fd/type gap with Linux/x86_64/ARM64 differential evidence for EBADF, regular-file ENOTSOCK, datagram EOPNOTSUPP, auto-bind, negative-backlog clamping, and re-listen. Remaining work is SYN/accept-queue, reuseport, and namespace-failure breadth; the row is PARTIAL rather than NEEDS-REWORK. B1971 fixed two real defects. The security hook was asked BEFORE the backlog was clamped to `net.core.somaxconn`, so it saw the caller's raw request rather than the backlog the socket gets; the reference clamps first. And a namespace carrying no `somaxconn` entry answered ENODEV, an errno `listen(2)` has no way to produce — the ceiling is a tunable that always has a value, and it now falls back to the compiled default. The whole ladder — clamp, security, shape — moved to ungated `net::listen_admit::listen_ladder`, whose security and shape rungs are caller-supplied so the order is observable: 6 tests, positive control turns 4 RED. The row's \"INET/UNIX listen permission-deny coverage\" clause is withdrawn (`net::sock::admission` covers both operations against AF_INET). Open: the security context carries no backlog operand, so the hook cannot see the value it decides about; and SYN/accept-queue, reuseport and differential breadth. |
