| 54 | 64 | `setsockopt` | `sys_setsockopt` | vfs | credentials, fd-table, namespaces, net, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:18` | `IMPL` | `B1966` | `net/socket.c:do_sock_setsockopt; net/core/sock.c:sock_setsockopt; net/ipv4/ip_sockglue.c:do_ip_setsockopt; net/ipv6/ipv6_sockglue.c:do_ipv6_setsockopt; net/core/filter.c:sk_attach_filter,sk_attach_bpf,sk_detach_filter,sk_get_filter`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `syscalls::054_setsockopt; net::mcast_filter; net::tests_igmp; net::tests_mld; net::tests_socket_filter; net::tests_ipv4_udp_errors; net::tests_ipv6_udp_errors; cargo test -p net --features hosted --lib` | B823 adds all six `IP_MTU_DISCOVER` and `IPV6_MTU_DISCOVER` modes, `IP_RECVERR`/`IPV6_RECVERR`, pre-bind `IPV6_V6ONLY`, IPv6 RFC 3678 group/source operations, socket-owned IPv4/IPv6 multicast memberships and source filters, transactional multicast/device selection, and per-socket classic/eBPF attach/detach/lock with filter verification and endpoint-local delivery. B1108 makes common SOL_SOCKET integer setters reject short values and invalid optval pointers before mutation. B1109 makes SO_LINGER and SO_RCVTIMEO/SO_SNDTIMEO struct copyin required-length and fault-recoverable. B1110 moves common scalar and SO_BINDTODEVICE imports to shared uaccess. B1111 moves multicast scalar, sockaddr, membership, and source-request imports to shared uaccess. B1112 makes TCP_NODELAY use required scalar copyin. Remaining: audit the full Linux option matrix, capability/LSM checks, filter ABI/JIT and reuseport-BPF semantics, multicast corner cases and namespace/device teardown, protocol families beyond INET, and direct syscall differential coverage. B854 resolves one File for family classification and option mutation, preventing close/reuse from changing the option target. B1598: unknown-option ENOPROTOOPT outranked length/pointer errors (every option but `SO_BINDTODEVICE` reads a leading `int` first, so EINVAL/EFAULT must come first); `SO_SNDBUFFORCE`/`SO_RCVBUFFORCE` had no `CAP_NET_ADMIN` check and no sysctl ceiling; `SO_MARK`, `SO_PRIORITY` above the interactive band, and `SO_DEBUG` enable were all uncapped; `SO_RCVTIMEO`/`SO_SNDTIMEO` silently clamped a denormalized `tv_usec` instead of EDOM and treated a negative `tv_sec` as wait-forever rather than an immediate timeout; `SO_LINGER` republished the caller's seconds when the switch was cleared; family gating was missing for `SO_REUSEPORT`/`SO_PASSCRED`/`SO_PASSSEC`/`SO_PASSPIDFD`/`SO_PASSRIGHTS`; `SO_BINDTODEVICE` returned EINVAL for an over-long name instead of truncating to `IFNAMSIZ-1`; and roughly 25 options did not exist at all. Timestamp personalities are split into the three Linux flags so each reads back only through the option that set it. REMAINING: `SO_PEERSEC`, `SO_GET_FILTER`, `SO_PEERGROUPS`, `SO_MEMINFO`, `SO_PEERNAME`, `SO_DEVMEM_DONTNEED` and the NAPI/busy-poll trio still return ENOPROTOOPT; the reuseport-BPF trio needs the reuseport group subsystem; `rmem_max`/`wmem_max` are compile-time constants rather than per-namespace sysctls. B1598 gives the SOL_SOCKET table one ungated owner (`net::sock_opts::sol_socket`); it was inline in two kernel-gated shims and therefore untestable. Harness: `cargo test -p net --lib -- --test-threads=1` 1095/0 (1061 before), `-p syscalls --lib` 897/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. `B1625` closes the SOL_SOCKET remainder by BUILDING it: `SO_PEERSEC` via a new network peer-security hook (ENOPROTOOPT with no labelling module installed is Linux's own answer when no LSM implements the hook, not a stub); `SO_GET_FILTER` (classic-block dump, eBPF source EACCES, len==0 block-count enquiry, published length counts BLOCKS); `SO_PEERGROUPS` over an extended AF_UNIX peer snapshot where `PeerCred` carries the group list taken at the SAME instant, so it cannot disagree with SO_PEERCRED; `SO_MEMINFO`; `SO_PEERNAME` sharing getpeername's resolver; `SO_DEVMEM_DONTNEED` with the full EBADF/EINVAL/EFAULT ladder; `SO_PREFER_BUSY_POLL`; `SO_BUSY_POLL_BUDGET` (capability check BEFORE the width screen, so an unprivileged over-wide raise is EPERM not EINVAL); `SO_INCOMING_NAPI_ID`; and the reuseport-BPF trio over a NEW `net::reuseport` group subsystem joined from the canonical bind/listen tables and consulted at all four demux sites before the flow hash. `net.core.rmem_max`/`wmem_max` are live GLOBAL sysctl leaves (Linux's are globals, read-only in a non-init netns). REMAINING: the non-SOL_SOCKET option families (IP/IPV6/PACKET), LSM behaviour beyond the peer hook, namespace/device teardown, and the glibc differential. Harness: `cargo test -p net --lib -- --test-threads=1` 1163/0 (1145 before), `-p security` 245/0, `-p fs` 540/0; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 79s. B1641: SO_PEERCRED answered with the CALLER's credentials when no peer was pinned (Linux: pid 0, uid/gid -1). Buffer defaults were 16384 for everything while /proc published a constant 212992 nothing read; now 212992 generic, 16384/131072 TCP off live per-namespace tcp_wmem/tcp_rmem. SO_INQ added, consumed via SCM_INQ. GAPS: TCP_ZEROCOPY_RECEIVE ENOPROTOOPT; fast-open stored, unconsumed; IP_OPTIONS/IPv6 sticky headers never emitted on transmit; IP_CHECKSUM inert; gaps 5-10 stored-only. F792: left `NEEDS-REWORK` deliberately. The gaps above are unimplemented behaviour, not missing evidence: TCP_ZEROCOPY_RECEIVE, stored-but-unconsumed fast-open, IP_OPTIONS and IPv6 sticky headers never emitted on transmit, inert IP_CHECKSUM, and the IP/IPV6/PACKET option families. F795 re-audit of that list, item by item. **FIXED, retract from the blocker list: (i) TCP_ZEROCOPY_RECEIVE.** `B1655` made it real and wired: `055_getsockopt/tcp.rs` intercepts the option BEFORE the generic value table and calls `syscalls::tcp_zerocopy::receive::get`, which plans a window, allocates via `pmm::setup::alloc_object_frame`, drains the TCP receive queue (the queue genuinely advances) and republishes translations after evicting the prior call's. `mmap(2)` on a TCP socket fd is live through `009_mmap.rs`, which passes the window as a FILE BACKING with `phys_base = None`; `TcpZcWindow` implements `vmm::FileBacking`, so the fault path inc_refs the refcounted frame per PTE and teardown dec_refs. Checked explicitly against the free-while-mapped rule: there is no `PhysRange`, no `map_phys_range`, and no `glue_mmap(phys_base=Some(..))` anywhere on this path. **(ii) IPv4 IP_OPTIONS on transmit** -- `B1660`'s `net::ipv4_options::write_header` sets IHL from the option area and computes the header checksum AFTER options are copied and filled, and it is called from the real tx path (`stack::ipv4` whole-datagram and per-fragment, with later fragments blanking uncopied options and `header_len` folded into the MTU/fragment math, plus `raw4::tx`); the sticky source is real for UDP, v4-mapped UDP, and raw4. **(iii) `B1662`'s three consumers are genuine**: the nonlocal-bind screen (`sock::nonlocal` + `bind_screen::can_nonlocal`, ORing the per-namespace sysctl, called on the real v4/v6/raw bind paths), the local-port-range window (`local_port::effective_bounds`, consumed by ephemeral allocation, TCP bind reservation, and `IP_BIND_ADDRESS_NO_PORT` deferral), and IP_ROUTER_ALERT delivery (`router_alert::v4_present`/`v4_deliver` on the real forwarding path). All three carry ungated tests that run. **STILL OPEN — this is the CURRENT blocker set, and it is unimplemented behaviour, not missing evidence: (1) TCP fast-open has no production caller.** `B1673`/`B1684` landed a complete, tested codec, but the only production producer of `SynOptions`, `TcpConn::syn_options`, hardcodes `fastopen: None`, and `fastopen::parse`/`classify` have ZERO callers outside their own module -- no SYN or SYN-ACK ever carries a TFO option, no listener validates a cookie, and there is no data-in-SYN acceptance path. The sockopt state (`fastopen_connect`, `fastopen_key`, `TCP_FASTOPEN` qlen, `TCP_FASTOPEN_NO_COOKIE`) is stored and read back only. **(2) IPv6 sticky extension headers are still stored-only.** `IPV6_HOPOPTS`/`IPV6_RTHDRDSTOPTS`/`IPV6_RTHDR`/`IPV6_DSTOPTS` and their `IPV6_2292*` aliases are validated hard then never emitted; `merge_sticky_raw6_control` merges only `multicast_loop` and `traffic_class` and never populates the `Raw6Control` header fields, so per-message cmsg ext headers work on raw6 while sticky setsockopt ones do not. `IPV6_PKTINFO` sticky is written with NO reader anywhere, not even getsockopt, and `Ipv6Opts::nexthop` has zero callers. **(3) IP_CHECKSUM is still inert.** It is modelled correctly as the Linux recv-cmsg flag and reaches a consumer, but the producer is hardcoded off (`recvmsg::inet` sets `checksum: None`; `RxMeta.checksum` is never `Some` outside a test), so setting it is observable only through getsockopt. `SO_NO_CHECK` likewise has zero consumers. **(4) TCP does not emit IP_OPTIONS** -- `send_tcp_ipv4_segment_in` passes a literal `None`, where Linux puts IP_OPTIONS on TCP segments; so (ii) above is fixed for UDP and raw4 only. **(5) remaining stored-but-unconsumed options.** SOL_IP: `IP_NODEFRAG`, `IP_RECVERR_RFC4884`, `IP_UNICAST_IF` (never influences route or source selection), `IP_TRANSPARENT` (consumed only as a bind permission -- no TPROXY foreign-address delivery or reply-source behaviour). SOL_IPV6: `IPV6_ADDR_PREFERENCES`, `IPV6_USE_MIN_MTU`, `IPV6_MTU`, `IPV6_UNICAST_IF`, `IPV6_AUTOFLOWLABEL` (no tx flow-label generator reads it), `IPV6_ROUTER_ALERT_ISOLATE` (constant with no consumer), and `IPV6_ROUTER_ALERT` whose selector is stored and admission-screened while `router_alert` has no v6 delivery chain at all -- join bookkeeping without delivery, unlike v4. SOL_PACKET is the most complete family (`PACKET_IGNORE_OUTGOING`, `PACKET_ORIGDEV`, `PACKET_LOSS`, `PACKET_TIMESTAMP`, and the version/ring/reserve/vnet/fanout set are all live); its remainder is `PACKET_COPY_THRESH`, `PACKET_TX_HAS_OFF`, and `PACKET_QDISC_BYPASS`. Missing entirely rather than stored: `IP_PROTOCOL` on the set path (settable on raw sockets in Linux), and `IPV6_JOIN_ANYCAST`/`IPV6_LEAVE_ANYCAST`, which are declared in uapi with no set-path arm. Phantom-test audit: every test module cited across this row is ungated and confirmed to execute (`cargo test -p net --lib` 1713 passed; the 6 `tcp_zerocopy::window::tests` run under `-p syscalls`); the one genuinely uncovered file is `syscalls/src/tcp_zerocopy/receive.rs`, which is `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]` by design, so the remap and frame-lifetime code has no hosted coverage and only the pure planner it delegates to does. **B1939 re-audited the whole "STILL OPEN" list above against current code before writing anything, and most of it is STALE — retract items (1), (2), (4) and nearly all of (5).** (1) fast-open HAS a production caller: `tcp_conn::segment::syn_options` reads the live `fastopen_opt`, the listener validates and mints cookies through `stack::tcp_fastopen` over `tcp_fastopen::decide_counted` and sets `fastopen_child`, and the client learns/blackholes via `tcp_conn::active_fastopen`. (2) IPv6 sticky extension headers ARE emitted: `merge_sticky_raw6_control` calls `merge_sticky_headers`; `IPV6_PKTINFO` sticky has two readers, `sol_ipv6/get.rs` and source selection in `sock_v6.rs`. (4) TCP DOES emit IP_OPTIONS: `send_tcp_segment_in` passes `policy.ipv4_options()`, not a literal `None`. (5) of the named stored-but-unconsumed options, `IP_NODEFRAG`, `IP_RECVERR_RFC4884`, `IP_UNICAST_IF`, `IPV6_ADDR_PREFERENCES`, `IPV6_AUTOFLOWLABEL`, `IPV6_MTU`, `IPV6_UNICAST_IF`, `IPV6_ROUTER_ALERT` (via `router_alert::v6_deliver` on the real forwarding path), `IPV6_ROUTER_ALERT_ISOLATE` (via `router_alert_matches`), `PACKET_COPY_THRESH`, `PACKET_TX_HAS_OFF`, `PACKET_QDISC_BYPASS` and `SO_NO_CHECK` all reach a real consumer. `IPV6_USE_MIN_MTU` is not a live option in the reference either, so `ENOPROTOOPT` is correct rather than a gap. The two items listed as "missing entirely" are also wrong: `IPV6_JOIN_ANYCAST`/`IPV6_LEAVE_ANYCAST` are fully implemented in `net::sock_anycast` (device-reference acquire/release, prefix-on-interface screen, wildcard-ifindex leave, release at final close, two ungated tests); and `IP_PROTOCOL` **is not settable in the reference at all** — it exists only as a send-side control message and a getsockopt, so `sol_ip/set.rs` returning `ENOPROTOOPT` is the correct answer, not an omission. **B1939 CLOSES item (3), IP_CHECKSUM, by building the producer.** The UDP/IPv4 receive validation now derives its verdict from an unfolded partial sum (`ipv4::ones_sum`/`fold_ones`/`ones_sub`, one owner for the arithmetic, and one pass instead of the previous per-datagram `Vec` allocation) and RETAINS that sum on the queued datagram beside `ttl`/`tos`/`options` — `udp::parse_rx` -> `UdpDatagram.checksum` -> `Received.checksum` -> `RxMeta.checksum` -> the existing cmsg planner. A datagram whose sender suppressed the checksum retains nothing and publishes nothing; a coalesced GRO run clears the retained sum, since it no longer describes the bytes handed to the reader; a native IPv6 receive publishes nothing, because the option is IPv4-level, while a v4-mapped receive does. The dead `checksum: None` lived in `syscalls/src/recvmsg/inet.rs`, which is target-gated through `kernel_body.rs`, so the hand-written `Received`->`RxMeta` field-by-field copy could silently drop a captured field with nothing red: that projection now has ONE ungated owner, `net::recv_result::Received::rx_meta`, and the shim is a one-line call. `Received` itself moved out of the kernel-gated `sock_io` into the ungated `net::recv_result` so it can be tested at all. STILL OPEN after this pass: `IP_TRANSPARENT` is consumed only as a bind permission — no foreign-address delivery and no reply-source selection — and the full option matrix, LSM behaviour beyond the peer hook, namespace/device teardown and the glibc differential remain unaudited, so the row stays `NEEDS-REWORK`. Harness: `net::udp_checksum_tests` 10/10 ungated and confirmed to execute (the `-p net --lib` count rose 2083 -> 2093); `cargo test -p net --lib -- --test-threads=1` 2093/0; positive controls all reinstated and confirmed RED then GREEN — restoring `checksum: None` at the delivery site, removing the GRO clear, and dropping `checksum` from the projection each fail exactly one named test. Both arches check clean (`xtask kernel --arch x86_64/--arch aarch64 --check`, rc=0), which is what compiles the target-gated shim. Two PRE-EXISTING failures confirmed on `main` and unrelated: `raw4::tx_control_tests::ip_nodefrag_keeps_raw_hdrincl_fragments_out_of_local_out_defrag` (process-global statics, parallel-only) and `syscalls::return_fastpath_tests::syscall_process_irqs_close_before_return_work` (stale `include_str!` source assertion). **B1946 CLOSES the `IP_TRANSPARENT` item.** The permission now has a second owner beyond the bind screen, `net::transparent`, shared by both families because the socket bit is shared by them (the `IPPROTO_IPV6` option number writes the `IPPROTO_IP` storage). Two decisions live there and nowhere else. **Delivery:** `delivers_locally` is one three-step decision — an always-local class, then a local-table route, then a configured interface address — run by BOTH `ipv4_dst_is_local_mark_in` and `v6_dst_is_local_in`. IPv4 already honoured a local-table route (F831); IPv6 consulted only its address table, so a destination no interface owned was FORWARDED rather than delivered and a v6 transparent proxy could not receive at all. The route is asked before the address table, which changes no answer for an owned address (an owned address has a local route too) while giving the routing decision the first word. **Reply source:** an explicit IPv4 source is screened at the point route output screens it — malformed (multicast/limited-broadcast) source rejected outright with EINVAL even WITH the permission; a fanout destination with no pinned interface still needs an owned source, because the source is what chooses the interface, so the permission does not excuse it either; otherwise a foreign source needs the transparent permission or a written header, and is ENETUNREACH (a routing failure, not EADDRNOTAVAIL — the bind screen already had its say). This is the distinction that was entirely absent: `IP_FREEBIND` behaved exactly like `IP_TRANSPARENT` on transmit, because nothing screened the source at all. Freebind buys the bind, not the source. Wired at the three v4 socket send paths that hold the live socket (`sock::udp::socket_sendto`, `connect_tcp4_locked`, `connect_tcp4_mapped_locked`), each reading the flag through the existing `sock::nonlocal::permission` — no copy of the bit is cached on a transport, a route or an endpoint. IPv6 route output has NO owned-source test in the reference, so an explicit v6 source stays verbatim; that is now pinned by test rather than incidental. REMAINING for this row, unchanged and unaudited: the full IP/IPV6/PACKET option matrix, LSM behaviour beyond the peer hook, namespace/device teardown, and the glibc differential — so the row stays `NEEDS-REWORK`; this was NOT its last gap. Also newly recorded rather than fixed: the raw v4/v6 transmit paths cannot screen their bound source because `Raw4Endpoint`/`Raw6Endpoint` retain no live `Arc<IpOpts>` handle (the hdrincl half IS covered, since `state.hdrincl` is the other half of the same permission); and TCP6 active open never resolves a wildcard source. Both filed in `scratch/issues.d/B1946-ip-transparent-delivery-and-reply-source.md`. Harness: `cargo test -p net --lib -- --test-threads=1` 2109/0 (2093 before — 16 new tests, `net::transparent::tests` 14 and `net::tests_ipv6_local` 2, all in ungated modules and confirmed to execute by the count rising). Positive controls reinstated and confirmed RED then GREEN: stubbing the v6 local-route consult to `false` fails `ipv6_foreign_unicast_reaches_a_socket_bound_to_it_when_a_local_route_covers_it`; making `any_source` return `true` unconditionally fails `a_socket_bound_through_freebind_cannot_source_from_its_own_bound_address` (`left: Ok(()) right: Err(Enetunreach)`) and `only_the_transparent_permission_or_a_written_header_grants_a_foreign_source`. Both arches build (`xtask kernel --arch x86_64`/`--arch aarch64`, rc=0); smoke-x86 PASS 58s, smoke-arm PASS 112s, both attempt 1. **B1961 works the four areas rows 54/55 named as remaining, and re-verified every claim before writing code — two of the four are now CLOSED and the other two are narrowed to filed, named work.** **(a) LSM surface — CLOSED.** The socket-option decision named neither the option nor the direction: one `Operation::Option` for reads and writes alike, carrying no level, no option number, and a socket type/protocol passed as zero, so a module could permit or refuse the whole interface and nothing finer. It is now two registrations (`SetOption`/`GetOption`) carrying the level and the option number, with the identity the socket reports about itself — `socket_type`/`protocol` come from one owner (`net::sock_opts::identity`) shared with `SO_TYPE`/`SO_PROTOCOL`, which also moved those helpers out of a target-gated file into hosted reach. One ungated boundary, `net::socket_security::option`, answers for the generic inet table, AF_UNIX, the filter options, vsock and netlink, in both directions. **(b) Namespace and device teardown — CLOSED, with NO work owed.** Audited option by option for every option referencing a device or namespace-scoped object. Device-side multicast/anycast aggregate state is released on unregister while per-socket join records survive until close (the same split the reference keeps); AF_PACKET binds and memberships are the one class actively torn down, with ENETDOWN, and both trees do it; the raw-ifindex options resolve lazily in both trees, so a dangling index is by design, not a leak. Namespace teardown releases routes, rules, reassembly, the packet table, mib counters, bind tables and port reservations, the security hooks and the registry entry, and a live socket pins its namespace so teardown cannot race one. **(c) The option matrix — walked at all seven levels; eight real divergences fixed.** Ordering: a negative `optlen` was screened AFTER the security hook on three of the four setsockopt routes, and `PACKET_FANOUT_DATA` asked the filter lock before the fanout group, reporting EPERM where the reference reports EINVAL. Over-acceptance: `IPV6_PKTINFO` answered a getsockopt read the reference has no arm for. Missing ceilings: a multicast source-filter write faced neither the option-memory limit, nor the family source-count maximum, nor the count whose byte size overflows 32 bits — all three ENOBUFS ahead of the length screen, now over live `net.ipv4.igmp_max_msf` (per-namespace) and `net.ipv6.mld_max_msf` (global) leaves. Split truth: `IPV6_AUTOFLOWLABEL` had two answers, a read resolving against a namespace default the shim hardcoded false (so an untouched socket read 0 where the reference reads 1) and a transmit path reading the raw bit, blind to the namespace policy and to both of its overrides; and `IPV6_DONTFRAG` reached the transmit refusal only as a per-message control, so the sticky option was stored, read back, and ignored. Biggest of them: **SOL_SOCKET on a netlink fd was a SECOND table** — three options answered, every other number accepted and silently discarded (a client setting its buffer sizes was told the write landed), its own timeval arithmetic with no EDOM screen and no negative-seconds rule, short reads refused instead of truncated, and a duplicate copy of the socket identity; it now uses the canonical import, admission ladder, capability gates, value transforms and read table, over the generic option state the netlink socket now carries. `NETLINK_LIST_MEMBERSHIPS` also copied whole bytes where the reference copies whole words. **(d) The glibc differential — behaviour clean, coverage owed.** Both targets are LP64: the wrappers are direct syscalls with no option-number aliasing, no struct translation and no errno remapping, and the `_OLD`/`_NEW` time64 split is a 32-bit concern whose `_OLD` numbers are what this table implements. No marshalling divergence exists to fix; what is missing is a probe corpus at SOL_NETLINK and SOL_SOCKET-on-netlink (`af_packet_diff` covers SOL_PACKET, the conformance corpus covers neither), which is why the netlink defects above were found by reading rather than by a run. **RETRACTED as stale, again:** IPv6 sticky extension headers ARE consumed (`Raw6Control::merge_sticky_headers` is called from the raw6, UDP6 and TCP transmit paths; the `header_chain` interface having only test callers is not the same claim), and `IP_PROTOCOL` still has no set arm in the reference. **Row stays `NEEDS-REWORK` for (c) and (d) only, and the remainder is now named rather than unaudited:** a netlink socket has no home for most generic SOL_SOCKET state (validated now, but `SO_PRIORITY`/`SO_MARK`/`SO_LINGER`/timestamps/`SO_BINDTODEVICE` land in a flag word with no family behaviour — the Linux shape is one `struct sock` base shared by every family); `UDP_ENCAP` and `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU` are stored with no consumer (no ESP-in-UDP/L2TP decapsulation exists, and no receive path emits the path-MTU ancillary message); `IPV6_2292PKTOPTIONS` answers zero bytes; `TCP_INFO` omits the AccECN tail; `SO_ERROR` has no soft-error fallback (B1959's area); and the netlink differential corpus does not exist. All filed in `scratch/issues.d/B1961-sockopt-option-matrix-and-teardown.md`. Harness: `cargo test -p net --lib -- --test-threads=1` 2151/0 (2136 before), `-p syscalls --lib` 1315/0 (1312 before), `-p netlink` 286/0, `-p security` 246/0, `-p procfs` 166/0, `-p socket` 49/0; every new test is in an ungated module and confirmed to execute by the counts rising. Positive controls reinstated and confirmed RED then GREEN for all eight fixes — including one that caught a WEAK check: a whole-file ordering assertion passed with the defect reinstated because another route's own screen stood in for it, so the assertion is now scoped per route. Both arches check clean (`xtask kernel --arch x86_64/--arch aarch64 --check`, rc=0); `make smoke` PASS both arches on attempt 1 (x86 74s, arm 145s); `make stack-gate` PASS, deepest 12129 B. B1964 closes the last consumer gaps on this pair and corrects one of the rows that named them. The published connection info block gained the congestion-feedback tail it ended short of, so a caller sizing its buffer to the current ABI is answered in full instead of reading its own memory for the last 32 bytes; its mode field names the feedback the handshake settled on and its congestion tally is fed by the acknowledgements that echo congestion. The pending-error read gained the non-fatal fallback, and the row that asked for it understated the defect: every ICMP error matched to a connection force-closed it and reported the errno as fatal, so an established connection died of a transient host-unreachable; an established connection now survives and keeps the report, which the acknowledgement path discards and the retransmission give-up reports as its cause. Three admitted generic writes on a netlink descriptor -- the linger switch and time, the receive-timestamp personality and the send timeout -- reached a catch-all and were discarded while reporting success; all three now land where their read looks, and the apply and read view moved out of the target-gated descriptor shim into the socket's own module so both halves are testable rather than asserted by matching source text. `UDP_ENCAP` stopped being stored-and-unread: the receive path consults it on both families, eating the address-translation keepalive, consuming an encapsulated payload and passing a key-exchange control packet. `IPV6_2292PKTOPTIONS` answers real bytes in both directions, and `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU` produces and delivers a path-MTU notification on its own single-slot channel rather than the error queue. `userspace/probes/sockopt_diff` is the first differential corpus for SOL_NETLINK and SOL_SOCKET on a netlink descriptor, diffed against a real run of the same binary. Closed in B1966, which unified the generic socket state onto ONE socket base (`net::sock_base::SockBase`), which the internet, netlink and virtual-socket families all embed: the reuse/keepalive/broadcast/urgent-inline switches, both buffer budgets and the receive-buffer lock, both timeouts, `SO_PRIORITY`, `SO_MARK`, the timestamp word and key, the device binding, the credential and label switches and the generic flag/scalar area each have exactly one home; the read table consults that base directly, so no family assembles a snapshot of it, and the four options a netlink socket had nowhere to keep -- the mark, the priority, the timestamp selection and the device binding -- arrived with it. The virtual-socket family's SOL_SOCKET writes now run the one argument import, the one admission ladder and the one set of value transforms instead of its own timeval arithmetic, and its zero-copy switch is the generic flag rather than a private bool. A netlink unicast is admitted by the destination's receive budget -- taken when the queue was empty or the result fits, otherwise failing a non-blocking send or blocking the sender bounded by its own send timeout, which is the wait that timeout was given a home for -- and a drained destination releases the senders it was holding. The IPv6 send-control extension-header admission has one owner shared with the option write, so a header the send path accepts is one the option write accepts.  The differential corpus for SOL_NETLINK and the generic level on a netlink descriptor has now run against the GUEST on both arches and matches the Linux oracle byte-for-byte (68 records each, `diff` clean, `make smoke-sockopt-diff-x86`/`-arm` PASS). Its first guest run earned its keep twice: the device-binding READ answered `ENOPROTOOPT` on a netlink descriptor where the reference answers the interface NAME, and `SO_NETNS_COOKIE` answered zero in the initial namespace because it published the stack-internal namespace index instead of the namespace's global identity. Both are fixed with one owner each. Residual and tracked in `scratch/issues.d/`: a stream socket stashes no per-segment receive snapshot, so the `IPV6_2292PKTOPTIONS` read answers from the socket's own sticky and receive state. |

The published value is the sum over the message as it arrived; combined with the pseudo-header it folds to all-ones, which is how a reader re-verifies, and that invariant is the test rather than a fixed constant. Row stays `NEEDS-REWORK` for row 54's remaining reason (`IP_TRANSPARENT` reply-source/foreign-address behaviour, plus the unaudited full option matrix, LSM surface and glibc differential). Harness and positive controls: see row 54. The AF_UNIX EOPNOTSUPP correction from B1641 stands. **B1946: the `IP_TRANSPARENT` reply-source/foreign-address item this row cited is CLOSED — see row 54 for the decision owner, the wiring and the evidence.** The get side is unchanged by it: `IP_TRANSPARENT` and `IPV6_TRANSPARENT` already read back the one shared bit, and that bit now selects observable delivery and transmit behaviour rather than only a bind permission. Row stays `NEEDS-REWORK` for row 54's remaining reasons — the full option matrix, LSM surface beyond the peer hook, namespace/device teardown and the glibc differential. SOL_SOCKET reads on a netlink fd refused a short buffer where the generic table truncates to it, and answered `SO_TYPE`/`SO_PROTOCOL` from a second copy of the socket identity; both now come from the canonical read table. `NETLINK_LIST_MEMBERSHIPS` copied whole bytes where the reference copies whole words, while still publishing the full length a caller sizes its buffer from. Every option-read route also now asks a `GetOption` security decision naming the level and the option number, separate from the write decision, so a module can publish state it will not let a caller change. Row stays `NEEDS-REWORK` for row 54's remaining reason — the named option-matrix remainder and the missing netlink differential corpus. Harness, positive controls and gates: see row 54. B1964 closes the last consumer gaps on this pair and corrects one of the rows that named them. The published connection info block gained the congestion-feedback tail it ended short of, so a caller sizing its buffer to the current ABI is answered in full instead of reading its own memory for the last 32 bytes; its mode field names the feedback the handshake settled on and its congestion tally is fed by the acknowledgements that echo congestion. The pending-error read gained the non-fatal fallback, and the row that asked for it understated the defect: every ICMP error matched to a connection force-closed it and reported the errno as fatal, so an established connection died of a transient host-unreachable; an established connection now survives and keeps the report, which the acknowledgement path discards and the retransmission give-up reports as its cause. Three admitted generic writes on a netlink descriptor -- the linger switch and time, the receive-timestamp personality and the send timeout -- reached a catch-all and were discarded while reporting success; all three now land where their read looks, and the apply and read view moved out of the target-gated descriptor shim into the socket's own module so both halves are testable rather than asserted by matching source text. `UDP_ENCAP` stopped being stored-and-unread: the receive path consults it on both families, eating the address-translation keepalive, consuming an encapsulated payload and passing a key-exchange control packet. `IPV6_2292PKTOPTIONS` answers real bytes in both directions, and `IPV6_RECVPATHMTU` produces and delivers a path-MTU notification on its own single-slot channel rather than the error queue. `userspace/probes/sockopt_diff` is the first differential corpus for SOL_NETLINK and SOL_SOCKET on a netlink descriptor, diffed against a real run of the same binary. Closed in B1966, which unified the generic socket state onto ONE socket base (`net::sock_base::SockBase`), which the internet, netlink and virtual-socket families all embed: the reuse/keepalive/broadcast/urgent-inline switches, both buffer budgets and the receive-buffer lock, both timeouts, `SO_PRIORITY`, `SO_MARK`, the timestamp word and key, the device binding, the credential and label switches and the generic flag/scalar area each have exactly one home; the read table consults that base directly, so no family assembles a snapshot of it, and the four options a netlink socket had nowhere to keep -- the mark, the priority, the timestamp selection and the device binding -- arrived with it. The virtual-socket family's SOL_SOCKET writes now run the one argument import, the one admission ladder and the one set of value transforms instead of its own timeval arithmetic, and its zero-copy switch is the generic flag rather than a private bool. A netlink unicast is admitted by the destination's receive budget -- taken when the queue was empty or the result fits, otherwise failing a non-blocking send or blocking the sender bounded by its own send timeout, which is the wait that timeout was given a home for -- and a drained destination releases the senders it was holding. The IPv6 send-control extension-header admission has one owner shared with the option write, so a header the send path accepts is one the option write accepts.  The differential corpus for SOL_NETLINK and the generic level on a netlink descriptor has now run against the GUEST on both arches and matches the Linux oracle byte-for-byte (68 records each, `diff` clean, `make smoke-sockopt-diff-x86`/`-arm` PASS). Its first guest run earned its keep twice: the device-binding READ answered `ENOPROTOOPT` on a netlink descriptor where the reference answers the interface NAME, and `SO_NETNS_COOKIE` answered zero in the initial namespace because it published the stack-internal namespace index instead of the namespace's global identity. Both are fixed with one owner each. Residual and tracked in `scratch/issues.d/`: a stream socket stashes no per-segment receive snapshot, so the `IPV6_2292PKTOPTIONS` read answers from the socket's own sticky and receive state. |

Now `PidNumberSpace` per namespace (cyclic allocation, RESERVED_PIDS floor, per-ns `pid_max`), `alloc_mappings` walking innermost-to-outermost with full unwind on failure at any level, `pid_nr_ns` returning 0 for a non-ancestor, and the global counter deleted so numbers only ever come from the namespace that owns them. Every reporting site now answers in the READER's namespace. Harness: 3665 passed / 0 failed across sched 1055, syscalls 1295, procfs 136, net 1163, namespace-identity 16; 14 new tests over a 3-level nest prove a distinct number per level, 0 for a non-ancestor, and an intermediate reader seeing the INTERMEDIATE number; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 79s. B1971 re-verified the remainder. The `PTRACE_EVENT_FORK`/`VFORK`/`CLONE` producers exist and fire after publication (`101_ptrace::event`, driven from the clone slot), `CLONE_PARENT` and `CLONE_IO` are both implemented and wired, so three quarters of that compound clause is withdrawn. What survives is `CLONE_SYSVSEM`: the semaphore undo list is keyed on the thread-group id, which reproduces the only pattern glibc produces (pthread_create always sets the flag, fork never does) and gives a hand-rolled `clone(CLONE_SYSVSEM)` without `CLONE_THREAD` its own empty list where the reference shares a refcounted one. Closing it needs a shareable per-task owner in `sched`. |

Remaining: direct syscall-current-task wait harness, signal interruption/restart, and reaping lifetime races. B1581: `rusage` filled 2 of 16 fields and `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` dropped grandchildren entirely; all tracked counters are now populated (block IO in sectors) with subtree folding and a shared encoder with `getrusage`, and accumulated-children accounting moved to the thread group so a sibling thread's reap is visible process-wide. REMAINING: `ru_maxrss` is CLOSED by `F773` — the earlier claim that no RSS accounting existed was stale: the per-mm counters and all four consumers already existed, and what was missing was every page-RETIREMENT path, so counters only grew and `account_pte_remove_at` had zero callers. munmap/MADV_DONTNEED/MADV_FREE leaves, migration markers, `process_madvise`/`process_mrelease`, the COW-fork child seed and UFFDIO_COPY/ZEROPAGE are now accounted, and `replace_mm` latches a departing mm's `hiwater_rss_pages` into the thread group at both exit sites so a reaped zombie still reports its peak. F773 also fixed `fork_cow_pages_with_swap` never registering the child in `LIVE_ADDRESS_SPACES`/`PAGE_TABLE_OWNERS` — COW fork is how every process after init is born, so swapoff's leaf sweep and global accounting saw exactly one address space. Harness: an RSS tier in the multi-AS invariant suite asserts each mm's counters equal an independent page-table leaf walk after EVERY op, both negative controls confirmed firing; `cargo test -p vmm` 308/0, `-p sched` 943/0. Harness: `cargo test -p sched -p syscalls -p syscall -p tty` 2280/0; ungated `clone_abi` tests 24; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 100s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. `B1967` re-read the `signal interruption/restart` remainder and found it stale: the engine has always had `do_wait`'s shape — an available event reported even with a signal pending, `ECHILD` ahead of `WNOHANG`, and the deliverable-signal abort ordered BEFORE the park so a parked waiter stays killable — with the restart-vs-EINTR decision centralised and the sentinel normalised at the frame boundary. What was genuinely missing was any check that could fail if that ORDER drifted: the whole engine sits in a target-gated slot file where a test block compiles away in silence. The ladder now lives in an ungated owner the engine obeys rather than restates, with six hosted tests pinning each rung against the rung above it plus the entire input space; reinstating the classic signal-before-ECHILD reorder turns three of them red. REMAINING: a hosted harness driving the syscall entry points themselves, nested PID-namespace differential coverage, and reaping lifetime races (no deterministic-interleaving facility exists in the tree to build the last on). `B1637`: the returned pid and `si_pid` now answer in the reader's namespace. `B1637` built the per-namespace pid map: `numbers.resize(depth, parent_visible_tid)` gave EVERY ancestor level a copy of ONE number, there was a single global `NEXT_VPID` counter so a nested namespace never numbered its own tasks, and the whole tree had exactly THREE namespace-aware sites — all input paths. Now `PidNumberSpace` per namespace (cyclic allocation, RESERVED_PIDS floor, per-ns `pid_max`), `alloc_mappings` walking innermost-to-outermost with full unwind on failure at any level, `pid_nr_ns` returning 0 for a non-ancestor, and the global counter deleted so numbers only ever come from the namespace that owns them. Every reporting site now answers in the READER's namespace. Harness: 3665 passed / 0 failed across sched 1055, syscalls 1295, procfs 136, net 1163, namespace-identity 16; 14 new tests over a 3-level nest prove a distinct number per level, 0 for a non-ancestor, and an intermediate reader seeing the INTERMEDIATE number; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 79s. |

Previously the `semflg` argument was DISCARDED ENTIRELY: no `IPC_CREAT`/`IPC_EXCL`, no mode bits, no credentials, and every key lookup silently returned or created regardless of permission. Now full Linux key rules: `IPC_PRIVATE` always creates; existing key with `IPC_CREAT\|IPC_EXCL` is `EEXIST`; `nsems` greater than the existing set is `EINVAL` (`sem_more_checks`); `ipcperms(semflg)` gates an existing set with `EACCES`; a missing key without `IPC_CREAT` is `ENOENT`. Create path enforces `nsems == 0` -> `EINVAL`, `nsems < 0 \|\| > SEMMSL` -> `EINVAL` ahead of everything, and `used_sems + nsems > SEMMNS` -> `ENOSPC` (per-namespace `used_sems` accounting did not exist before, so `SEMMNS` was unenforceable). mode is `semflg & S_IRWXUGO`, uid=cuid=euid, gid=cgid=egid, values zeroed, `sem_ctime` stamped. Fixed: `sem_op == 0` (wait-for-zero) was treated as a no-op that always committed instead of blocking until `semval == 0`; **`SEM_UNDO` did not exist at all** and is now real (per-process `semadj[nsems]` keyed by thread group, `semadj -= sem_op` with the `[-(SEMAEM+1), SEMAEM]` bound -> `ERANGE`, rolled back with the rest of a non-committing batch, applied and clamped to `[0, SEMVMX]` by `exit_sem` at process exit and invalidated by `IPC_RMID`); errno ordering corrected to E2BIG (`nsops > SEMOPM`) -> EINVAL (`nsops < 1`) -> EFAULT -> EFBIG (`max sem_num >= nsems`, previously EINVAL) -> EACCES -> EIDRM; `IPC_NOWAIT` now honours the BLOCKING sop rather than "any sop"; `alter` is computed so a pure wait-for-zero batch needs only `S_IRUGO`; `semtimedop` honoured its timeout not at all and now converts to an absolute deadline (`EAGAIN` on expiry); `EINTR` on a signalled wake; `sempid`/`semncnt`/`semzcnt` now tracked. Only the incremental (`perform_atomic_semop_slow`) form is implemented, deliberately: the two-pass fast path is wrong for duplicated `sem_num` (which is why Linux routes `dupsop` away from it) and is a write-avoidance optimisation, not a semantic. `exit_sem` is wired into both the syscall exit path (`060_exit.rs`, guarded on the last thread of the group) and the fatal-exit path (`zombies.rs`, via a new `set_sysvsem_exit_hook` mirroring the robust-futex hook, since `sched` cannot depend on `ipc`). F765 EXECUTED the sleeping path: `userspace/wait_diff` runs 11 `wdiff\|sysv_sem\|*` records on this machine's Linux and inside one oxide boot and requires them byte-identical, on BOTH arches. Proved correct by execution, not inspection: a `-1` on a zero semaphore parks and is released by a peer's `+1` (a `slept=` bucket rules out a degenerate never-blocks return); wait-for-zero parks while `semval != 0`; `semncnt`/`semzcnt` read 1/1 against two REAL parked waiters, and one `+1` drains both, so a committing waiter wakes the next; `IPC_NOWAIT` is `EAGAIN`; `semtimedop`'s relative timeout expires with `EAGAIN` after actually waiting; `IPC_RMID` landing on a parked waiter is `EIDRM`, not a lost wakeup; a signal returns `EINTR` WITH AND WITHOUT `SA_RESTART` — `ipc/sem.c` contains no `ERESTART*` at all (`queue.status` is seeded `-EINTR` at `sem.c:2158` and the wake loop exits with it at `sem.c:2211`), and the two deliberately identical `signal_*` records ARE that assertion; `SEM_UNDO` is applied by `exit_sem` and not before, read as `semval` while the adjusting child still lives (`live=1`) and again after it exits (`after_exit=0`), against a no-`SEM_UNDO` control that stays `1`. Each record is falsifiable: the `sysvnopost` / `sysvnormid` / `sysvnoundo` / `sysvavail` / `nosig` mutants break exactly these rows under `tools/wait-diff-selftest.sh`. Still keyed on thread group, still exact for every real caller (`ipc/sem.c` `get_undo_list` keys `task->sysvsem.undo_list` per TASK, `copy_semundo` shares it under `CLONE_SYSVSEM`, and `exit_sem` applies on the last sharer's exit — which for glibc, whose `pthread_create` always passes `CLONE_SYSVSEM` and whose `fork` never does, is exactly the thread group); a hand-rolled `clone(CLONE_SYSVSEM)` WITHOUT `CLONE_THREAD` is the one shape that still differs and is not covered by the differential. `SEM_UNDO` sharing is keyed by thread group, which matches Linux for every real caller (glibc always passes `CLONE_SYSVSEM` from `pthread_create` and never from `fork`) but is not exact for a hand-rolled `clone(CLONE_SYSVSEM)` without `CLONE_THREAD`; documented at the module head. The `un->semid == -1` `EIDRM` branch (an `IPC_RMID` landing inside one `semop` between `find_alloc_undo` and the set lock) is correct by inspection but unreachable from a hosted test. Shared substrate landed with this branch: `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/` now carries one `ipcperms()`/`ipc_update_perm()` (`perm.rs`, also adopted by `sysv_shm`, deleting its private copy), the real per-namespace cyclic idx+seq identifier space (`ids.rs`, so a stale id whose slot was recycled fails `EINVAL` per `ipc_checkid`), and the `ipc64_perm`/`semid64_ds`/`msqid64_ds`/`seminfo`/`msginfo` byte layouts (`uapi.rs`). `semid64_ds` is arch-divergent and is cfg-gated: x86 interleaves an unused word after `sem_otime` and `sem_ctime` (104 bytes) where asm-generic/aarch64 does not (88 bytes) — a single layout would have handed aarch64 userspace a `sem_nsems` read out of padding. |

| 67 | common | `shmdt` | `sys_shmdt` | ipc | ipc, ipc-namespace, permissions, sched, syscall-abi, usercopy, vmm | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:64` | `IMPL` | `F765-sysv-blocking-differential` | `ipc/shm.c:ksys_shmdt, ipc/shm.c:shm_close, ipc/shm.c:shm_destroy` | `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/{sem,msg}/tests/*.rs`; `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/ids/tests.rs`; `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv_shm/shmdt/tests.rs`; `cargo test -p ipc` (153 lib + 13 integration); `cargo test -p sched` (251); `cargo test -p syscalls` (188); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `tools/boot-smoke.sh x86` | Completed `shmdt(2)` audit against `ksys_shmdt`. Previously it unmapped exactly `PAGE_SIZE` at the given address, returned 0 for ANY page-aligned address, and never touched `shm_nattch` — so a multi-page segment was never fully detached (the comment admitted it and deferred the rest to execve/exit), an address that was not an attachment reported a successful detach that had not happened, and an `IPC_RMID`-marked segment was never destroyed. Now implements the real algorithm: locate the anchor VMA whose file offset equals its distance from `shmaddr` (Linux `(vm_start - addr)/PAGE_SIZE == vm_pgoff`), take the attachment extent from the SEGMENT rather than the anchor (the anchor may be the surviving fragment of a partially unmapped attach), sweep the remaining same-segment fragments inside that extent, `EINVAL` when no anchor exists, then run `shm_close` accounting so the last detach destroys a segment already marked `SHM_DEST`. Geometry is factored into a pure function over VMA descriptors and covered hosted. F765 EXECUTED the address-space half: `userspace/wait_diff` runs 8 `wdiff\|sysv_shm\|*` records on this machine's Linux and inside one oxide boot, byte-identical on BOTH arches. The observable is not a return value — a kernel that unmapped one page and returned 0 looks identical — but a CHILD THAT FAULTS: every page of a 3-page attachment is writable before the detach and SIGSEGVs after it, so the whole segment demonstrably goes. Also executed: `shm_nattch` 1 -> 0 across the detach, a second `shmdt` of the same address is `EINVAL`, and an interior address, a misaligned address and a plain anonymous mapping are each `EINVAL` with the attachment left intact. The `sysvdt1page` mutant is the historical bug in mutant form (shmdt -> one-page munmap) and the selftest catches it. The `shm_nattch` teardown gap this row used to name is CLOSED, by the record that caught it (`nattch_tracks_fork\|forked=1` against Linux's `2`): Linux counts `shm_nattch` per VMA through `shm_vm_ops.open`/`.close`, so `VmaFlags::SYSVSHM` now marks an attachment VMA, `mm-vmm/src/vm_ops.rs` carries the two callbacks `ipc` installs at boot, and every VMA-tree birth and death funnels through `VmaTree::map_put`/`map_take` — fork copy, mprotect split, merge and `exit_mmap` alike. Splits open the fragments BEFORE closing the original, because closing first takes a single-attachment `SHM_DEST` segment to zero mid-split and destroys it under the fragments about to reference it. `shmdt` itself now does no accounting: each unmapped fragment closes, exactly as `remove_vma` does. Previously `msgflg` was DISCARDED ENTIRELY: no `IPC_CREAT`/`IPC_EXCL`, no `ENOENT`/`EEXIST`, no mode, no credentials, no `ENOSPC`. Now full Linux key rules (msg has no `more_checks`), evaluated under one registry lock so two racing creates cannot both win: `IPC_PRIVATE` always creates; `IPC_CREAT\|IPC_EXCL` on an existing key is `EEXIST`; `ipcperms(msgflg)` gates an existing queue with `EACCES`; a missing key without `IPC_CREAT` is `ENOENT`; `MSGMNI` exhaustion is `ENOSPC` (implemented, but untested — it needs 32000 queues in one namespace). New queues initialise `q_qbytes = MSGMNB`, `q_ctime` stamped, mode `msgflg & S_IRWXUGO`. Shared substrate landed with this branch: `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/` now carries one `ipcperms()`/`ipc_update_perm()` (`perm.rs`, also adopted by `sysv_shm`, deleting its private copy), the real per-namespace cyclic idx+seq identifier space (`ids.rs`, so a stale id whose slot was recycled fails `EINVAL` per `ipc_checkid`), and the `ipc64_perm`/`semid64_ds`/`msqid64_ds`/`seminfo`/`msginfo` byte layouts (`uapi.rs`). `semid64_ds` is arch-divergent and is cfg-gated: x86 interleaves an unused word after `sem_otime` and `sem_ctime` (104 bytes) where asm-generic/aarch64 does not (88 bytes) — a single layout would have handed aarch64 userspace a `sem_nsems` read out of padding. |

Fixed: `mtype < 1` was accepted and is now `EINVAL`; the per-message cap was 4096 and is now `MSGMAX` (8192); queue capacity was "16 messages" and is now Linux's byte-based `msg_fits_inqueue`, BOTH halves (`msgsz + q_cbytes <= q_qbytes && 1 + q_qnum <= q_qbytes`, the count half covered by a dedicated zero-length-message test); no permission check existed and `ipcperms(S_IWUGO)` now applies; user I/O was raw unvalidated pointer dereference and now goes through a real VMA-walk validation (`EFAULT`); `q_lspid`/`q_stime`/`q_cbytes`/`q_qnum` accounting did not exist; a signalled wake returns `ERESTARTNOHAND` (`ipc/msg.c:930`), which the return tail turns into `EINTR` only when a handler ran. Stale ids are now rejected by `ipc_checkid` sequence validation instead of a linear match on the raw id. F765 EXECUTED the sleeping path: `userspace/wait_diff` runs 16 `wdiff\|sysv_msg\|*` records on this machine's Linux and inside one oxide boot and requires them byte-identical, on BOTH arches. Proved correct by execution, not inspection: `msgrcv` parks on an empty queue and takes the peer's message; `msgsnd` parks on a queue filled to `q_qbytes` and completes when a receiver drains one; `IPC_NOWAIT` is `ENOMSG` receiving and `EAGAIN` sending; `IPC_RMID` on a parked waiter is `EIDRM`; a delivered handler yields `EINTR` in BOTH directions with and without `SA_RESTART`, because `ERESTARTNOHAND` (`ipc/msg.c:930`, `ipc/msg.c:1241`) is the one class a handler always collapses; and the restart itself is observed the only way it can be — SIGSTOP/SIGCONT makes `signal_pending` true with no handler frame, and BOTH directions re-enter the call and still complete it against a peer that acts only AFTER the SIGCONT. Selection rules ran too: `E2BIG` leaves the message queued, `MSG_NOERROR` truncates, a negative `msgtyp` drains 3/1/2 as 1,2,3, `MSG_EXCEPT` skips the named type. Falsifiable via the `sysvnopost` / `sysvnormid` / `sysvavail` / `sysvmsgflags` / `handler` / `nosig` mutants. Linux's `pipelined_send` targeted hand-off is deliberately NOT reproduced: `WaitList` cannot wake one specific task, so a `wake_one` to a non-matching receiver would hang. The message is always enqueued and all receivers are woken to re-run `find_msg` under the lock, reaching the same observable state; the transient difference is that `q_cbytes`/`q_qnum` briefly count a message Linux would have handed straight over. Now `bufsz < m_ts && !MSG_NOERROR` is `E2BIG` with the message LEFT ON THE QUEUE (asserted by test), and `MSG_NOERROR` truncates, removes, and returns `bufsz`. `MSG_NOERROR`, `MSG_EXCEPT` and `MSG_COPY` did not exist at all and are implemented, including `MSG_COPY`'s index semantics, its non-removal, its `EINVAL` without `IPC_NOWAIT` or with `MSG_EXCEPT`, and the `copy_msg` `EINVAL` only reachable with `MSG_NOERROR`. Type selection was also wrong: for `msgtyp < 0` the old code took the FIRST match, where Linux `find_msg` narrows `msgtyp` to `m_type - 1` on each `SEARCH_LESSEQUAL` hit and keeps scanning, yielding the LOWEST type with a FIFO tie-break and an immediate return on `m_type == 1`; both the tie case and the `LONG_MIN -> LONG_MAX` edge are tested. A bogus `bufsz > 4096 -> EINVAL` was removed (Linux caps only `MSG_COPY`). Added `ipcperms(S_IRUGO)`, validated user I/O, `q_lrpid`/`q_rtime`/`q_cbytes`/`q_qnum` accounting, `ENOMSG` on `IPC_NOWAIT`, and `EINTR`. Previously `IPC_STAT`/`IPC_SET`/`IPC_INFO` returned 0 WITHOUT TOUCHING THE USER BUFFER — a syscall that lied — and `MSG_STAT`/`MSG_STAT_ANY`/`MSG_INFO` did not exist. | 72 | common | `fcntl` | `sys_fcntl` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:52` | `PARTIAL` | `B768-fasync-fileops-gate` | `fs/fcntl.c:do_fcntl, fs/file.c:f_dupfd`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `syscall-abi-model` | B768 fixes the `F_SETFL(O_ASYNC)` side of Linux `fs/fcntl.c::setfl`: changed async state calls backend `f_op->fasync` when present and no longer generic-registers unsupported files; absent `fasync` remains ignored as Linux does for `F_SETFL`, while backend errors abort before the final flag store. Evidence: x86_64 `cargo check -p syscalls`; shared VFS/ioctl fasync harness in syscall 16 evidence. Fixed `F_DUPFD`/`F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` to use the caller's `RLIMIT_NOFILE` soft limit and return `EINVAL` when the requested minimum is at/above that limit. Remaining: full command-by-command fcntl audit and hosted syscall-context harness. B854 makes `F_DUPFD` and `F_DUPFD_CLOEXEC` install the already-pinned open file description; CLOEXEC is published atomically, and deterministic source close/reuse and reservation schedules pass. LEASE GAP (F750): `F_SETLEASE` registers a lease, but this kernel has NO BLOCKING LEASE BREAK. `vfs::file::lease_force_break` revokes every conflicting lease immediately and returns, where Linux `__break_lease` (`fs/locks.c:1664-1792`) BLOCKS the breaking opener in `wait_event_interruptible_timeout` until the holder downgrades or `break_time` expires, sends the holder its lease-break signal, and only then proceeds; `LEASE_BREAK_NONBLOCK` is the sole path that returns `-EWOULDBLOCK` (`:1743`) and an interrupted break is `-ERESTARTSYS`, never EINTR. So an open that should have waited for the lease holder proceeds instantly here and the holder is never given its grace period. No wait exists to classify, which is why the interruptible-wait sweep found nothing to change on this path. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL (x86): **DIVERGES**, worst in the family. `fcntl(F_SETLKW)` under contention PARKS and never returns (`wdiff\|lock\|setlkw_sarestart\|outcome=blocked`); with the lock probes running first the stall was not bounded by any in-probe guard, so from userspace the park looks UNKILLABLE — the class F745/F747 fixed for mqueue. Without SA_RESTART it returns ENOSYS where Linux returns EINTR. Plan §17 D5. B1600: `kill_fasync` had NO production caller — pipes, unix/inet sockets, vsock and netlink all implemented `f_op->fasync` and registered holders, and nothing ever fanned a readiness event out, so `O_ASYNC` was inert kernel-wide. The list now hangs off the source's poll queue (Linux `pipe->fasync_readers`/`socket_wq->fasync_list`) so all ~100 existing readiness sites drive it unchanged, and the global inode-filtered registry (a second copy of the source-to-holder relation) is gone. Delivery carried no siginfo — an `F_SETSIG` handler read si_code/si_band/si_fd as zero; the full `_sigpoll` arm now exists end to end and `fa_fd` is no longer a dropped argument. `f_owner.pid_type` did not exist (the type was inferred from the id's sign), so `F_SETOWN_EX(TID)` read back as `F_OWNER_PID` and delivery always used the process-directed set. Added `F_CREATED_QUERY` (its `Fmode::CREATED` state was defined, unit-tested, and never set or read) and `F_GETOWNER_UIDS` (the only reader of the uid/euid snapshot `f_setown` stores); `F_SETOWN`/`F_SETOWN_EX` now ESRCH on a nonexistent target. REMAINING: `F_GETDELEG`/`F_SETDELEG`; no blocking lease break (`lease_force_break` revokes instantly instead of `__break_lease`'s interruptible wait). Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1972 re-verified this row's three named gaps and two are CLOSED. The lease break DOES block: `open_common.rs` `break_lease_for_open` tests conflict, signals the holder, returns EWOULDBLOCK for the non-blocking path, otherwise waits to a `LEASE_BREAK_NS` deadline checking deliverable signals each round, and force-revokes only on timeout -- the immediate-revoke reading this row carried is no longer what the code does. `F_SETLKW` under contention DOES return: `fs::posix_lock::setlkw` classifies deadlock as EDEADLK and returns the restart sentinel when a signal interrupts the wait, so the answer without SA_RESTART is EINTR through the ordinary restart decision rather than a park. Dispatch has one owner: `072_fcntl.rs`, with the record-lock commands routed onward inside the same file and no second command table anywhere. F_GETDELEG/F_SETDELEG is the one REAL remaining gap and is confirmed present in the reference's `do_fcntl`; every other command that switch handles has a case here. Closing it needs a lease FLAVOR bit in vfs, because the get-lease query filters the lease list by flavor -- F_GETLEASE must report only plain leases and F_GETDELEG only delegations while both share one break path -- and `vfs::File::lease` is a single unflavored int whose conflict test matches the bare type, so an OR-ed flavor bit breaks it. Note the asymmetry: set-lease rejects a directory fd, set-deleg does not. Residual nit: the lease-break wait reports EINTR directly where the record-lock wait returns the restart sentinel. |
| 73 | common | `flock` | `sys_flock` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `F726-fs-simple-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/locks.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE2(flock), fs/locks.c:flock_translate_cmd, fs/locks.c:flock_make_lock, fs/locks.c:flock_lock_inode, fs/locks.c:locks_remove_file` | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_flock_shape.rs` | Audited against `fs/locks.c`. Ownership confirmed Linux-correct: the key is the `Arc<vfs::File>` address = `flc_owner = filp`, so `dup(2)` and `fork(2)` share one lock and only the final reference drop releases it (proved by `dup_shares_the_lock_because_the_description_is_the_owner`, `fork_inherits_the_same_lock_owner`, `last_reference_drop_releases_the_lock`). Four divergences FIXED: (1) `LOCK_MAND` returned `EINVAL`, Linux accepts and IGNORES it (`pr_warn_once` then `return 0`); (2) the operation validity check ran AFTER the fd lookup, so `flock(badfd, bogus_op)` reported `EBADF` where Linux reports `EINVAL` — `flock_translate_cmd` precedes `CLASS(fd, f)`; (3) no `f_mode` gate, so an `O_PATH` fd could take a lock — Linux returns `EBADF` when neither `FMODE_READ` nor `FMODE_WRITE` is set and the op is not `LOCK_UN`; (4) `File::Drop` released the flock but never woke contenders, so a task parked in blocking `flock(2)` slept forever when the holder closed — Linux `locks_delete_lock_ctx` calls `locks_wake_up_blocks` (regression-pinned by `last_reference_drop_wakes_parked_contenders`). Blocked-upgrade non-atomicity, `LOCK_NB`→`EWOULDBLOCK`, `EINTR`, and shared-lock stacking already matched. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_flock_shape` (10 passed); both-arch kernel builds. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL (x86): **DIVERGES**. `flock(LOCK_EX)` under real contention RESUMES correctly under SA_RESTART (`outcome=ok`, MATCHES the oracle — F750's ERESTARTSYS change verified working), but without SA_RESTART returns **ENOSYS** where Linux returns EINTR (`wdiff\|lock\|flock_norestart`). Same ENOSYS shape as pipe/mqueue/F_SETLKW — one return-tail defect, four subsystems. Pre-F750 this path returned a flat EINTR and never entered the restart tail, so F750 turned a right-answer-wrong-reason into garbage. Plan §17 D1. |
| 74 | common | `fsync` | `sys_fsync` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:104` | `IMPL` | `F726-fs-simple-audit`; `B1440-fsync-per-inode-not-syncfs`; `B1462-durable-write-path` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/sync.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE1(fsync), fs/sync.c:do_fsync, fs/sync.c:vfs_fsync, fs/sync.c:vfs_fsync_range, fs/libfs.c:noop_fsync` | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_fsync_shape.rs` | Audited against `fs/sync.c`. Work moved out of the shim into `fs::sync::vfs_fsync`. Two divergences FIXED: (1) a description with no backing store returned SUCCESS — Linux `vfs_fsync_range` returns `EINVAL` when `!file->f_op->fsync`, which is the case for pipes/FIFOs (`pipefifo_fops`), sockets (`socket_file_ops`), the anon-inode fds (eventfd/epoll/timerfd/signalfd/inotify), and character devices (`memory_fops`, `tty_fops`); reporting durability for a descriptor with no backing store is a data-integrity lie. The decision lives in a `FileOps::fsync` slot (Linux `f_op->fsync`) whose generic default answers by description type and which any backend may override — no list kept at the syscall layer. (2) The metadata commit went through `ext4::commit_rootfs_journal()`, which only ever commits the ROOT mount's journal, so `fsync` on a file on any OTHER ext4 mount returned success without committing. **B1440 correction:** the first attempt at (2) routed the commit through the superblock's `sync_fs`, which is WRONG — `sync_fs` is the whole-mount pass behind `sync(2)`/`syncfs(2)` and ext4's writes back every dirty page on the filesystem plus a device flush, so every `fsync` cost a full `syncfs`. ext4 now installs its own `f_op->fsync` (`ext4_sync_file`) on BOTH its regular-file and directory operations, committing only the journal transaction carrying that inode, resolved from the inode's own `i_private` so a non-root mount is still durable. Causality-checked: restoring the `sync_fs` call fails `fsync_uses_the_per_inode_slot_and_never_the_whole_fs_pass` with 2 observed invocations vs the expected 0. Page-cache writeback via `i_mapping()->writeback()` retained; `EBADF` ordering already matched. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_fsync_shape` (4 passed). B1462: ORDERING FIXED — `vfs_fsync` ran `f_op->fsync` (ext4's journal commit + `dev.flush`) BEFORE `mapping.writeback()`, so the transaction made durable did not yet contain the data or the extents describing it; Linux `fs/ext4/fsync.c:166-198` does `file_write_and_wait_range` FIRST, then the commit, then `blkdev_issue_flush`. Also added: `vfs_fsync_range` (the byte-range form Linux's `vfs_fsync` wraps), the `errseq_t` `wb_err` latch on the inode + superblock with `f_wb_err` sampled at open so a deferred writeback error is reported once per fd (`mm/filemap.c:file_check_and_advance_wb_err`), and a writeback failure now ABORTS before the commit (Linux's `goto out`). Ordering is asserted as a call sequence in `crates/kernel/vfs/tests/durable_write_ordering.rs` (12 of its 21 tests fail against the pre-fix order). |
| 75 | common | `fdatasync` | `sys_fdatasync` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:104` | `IMPL` | `F726-fs-simple-audit`; `B1440-fsync-per-inode-not-syncfs`; `B1462-durable-write-path` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/sync.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE1(fdatasync), fs/sync.c:do_fsync` | `crates/kernel/fs/tests/sys_fsync_shape.rs` | Audited against `fs/sync.c`. Divergence FIXED: slot 75 was routed to the SAME handler as slot 74 with no way to tell them apart, so the `datasync` argument of Linux `do_fsync(fd, datasync)` did not exist. Slot 75 now has its own `sys_fdatasync` entry that passes `datasync = true` down to `f_op->fsync`. Same `EINVAL`-for-unsyncable fix and the same B1440 per-inode `ext4_sync_file` correction as slot 74. REMAINING (why PARTIAL, not IMPL): `fdatasync` does not yet SKIP the non-essential metadata Linux skips — the backend commits identically for both, so this is currently a correct SUPERSET (more durable than required, never less) rather than full Linux-row semantics. No program can observe wrong data or a wrong errno; the gap is the missing timestamp-only-writeback elision. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_fsync_shape` (4 passed). B1462: same ordering fix as slot 74; `datasync` now reaches the backend through the shared `vfs_fsync_range`, and `O_DSYNC`/`RWF_DSYNC` writes route here via `generic_write_sync`. B1600 closed a durability hole: `fsync` on a BLOCK-DEVICE fd issued no FLUSH — `DeviceFileOps` had no fsync slot so it took the generic default's `Ok(())`, and writes to `/dev/sdX` sat in the controller's volatile cache while fsync reported success. REMAINING: the `datasync` flag reaches `ext4_sync_file` and is discarded (a safe over-sync, never data loss); FUSE has NO fsync slot at all and the `FUSE_FSYNC`/`FUSE_FSYNCDIR` opcodes are not defined, so fsync on a FUSE file returns 0 without contacting the server. Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. `F776` built the missing inode-metadata writeback path and found a durability bug under it: `wb_writeback` cleared `I_DIRTY` and dropped the writeback pin WITHOUT ever calling `s_op->write_inode`, so the sync path was a no-op that presented itself as durable. Also fixed: `sync_filesystem` ran the inode pass AFTER each `sync_fs`, writing inodes into a transaction that was never committed; and `iput_final` was INVERTED versus Linux, flushing metadata for exactly the deleted inodes and nothing else. `I_DIRTY_TIME` is now its own bit with `dirtied_time_when` as the expiry clock and `__mark_inode_dirty`'s supersede rule exact, and `SB_LAZYTIME` finally has a consumer through the single `writeback::inode_update_time` entry point. This row's stated remainder — the timestamp-only-writeback elision — is CLOSED: `fdatasync` on an inode whose only dirt is a timestamp now skips the metadata write and leaves the deferral pending, gated on `I_DIRTY_DATASYNC` per `simple_fsync_noflush`. REMAINING: FUSE still has no fsync slot. Harness: `vfs --test lazytime_writeback` 13 (each forcing point READS THE TIMESTAMP BACK from a store reachable only via `i_op->update_time`/`s_op->write_inode`, not flag assertions) + `writeback_policy` 13, revert-proved both ways; `-p vfs -p fs -p ext4 -p syscalls` 4239/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 60s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1970 closes the stated remainder: fuse had NO fsync slot and the FSYNC/FSYNCDIR opcodes were not defined, so fsync on a fuse file or directory took the generic default's `Ok(())` and reported durability the daemon was never asked for. The slot now issues the request with the `datasync` argument on the wire as the fdatasync flag bit, sends the directory opcode for a directory, and treats an `ENOSYS` reply as success plus a per-connection latch so no later call pays the round trip. Decisions live in an ungated module: `cargo test -p fs --lib fuse::fsync` 4 passed, all 4 RED with the opcode selection, the flag and the ENOSYS classification reverted, GREEN restored. |

Work moved out of the shim into `fs::truncate::vfs_truncate`. Divergences FIXED: (1) only a DIRECTORY was rejected (`EISDIR`) — Linux rejects every non-regular type, `EISDIR` for a directory and `EINVAL` for anything else (`vfs_truncate`: "For directories it's -EISDIR, for other non-regulars - -EINVAL"), so truncating a FIFO/socket/char-device by path wrongly proceeded to `notify_change`; (2) no `inode_newsize_ok` gate at all — growing past the soft `RLIMIT_FSIZE` now posts `SIGXFSZ` and returns `EFBIG`, and growing past the filesystem's `s_maxbytes` returns `EFBIG`, both only when the file GROWS; (3) the set-user-ID / set-group-ID bits survived a size change — Linux `dentry_needs_remove_privs` folds `ATTR_KILL_SUID`/`ATTR_KILL_SGID` into the truncate. Error ORDER now matches Linux exactly (`EINVAL` negative length → type gate → `EACCES` → `EROFS` → `EPERM` append-only), which required moving `inode_permission(MAY_WRITE)` to the caller and giving `setattr_prepare` a Linux `ATTR_FORCE` short-circuit. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test sys_truncate_shape` (7 passed). NOT DONE: `ETXTBSY` for truncating a running executable — oxide has no `i_writecount` / `deny_write_access` at all (Linux `get_write_access` in `vfs_truncate`), so this is a cross-subsystem exec+open gap, not a truncate-local one. DOWNGRADED IMPL->PARTIAL (B1441): this row's own Evidence discloses that ETXTBSY on truncating a running executable is absent (no `i_writecount`/`deny_write_access` in-tree). The legend defines IMPL as FULL Linux-row semantics, so a disclosed divergence cannot be IMPL however well documented. Closed in `B1586`, which fixed the shared setattr ladder: there was NO `may_setattr` gate at all (so `chattr +i`/`+a` inodes accepted chmod/chown/chgrp and explicit timestamp changes), `inode_permission` never consulted `S_IMMUTABLE` (so truncate and `open(O_WRONLY)` on an immutable file succeeded), `chown_ok`/`chgrp_ok` were gated such that ANY caller could name the file's current uid/gid and get success where Linux returns EPERM, a combined iattr applied in the wrong order with chmod's S_ISGID test ignoring an accompanying `ATTR_GID`, `apply_kill_priv` re-tested S_IXGRP and undid whatever set the flag, there was no `EOVERFLOW` rule, and symlink `EOPNOTSUPP` preceded the EROFS gate. Also removed a split source of truth: `vfs::{may_chmod, may_chown, chmod_sgid_strip, chown_kill_priv}` was a second DAC ladder with zero production callers that had already drifted. The disclosed ETXTBSY gap is closed (`i_writecount` + exec `deny_write_access`), now pinned by test alongside immutable EPERM. Harness: `setattr_may_setattr_gate` (6), `setattr_chown_ok_ladder` (7), `chattr_model` (9), `fs::mknod` (4), `sys_truncate_shape` (9), `perm_enforce` rewritten (23); `cargo test -p vfs` 1457/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 66s, smoke-arm PASS 92s. |

| 78 | common | `getdents` | `sys_getdents` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/217_getdents64.rs:66` | `IMPL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `fs/readdir.c, include/linux/dirent.h, include/linux/fs.h dir_emit_dots, fs/ext4/dir.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rewritten against `fs/readdir.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(getdents)`/`filldir`. `linux_dirent` layout byte-exact (d_ino@0, d_off@8, d_reclen@16, d_name@18, d_type at reclen-1) and distinct from `linux_dirent64`; reclen = ALIGN(offsetof(d_name)+namlen+2, sizeof(long)). Return rule is Linux's tail: bytes already packed win over a late backend error, EINVAL only when the buffer cannot hold the FIRST record, 0 only at end-of-directory; `iterate_dir` stores f_pos error-or-not; last record's d_off sealed with the final ctx.pos; count truncated to unsigned int; ENOTDIR decided before any user access; `verify_dirent_name` EIO on empty/`/`/>=PATH_MAX names; signal_pending abandons the walk after the first record. `.`/`..` now emitted for EVERY filesystem (`vfs::readdir_dots`, cursors 0/1, child cookies shifted by 2) with an opt-out for ext4/FUSE which carry their own; before this they were missing on tmpfs, kernfs, devfs, devpts, procfs, sysfs, cgroupfs, tracefs, binfmt_misc and autofs. d_type is honest per backend: tmpfs/kernfs/procfs/sysfs/cgroupfs report the real per-inode type; ext4 decodes the on-disk file_type byte but only when EXT4_FEATURE_INCOMPAT_FILETYPE is set (otherwise DT_UNKNOWN, previously DT_REG for every entry including directories); FUSE passes the daemon's DT_* through including DT_UNKNOWN. ext4 readdir cookies are now byte offsets (Linux `ext4_readdir`) so lseek(d_off) is stable and one listing is O(N) not O(N^2), and a block read error or corrupt rec_len surfaces EIO instead of looking like end-of-directory. PARTIAL: the synthetic filesystems still use ordinal cursors, so an insert/unlink between two getdents calls shifts the cookies of later entries (Linux uses cursor dentries / stable hashes); the f_version stale-cursor restart limits this to a restart rather than a torn listing. Tests: syscalls::getdents_abi (20 hosted), crates/kernel/fs/tests/getdents_dots_tmpfs.rs (6, real tmpfs), crates/kernel/ext4/tests/getdents_dtype_image.rs (5, real ext4 image). B1600: ext4 emitted the RAW on-disk `d_ino` while `stat` reports the wrapped value — they disagreed on every entry, breaking `find -inum`, `getcwd(3)`'s `..`-walk fallback, and tar/rsync hardlink detection. The `f_version` check REWOUND `f_pos` to 0 (that is not what f_version is; `iterate_dir` never moves f_pos for it), so a chmod/utimes on a directory mid-listing re-emitted the whole prefix and discarded a `seekdir(3)` cookie. REMAINING: synthetic filesystems still use ordinal cursors (~53 sites behind one shared `kernfs::PseudoDir::op_readdir` loop); `/proc/self` is DT_DIR not DT_LNK and `thread-self` is absent; 57 sites emit `d_ino == 0` on a transient lookup failure; FUSE re-OPENDIRs inside every getdents call, losing the daemon's cursor. Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. Closed in `F775`: the three stated remainders are gone. Synthetic filesystems used ORDINAL cursors — not `d_off` cookies — so entries added or removed mid-listing shifted the position and `seekdir(3)` duplicated or skipped; the shared `kernfs::PseudoDir` loop and all ~50 hand-rolled loops in sysfs, procfs, cgroupfs, tmpfs, binfmt_misc and tracefs/debugfs now use `vfs::readdir_cookie` (name-hash ordered, stable per entry, collisions separated). All 56 `d_ino == 0` sites are gone — a vanished name is omitted from the listing rather than emitted as a deleted placeholder. FUSE re-`OPENDIR`ed inside every getdents call, discarding the daemon's cursor; the new Linux-shaped `FileOps::iterate_file` carries the open description so FUSE reuses its tracked `fh`. Incidental: sysfs `net_class` and `tty` never advanced past their second-to-last entry, so `subsystem` was reachable only by resuming exactly there. `/proc/self` and `/proc/thread-self` were already DT_LNK from the `B1603` symlink work. Harness: `atime_touch` 16, `atime_read_tmpfs` 7, `readdir_cursor` (kernfs 9, procfs 9, cgroup 6, tmpfs 5), `sysfs::readdir` 75->81; 2717 passed / 0 failed across vfs/kernfs/sysfs/procfs/fs, with every mutation test revert-proved (restoring the ordinal loop makes it FAIL); both arches build; stack-gate PASS; both smokes PASS. |
