| 82 | common | `rename` | `sys_rename` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/082_rename.rs` | `IMPL` | `F740-rename-family-audit` | `fs/namei.c:filename_renameat2`, `fs/namei.c:__start_renaming`, `fs/namei.c:lock_two_directories`, `fs/namei.c:vfs_rename`, `fs/namei.c:may_delete_dentry`, `fs/namei.c:may_create_dentry`, `fs/namei.c:lookup_one_qstr_excl`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_rename2`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_rename`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_cross_rename`, `mm/shmem.c:shmem_rename2`, `fs/libfs.c:simple_rename_exchange`, `fs/kernfs/dir.c:kernfs_iop_rename` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rebuilt as `filename_renameat2` step for step, with the order-sensitive decisions in the ungated `syscalls/src/rename_policy.rs` so they are unit-testable: flags validated before either pathname is fetched (`RENAME_NOREPLACE\|RENAME_WHITEOUT` legal, EXCHANGE conflicts with both); old parent resolved before the new pathname is read; EXDEV before the LAST_NORM tests; non-`LAST_NORM` old side EBUSY, new side EBUSY except EEXIST under NOREPLACE (Linux's `error = -EEXIST` assignment sits between the two tests); EROFS via the owning mount; source-missing ENOENT, EXCHANGE-needs-both ENOENT, NOREPLACE EEXIST; the `lock_rename` ancestor TRAP split — source-is-ancestor EINVAL, target-is-ancestor ENOTEMPTY (EINVAL under EXCHANGE) — which had no counterpart before; trailing-slash ENOTDIR; same-inode no-op 0 after those gates; mountpoint EBUSY via `d_mountpoint`; then Landlock and `may_rename`. Backends: ext4 gained the `ext4_empty_dir` ENOTEMPTY gate (a populated directory victim previously went straight to `Mount::rmdir`, whose contract is caller-verified-empty, freeing the destination subtree's blocks and inode with its children still linked), the cross-parent `..` repoint plus both parents' `i_links_count` fixups on plain rename AND on EXCHANGE's mixed pair, the `EXT4_LINK_MAX` EMLINK ceiling, and the four `ext4_rename` timestamp stamps; the three path-based ext4 entry points now share one core with `i_op->rename` instead of three divergent copies. tmpfs gained the same ENOTEMPTY gate, `simple_rename`/`simple_rename_exchange` parent-link accounting, and EINVAL for flags outside the three shmem implements. kernfs now answers ANY renameat2 flag with EINVAL (`kernfs_iop_rename`'s `if (flags) return -EINVAL`) instead of honouring NOREPLACE/EXCHANGE and dropping WHITEOUT. A filesystem with no `->rename` slot now answers EPERM, not EROFS. Remaining for IMPL: `IS_SWAPFILE` EPERM, `HAS_UNMAPPED_ID`/invalid-vfsuid EOVERFLOW and `IS_DEADDIR` ENOENT inside `may_delete`/`may_create`, and the ESTALE `LOOKUP_REVAL` retry loop. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --test rename_ladder_oracle` (7 differential cases run the REAL `renameat2(2)` on the host kernel and require the oxide ladder to return the identical errno); `cargo test -p syscalls --lib rename_policy` (15); `cargo test -p ext4 --test rename_nonempty_victim_image --test rename_dotdot_image --test rename_overwrite_image --test rename_exchange_image` (14); `cargo test -p fs --lib tmpfs::tests::rename` (7); `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch x86_64` and `--arch aarch64`. Closed in `B1597`, which completed the shared gates (`vfs/namei/may_{create,delete,link,rename}.rs`, split out of an over-cap `permission.rs`): `may_create` was missing dead-directory ENOENT and unrepresentable-caller EOVERFLOW ahead of the DAC check; `may_delete` was missing `IS_SWAPFILE` EPERM, `HAS_UNMAPPED_ID`/invalid-vfsuid EOVERFLOW, `IS_DEADDIR` ENOENT and `IS_ROOT` EBUSY; and the `i_op` default for the mutating ops was EROFS where Linux uses EPERM (EROFS is the read-only-MOUNT verdict, decided a layer up). Order-sensitive decisions moved to ungated `syscalls::path_ops_policy` since the slot files are target-gated. The three legs F740 listed as remaining all arrive through these gates. The ESTALE `LOOKUP_REVAL` retry is not rename-specific and has no trigger — no oxide backend returns ESTALE, so adding it would be machinery without callers. Harness: `syscalls --test path_ops_ladder_oracle` 7 (DIFFERENTIAL against the real host kernel), `--test path_ops_policy` 11, `vfs --test may_gate_ladder` 8, `namei_hardlink_perm` 11, `ext4 --lib links` 4; `-p vfs -p ext4 -p fs -p syscalls -p ipc` 4282/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 105s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. |

Hosted VFS model covers symlink success and non-symlink `EINVAL`; remaining work: full usercopy fault matrix under live syscall harness. B1597: `bufsiz` is a SIGNED int — `-1` was reinterpreted as a ~4 GiB length; now EINVAL, differentially verified against the host kernel over 9 sizes including `i32::MIN`. Truncation-without-error, non-symlink EINVAL and empty-path ENOENT verified correct. REMAINING: `touch_atime` — oxide has atime policy (`vfs::inode_times`, `AtimePolicy`) with ZERO call sites anywhere, including `read()` and `open()`, so wiring it for readlink alone would be a one-off; it belongs to a dedicated atime lane. Harness: `syscalls --test path_ops_ladder_oracle` 7 (DIFFERENTIAL against the real host kernel), `--test path_ops_policy` 11, `vfs --test may_gate_ladder` 8, `namei_hardlink_perm` 11, `ext4 --lib links` 4; `-p vfs -p ext4 -p fs -p syscalls -p ipc` 4282/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 105s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. Closed in `F775`: `touch_atime` was the sole stated gap. `vfs::inode_times::atime_needs_update` existed with ZERO call sites — access times never moved anywhere in the tree. New ungated `vfs::atime` is wired at every site Linux uses (read/pread/read_iter, getdents, readlink/readlinkat, symlink follow in the walk, file mmap; execve inherits it through mmap as in Linux). Latent bug fixed with it: a new mount was created with `mnt_flags == 0`, which `relatime_need_update` reads as STRICTATIME (relatime is a set bit; strictatime is the absence of NOATIME+RELATIME), so every boot and internal mount would have stamped on every read — `new_mount` and the plain-bind graft now take `path_mount`'s `ms_to_mnt(0)` relatime default. Confirmed against the primary source and pinned by tests: `S_IMMUTABLE` is NOT an atime gate; a read-only mount blocks via write access and a read-only superblock via `IS_NOATIME`. REMAINING (kernel-wide, not this row): lazytime is parsed and stored but not deferred — `wb_writeback` clears `I_DIRTY` without calling `s_op->write_inode`, so deferring the stamp would DISCARD it; atime persists eagerly and lazytime mounts behave as non-lazytime until a timestamp writeback path exists. Harness: `atime_touch` 16, `atime_read_tmpfs` 7, `readdir_cursor` (kernfs 9, procfs 9, cgroup 6, tmpfs 5), `sysfs::readdir` 75->81; 2717 passed / 0 failed across vfs/kernfs/sysfs/procfs/fs, with every mutation test revert-proved (restoring the ordinal loop makes it FAIL); both arches build; stack-gate PASS; both smokes PASS. |
| 90 | common | `chmod` | `sys_chmod` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/perms.rs:16` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/open.c, fs/namei.c, fs/stat.c, fs/xattr.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Ledger D5/B641: chmod/chown/utime family now routes through VFS setattr and ext4 persists mode, owner, and nanosecond times; remaining full-row audit covers all permission/idmap/symlink errno ordering. Closed in `B1586`, which fixed the shared setattr ladder: there was NO `may_setattr` gate at all (so `chattr +i`/`+a` inodes accepted chmod/chown/chgrp and explicit timestamp changes), `inode_permission` never consulted `S_IMMUTABLE` (so truncate and `open(O_WRONLY)` on an immutable file succeeded), `chown_ok`/`chgrp_ok` were gated such that ANY caller could name the file's current uid/gid and get success where Linux returns EPERM, a combined iattr applied in the wrong order with chmod's S_ISGID test ignoring an accompanying `ATTR_GID`, `apply_kill_priv` re-tested S_IXGRP and undid whatever set the flag, there was no `EOVERFLOW` rule, and symlink `EOPNOTSUPP` preceded the EROFS gate. Also removed a split source of truth: `vfs::{may_chmod, may_chown, chmod_sgid_strip, chown_kill_priv}` was a second DAC ladder with zero production callers that had already drifted. chmod/chown work moved out of the shim into `fs/src/chattr.rs` per `53`: neither carried `ATTR_CTIME`, so a mode/owner change left timestamps untouched and `chown(-1,-1)` was a total no-op; chown set a blanket `ATTR_KILL_SGID`, destroying a bare S_ISGID mandatory-lock mark for an in-group caller; ids now cross the user namespace through the B1573 owner rather than a second translation path. Harness: `setattr_may_setattr_gate` (6), `setattr_chown_ok_ladder` (7), `chattr_model` (9), `fs::mknod` (4), `sys_truncate_shape` (9), `perm_enforce` rewritten (23); `cargo test -p vfs` 1457/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 66s, smoke-arm PASS 92s. |

| 94 | common | `lchown` | `sys_lchown` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/094_lchown.rs` | `IMPL` | `F740-rename-family-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl:103`; `fs/open.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(lchown)`; `fs/open.c:do_fchownat`; `fs/open.c:chown_common`; `fs/attr.c:setattr_prepare` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Slot 94 shared `092_chown`'s follow=true resolver, so `lchown(2)` changed the SYMLINK TARGET's ownership — the single behaviour the call exists to avoid. Split into its own `094_lchown.rs` with `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW` resolution (Linux `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(lchown)` = `do_fchownat(AT_FDCWD, ..., AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW)`), routed from `perms.rs` on `NR_LCHOWN`. The shared `do_chown` supplies the rest of `chown_common`: `(uid_t)-1` leaves that id untouched, a non-directory target gets `ATTR_KILL_SUID\|ATTR_KILL_SGID` even for the no-op `(-1,-1)` form, and CAP_CHOWN / owner-plus-group-membership plus the EROFS mount gate run inside `notify_change`. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --test rename_ladder_oracle lchown_does_not_follow_the_final_symlink` (host oracle: `lchown` on a DANGLING symlink succeeds while `chown` on the same path returns ENOENT, and `lstat` owner of link and target are both unchanged by `lchown(-1,-1)`); `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch x86_64` and `--arch aarch64`. |
| 95 | common | `umask` | `sys_umask` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:50` | `IMPL` | `F726-fs-simple-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE1(umask), include/linux/fs_struct.h` | `crates/kernel/sched/src/tests/umask.rs` | Audited against `kernel/sys.c` (`mask = xchg(&current->fs->umask, mask & S_IRWXUGO); return mask;`). Returning the PREVIOUS mask and masking to `0777` were already correct. Divergence FIXED: the mask lived on the TASK (`Task.umask`), not on the shared `fs_struct` owner — so threads of one process (`CLONE_FS`) each had a PRIVATE umask, and `umask(2)` in one thread was invisible to its siblings, while `unshare(CLONE_FS)` did not split it. It now lives in `FsContext` (oxide's `fs_struct`), so `CLONE_FS` siblings share one mask, `fork(2)` snapshot-copies it through `inherit_fs_context_from`, and `unshare(CLONE_FS)` detaches it; the explicit umask copy in `clone` is gone as redundant. All readers (`open`/`openat`/`mkdir`/`mkdirat`/`mknod`, `/proc/<pid>/status`) moved to `Task::umask()`. Evidence: `cargo test -p sched` (254 passed, incl. 3 new umask tests). |
| 96 | common | `gettimeofday` | `sys_gettimeofday` | time | fd-table, sched-clock, syscall-abi, time, timers, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/096_gettimeofday.rs` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/time/time.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE2(gettimeofday), include/uapi/linux/time.h` | `time-timeofday-model` | Audited against Linux: writes `tv` when non-NULL, writes `struct timezone` when non-NULL, returns `EFAULT` on either bad output pointer, and preserves syscall ordering. |
| 97 | common | `getrlimit` | `sys_getrlimit` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:14` | `IMPL` | `F777-rlimit-rusage-accounting` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | F777 closes this row. The syscall ladder was already Linux-shaped (F727/F742); PARTIAL was about ENFORCEMENT and about two wrong defaults nobody had checked against `INIT_RLIMITS`. ENFORCEMENT is now complete and every slot has a live consumer: CPU and RTTIME (SIGXCPU-then-SIGKILL ladder off the periodic CPU-timer walk, soft limit bumped a second per hit), FSIZE (SIGXFSZ+EFBIG on write/truncate/copy_file_range), DATA (brk), STACK (execve mmap_base AND the fault-time growth bound, which reads the live rlimit rather than a hardcoded 8 MiB), CORE (dump truncation + file-dump disable), NPROC (per-ucount fork EAGAIN with the init-user and CAP_SYS_RESOURCE/CAP_SYS_ADMIN exemptions), NOFILE (every fd-allocating path + the fs.nr_open ceiling), MEMLOCK (mmap/mlock/mlock2/mlockall admission), AS (mmap/mremap/brk admission + the stack-growth headroom), SIGPENDING (per-user queued-record admission), MSGQUEUE (mq_open charge), NICE (setpriority + sched_setscheduler nice reductions), RTPRIO (sched_setscheduler/sched_setattr RT admission). RSS and LOCKS are inert upstream too. FIXED by F777, two defects, both of the 'machinery without callers' class: (1) `DEFAULT_RLIMITS` had FOUR slots at `RLIM_INFINITY` that `INIT_RLIMITS` (after `fork_init`'s two `max_threads/2` overwrites) sets otherwise -- NICE {0,0}, RTPRIO {0,0}, MSGQUEUE {819200,819200}, NPROC {max_threads/2, max_threads/2}. NICE and RTPRIO are the load-bearing pair: the `setpriority(2)` and `sched_setscheduler(2)` ladders consult exactly those two slots and are written correctly, so an infinite default made both unable to refuse ANYTHING -- any unprivileged process could take nice -20 or SCHED_FIFO priority 99 with no CAP_SYS_NICE. A procfs test had even PINNED the wrong MSGQUEUE value as correct. (2) The hard-limit-raise gate in slots 160 and 302 passed `has_cap(CAP_SYS_RESOURCE)`, a plain effective-set test, where Linux asks `capable()` = `ns_capable(&init_user_ns, ...)`; `perm_common::capable` existed and encoded exactly this, and neither slot called it, so root inside an unprivileged user namespace could raise any hard limit after one `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)`. Both slots now route through `rlimit_policy::cap_sys_resource`, an UNGATED helper, so the composition the slot actually evaluates is the thing under test. Ordering re-verified: resource index EINVAL precedes the copy-out on 097; the copy-in precedes everything on 160; EFAULT outranks ESRCH/EPERM on 302; `old` is written only on success. ARM routing verified: aarch64 163/164/165/153/261 map to x86 97/160/98/100/302. Harness: `cargo test -p sched -p syscalls -p syscall -p procfs -p ipc -p vmm -p fs` 4633 passed / 0 failed, including 2 new `rlimit_policy` tests driving the real `cap_sys_resource` over a real `sched::Task` moved into and out of a non-initial user namespace (raise refused with the capability held, permitted once back in the initial namespace, lower still unprivileged), a full 16-slot `INIT_RLIMITS` conformance table, and a `nice_to_rlimit` allowance test proving the default admits no priority increase. Both arches `xtask kernel --check` clean. |
| 98 | common | `getrusage` | `sys_getrusage` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:16` | `IMPL` | `F779-rusage-times-child-rollup` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | F779 closes this row. Audited against `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(getrusage)` -> `getrusage(current, who, &r)`. Ordering: `who` is validated BEFORE the buffer is touched, so a bad `who` with a bad pointer reports EINVAL and not EFAULT; `ru == NULL` is a genuine EFAULT here (unlike the wait family's optional out-param). `RUSAGE_BOTH` is kernel-internal and rejected. Field fill matches upstream exactly: 9 of 16 members have a counter behind them (ru_utime, ru_stime, ru_maxrss, ru_minflt, ru_majflt, ru_inblock, ru_oublock, ru_nvcsw, ru_nivcsw); the other 7 (ru_ixrss, ru_idrss, ru_isrss, ru_nswap, ru_msgsnd, ru_msgrcv, ru_nsignals) are left ZERO, which is what upstream reports too -- they are not omissions. ru_inblock/ru_oublock are 512-byte sectors, not syscall counts. `RUSAGE_SELF` is the whole thread group (live threads plus the residue of exited ones), `RUSAGE_THREAD` is the calling thread alone but still takes `ru_maxrss` from the process (RSS is a property of the shared mm), `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` is the reaped-children accumulator with NO mm fold, matching the `out_children` arm. FIXED by F779, the child-accounting rollup ran at the WRONG POINT: the fold into the parent's c* counters happened in `enqueue_zombie` (exit) instead of the reap. `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` is defined over children that terminated AND WERE WAITED FOR, and upstream does this work in `wait_task_zombie` under `state == EXIT_DEAD && thread_group_leader(p)`. Three observable divergences fell out of that: an unreaped zombie was already counted; a `waitid(WNOWAIT)` peek left the child waitable but already accounted (systemd's peek-then-reap SIGCHLD handler is the real-world caller); and an AUTO-REAPED child (SIGCHLD=SIG_IGN / SA_NOCLDWAIT) was accounted at all, which upstream never does. The fold now happens on the one reap arm that consumes the zombie -- not the `WNOWAIT` peek, not the `EXIT_TRACE` tracer hand-back (the real parent's later reap accounts it) -- is gated on thread-group leadership, credits the REAPER's thread group, and reuses the same `RUSAGE_BOTH` snapshot the wait already computed for its out-param, so the reap reports and accounts one number. Also FIXED: the `getrusage` `who` dispatch was a `match` with a catch-all arm that answered `RUSAGE_SELF` for anything unrecognised; it is now the ungated `syscall::rusage::getrusage_source` returning a 3-variant enum, so the dispatch is exhaustive and hosted-testable. Harness: 7 tests in a new `sched::tests::child_rusage` driving the REAL global zombie list and the real `enqueue_zombie`/`peek_one`/`reap_one` path -- subtree fold, unreaped zombie invisible, WNOWAIT peek not accounted then accounted exactly once by the reap, double-reap impossible, autoreaped child never accounted, thread-group visibility from a sibling, high-water mark a max not a sum. Both existing exit-time tests FAILED against the fix before being rewritten, which is the negative control. `cargo test -p syscall -p sched -p syscalls -p procfs -p elf-load -p ipc -p vmm -p fs` 4654 passed / 0 failed. Both arches `xtask kernel --check` clean. |
| 99 | common | `sysinfo` | `sys_sysinfo` | security | procfs, security, syscall-abi, sysfs, system-control, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:18` | `IMPL` | `F727-sysinfo-limits-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c, kernel/module/, kernel/reboot.c` | `cred-security-model` | Completed `sysinfo(2)` audit against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master kernel/sys.c `do_sysinfo` + mm/show_mem.c `si_meminfo` + kernel/sched/loadavg.c `get_avenrun`. Verified `struct sysinfo` is 112 B LP64 with every field at its uapi offset, and that `mem_unit=1` with byte-valued fields is `do_sysinfo`'s post-rescale state on a 64-bit host (the page->byte shift never overflows). FIXED divergences: (1) `uptime` truncated instead of Linux's `tv_sec + (tv_nsec ? 1 : 0)` round-up; (2) `sharedram` was hardcoded 0 -- now `NR_SHMEM` from the VFS shmem accounting that already feeds `/proc/meminfo` `Shmem:`. Confirmed-correct zeros (Linux reports the same, NOT stubs): `totalhigh`/`freehigh` (no CONFIG_HIGHMEM on 64-bit) and `bufferram` (`nr_blockdev_pages()` counts RAW BLOCK-DEVICE inode mappings; oxide has no bdev-inode page cache, so every cached page is a file page Linux counts under `Cached`). `loads[]` were already correctly rescaled to `SI_LOAD_SHIFT`=16 (not the scheduler's FSHIFT=11) -- now locked by test. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib sysinfo_abi` (9 tests: 112-byte size, all uapi offsets, per-field placement, u16 `procs` not bleeding into `pad`, zeroed `_f[]` tail, mem_unit rationale, SI_LOAD_SHIFT rescale, uptime round-up, highmem zeros). Both arches build. |
| 100 | common | `times` | `sys_times` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:17` | `IMPL` | `F779-rusage-times-child-rollup` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | F779 closes this row. Audited against `SYSCALL_DEFINE1(times)` / `do_sys_times`. `tms_utime`/`tms_stime` are the whole PROCESS's user/system time (not the calling thread), `tms_cutime`/`tms_cstime` are the reaped-children accumulator, all four process-wide so a sibling thread's reap is visible. A NULL `tms` is legal and still returns the tick count rather than EFAULT. The return is a tick count since boot, forced successful, so a large count is never read as an errno; only the exact `(clock_t)-1` bit pattern is the error report, and at USER_HZ a 64-bit ns source cannot reach the errno window -- the only wraparound that could is the ns counter's own. FIXED by F779: the CLK_TCK divisor was open-coded in FOUR places that must agree by construction -- `AT_CLKTCK` (the value `sysconf(_SC_CLK_TCK)` reads, in `elf-load`), the `times(2)`/`/proc/<pid>/stat` conversion in `sched::clock`, and two more in `/proc/uptime`'s centisecond math -- a split source of truth whose only symptom would be userspace's `ticks / sysconf(_SC_CLK_TCK)` being quietly wrong. `USER_HZ`, `NS_PER_USER_TICK` and `ns_to_clock_t` now live in the leaf ABI crate `syscall::rusage` and every consumer reads them. The child rollup is shared with `getrusage(RUSAGE_CHILDREN)` -- ONE accumulator, so the two syscalls cannot disagree -- and moved from exit to reap; see row 98 for that fix. Harness: 3 new `syscall::rusage` tests (USER_HZ agrees with the auxv value; the conversion truncates rather than rounding, so `times` never reports more CPU than was consumed; the widest possible sample still encodes as a positive `clock_t`), plus `a_reaped_childs_whole_subtree_cost...` asserting `child_acct().cpu_ns()` equals the `getrusage` snapshot's utime/stime. Totals as row 98. |
| 101 | 64 | `ptrace` | `sys_ptrace` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/101_ptrace.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `B1638-ptrace-prctl-personality` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | Audited vs `kernel/ptrace.c` + `arch/x86/kernel/ptrace.c` (linux-master v7.2.0-rc4). Fixed: PEEK now `put_user`s the word into `data` and returns 0 (was returning the word as the syscall rv, so any word with the sign bit set surfaced as a bogus errno through glibc); PEEK/POKE short foreign access is EIO not EFAULT; PEEKUSER/POKEUSER bound-check `struct user` (misaligned or >= 928 = EIO, out-of-range register write no longer fake-succeeds, DR writes EIO); GETREGS/SETREGS now build a real 27-quadword `user_regs_struct` from the entry frame (was a verbatim 15-slot frame copy = wrong field in every slot) and are EIO on aarch64 as in Linux; GETREGSET/SETREGSET now read the `struct iovec` from `data`, honour the NT_ type in `addr`, clamp and write back `iov_len` (previously treated `data` as the buffer, corrupting the tracer's iovec — and GETREGSET is arm64's only GP-register path); aarch64 frame base corrected 0xD0 -> the recorded `svc_frame`/0x120 (old offset read x19..x28 as x0..x17 and wrote 0x40 past the stack top); `__ptrace_may_access` now uses the caller's REAL uid/gid per PTRACE_MODE_ATTACH_REALCREDS and applies the dumpable gate; ATTACH refuses kthreads and exiting targets; TRACEME on an already-traced task is EPERM; CONT/SYSCALL/SINGLESTEP/DETACH validate the signal (EIO); SEIZE validates addr/options (EIO) vs SETOPTIONS (EINVAL) incl. SUSPEND_SECCOMP CAP_SYS_ADMIN; INTERRUPT/LISTEN require PT_SEIZED; GETSIGINFO/SETSIGINFO are EINVAL with no last_siginfo (was a synthesised SIGTRAP); GETSIGMASK/SETSIGMASK added; unknown request is EIO not EINVAL; syscall stops report `SIGTRAP\ B1638: PTRACE_LISTEN had no latch (new sched::jobctl); group-stop participation bookkeeping absent repo-wide (one ^Z on a threaded process owes one CLD_STOPPED per thread); stop_until_cont_code posted CLD_CONTINUED on every resume and any signal woke a STOPPED task. HW debug regs DR0-7 end-to-end (DR7 ladder, DR6 classifier, ctxsw install, #DB -> TRAP_HWBKPT) plus arm64 NT_ARM_HW_BREAK/WATCH with slot count from ID_AA64DFR0_EL1. Yama holes: pidfd_getfd and process_vm_readv/writev used the READ-class ladder, so at ptrace_scope=1 a same-uid process could read another's memory. Seccomp ran BEFORE the ptrace entry stop, letting a tracer substitute an already-approved call. GAPS: arm64 step-over-a-hit re-arm latches; dual-parent notify on reparented group-stop completion. \|0x80` under PTRACE_O_TRACESYSGOOD. Hosted tests: 37 in `s101_ptrace_{decide,perm,regs}`. NOT implemented, honestly EIO: PEEKSIGINFO, GET/SET_SYSCALL_INFO, SECCOMP_GET_FILTER/METADATA, GET_RSEQ_CONFIGURATION, SYSCALL_USER_DISPATCH_CONFIG, GETFDPIC; no hardware debug registers (DR0-7 read 0, write EIO); PTRACE_EVENT_* fork/clone/exec stops are not generated; Yama `ptrace_scope` not consulted; single-stop-per-request state machine not exercised under a real tracer (no boot verification in this lane). B1972 re-verified the six gaps this row's tail names; FOUR are CLOSED. PEEKSIGINFO, GET/SET_SYSCALL_INFO, SECCOMP_GET_FILTER/METADATA, GET_RSEQ_CONFIGURATION and SYSCALL_USER_DISPATCH_CONFIG all have real bodies in `101_ptrace/info.rs` with buffer validation and encoding through `sysinfo.rs`; GETFDPIC stays EIO, which is correct, since it serves a memory model neither target architecture uses. Hardware debug registers are live on both arches -- x86 through a real validation ladder, context-switch install and trap classifier, arm64 through the hardware breakpoint and watchpoint regsets. PTRACE_EVENT_* fork/clone/exec stops ARE generated, from the clone path (fork/vfork/clone plus vfork-done) and the exec path. The ptrace scope sysctl IS consulted, on both the traceme and the attach/seize routes. The hosted test count is 98 across decide/event/perm/regs/sigstop/sysinfo, not 37. The arm64 step-over-a-hit item is MIS-SCOPED: the reference registers a non-default overflow handler for ptrace hardware breakpoints, which skips the step-over dance entirely and delivers SIGTRAP -- exactly what this kernel does -- so the step-over primitive serves default-handler (perf) breakpoints, has no caller here, and is filed as its own row rather than as a ptrace gap. The ONE real remaining gap is dual-parent notification, and this row states it wrongly: the reference's second notify is not on group-stop completion. A ptrace-stop notifies the tracer AND, when that stop completed a group stop and the two parents differ, the real parent as well; and the continued path notifies the real parent, then the group leader's tracer when the task is reparented. This kernel issues exactly one call with a single target, in `sched::live::stop` via `jobctl::notify_target`. Owned by the sched lane. Details in `scratch/issues.d/B1972-syscall-abi-partial-rows.md`. |
| 102 | common | `getuid` | `sys_getuid` | security | credentials, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/sched/src/cred/uid.rs:45` | `IMPL` | `F724-cred-syscall-audit` | `kernel/sys.c`, `kernel/cred.c:commit_creds`, `kernel/groups.c`, `security/commoncap.c:cap_task_fix_setuid/cap_emulate_setxuid`, `include/uapi/linux/limits.h:NGROUPS_MAX`, `include/linux/uidgid.h:uid_valid` | `sched::cred::tests::{setuid,setresid,groups,getres,fsid,capfix}` (67 hosted cases); `vfs::namei::group_list::tests`; x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds | Audited against `kernel/sys.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE0(getuid)`. Returns `cred->uid` (real uid); no arguments, no error path. Covered by the transition harness, which asserts the real uid after every set*id form. REMAINING: user-namespace id translation (`make_kuid`/`from_kuid_munged`, `kernel/user_namespace.c`) is not modelled anywhere in-tree — every subsystem (chown/stat/getuid/setuid alike) operates on HOST ids, so inside a non-init user namespace this syscall accepts/returns host rather than namespace-relative ids. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. Closed in `B1573`: uid/gid arguments now map through `make_kuid`/`make_kgid` before any permission check (unmapped -> `EINVAL`, ahead of the EPERM ladder and the `setres*id` no-op short circuit) and results leave through `from_kuid_munged`/`from_kgid_munged` (unmapped -> overflow id); `setfsuid`/`setfsgid` report the previous id in namespace numbering and treat an unmapped argument as the invalid id; `setgroups` validates per element EFAULT-then-EINVAL left to right and sorts like `groups_sort`. `cap_emulate_setxuid` compares against `make_kuid(ns, 0)` rather than the literal 0, so a namespaced root drops capabilities on leaving root and a host-uid-0 task in a namespace that cannot name it is not root. `ns_capable_setid`'s target namespace is the caller's own, so it reduces exactly to an effective-set check. Harness: `sched::cred::tests::userns` (20 cases), `user_namespace::resolve::tests` (6); `cargo test -p sched` 678/678, `-p user-namespace` 28/28; both arches build and boot to `basic.target`. |
| 103 | common | `syslog` | `sys_syslog` | security | procfs, security, syscall-abi, sysfs, system-control, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/103_syslog.rs` | `IMPL` | `F732-ptrace-syslog-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c, kernel/module/, kernel/reboot.c` | `cred-security-model` | Audited vs `kernel/printk/printk.c` `do_syslog`/`check_syslog_permissions` (linux-master v7.2.0-rc4). Fixed: added the permission gate that was entirely absent — CAP_SYSLOG (not CAP_SYS_ADMIN) with `syslog_action_restricted`, so an unprivileged caller gets READ_ALL and SIZE_BUFFER only, and nothing when `kernel.dmesg_restrict` is set; that sysctl leaf is now bound to the live klog flag instead of a dead `Int(0)`; READ blocks until the ring has unread bytes and consumes from a real syslog cursor (was a non-blocking tail read that never advanced); READ_CLEAR/CLEAR now move the clear point (were no-ops); READ_ALL honours the clear point; SIZE_UNREAD reports unread bytes from the cursor (was `min(total, ring_size)`); CONSOLE_LEVEL validates 1..=8 with EINVAL outside and now actually gates console output — klog records carry a Linux loglevel and `console::fan_out` is suppressed when `level >= console_loglevel`, with CONSOLE_OFF/ON saving and restoring it; `/dev/kmsg` writes are classified by their `<N>` prefix; NULL buf or negative len is EINVAL before the EFAULT check (was EFAULT). Work moved out of the ABI crate (`syscall::dmesg`, deleted) into `103_syslog.rs` + hosted-tested `103_syslog/decide.rs` per `53`. Hosted tests: 10 decide + 5 klog console-level. Divergence: console default loglevel is 8 (`CONSOLE_LOGLEVEL_DEBUG`) so existing kdebug boot output is unchanged until userspace lowers it; a blocked READ is woken by a 20ms deadline re-park rather than from the emit path (klog's `ring_push` runs in NMI/lock-held contexts, where Linux defers via irq_work); READ_ALL/SIZE_UNREAD are byte-granular, not record-granular; not boot-verified in this lane. |

FIXED: `(uid_t)-1` is now `EINVAL` (`uid_valid`) instead of being stored as a real uid; the unprivileged target set is now `{ruid, suid}` ONLY — the previous code also accepted the current EFFECTIVE uid, letting a task re-acquire an identity Linux forbids. `CAP_SETUID` sets real+saved+effective+fs; without it only effective+fs move. `cap_emulate_setxuid` runs on the resulting triple, and `commit_creds` downgrades dumpability + clears `pdeath_signal`. REMAINING: user-namespace id translation (`make_kuid`/`from_kuid_munged`, `kernel/user_namespace.c`) is not modelled anywhere in-tree — every subsystem (chown/stat/getuid/setuid alike) operates on HOST ids, so inside a non-init user namespace this syscall accepts/returns host rather than namespace-relative ids. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. Closed in `B1573`: uid/gid arguments now map through `make_kuid`/`make_kgid` before any permission check (unmapped -> `EINVAL`, ahead of the EPERM ladder and the `setres*id` no-op short circuit) and results leave through `from_kuid_munged`/`from_kgid_munged` (unmapped -> overflow id); `setfsuid`/`setfsgid` report the previous id in namespace numbering and treat an unmapped argument as the invalid id; `setgroups` validates per element EFAULT-then-EINVAL left to right and sorts like `groups_sort`. `cap_emulate_setxuid` compares against `make_kuid(ns, 0)` rather than the literal 0, so a namespaced root drops capabilities on leaving root and a host-uid-0 task in a namespace that cannot name it is not root. `ns_capable_setid`'s target namespace is the caller's own, so it reduces exactly to an effective-set check. Harness: `sched::cred::tests::userns` (20 cases), `user_namespace::resolve::tests` (6); `cargo test -p sched` 678/678, `-p user-namespace` 28/28; both arches build and boot to `basic.target`. REMAINING: `set_user()`/`flag_nproc_exceeded()` — no per-user process accounting (ucounts) exists kernel-wide, so a setuid to a uid at `RLIMIT_NPROC` cannot arm the later execve `EAGAIN`. Fork/exit/exec accounting lane. Closed in `F772`: per-user hierarchical process accounting now exists (`crates/kernel/ucounts`), so `set_user()`/`flag_nproc_exceeded()` is real — the charge moves between accounts on a real-uid change, `PF_NPROC_EXCEEDED` is a `Task` field, and the deferred `EAGAIN` fires at the next `execve` on both arches while the `set*id` call itself still succeeds. Charges are hierarchical (a nested user namespace also charges its creator's account, bounded by the ceiling recorded at namespace creation), so `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` is not an escape from a uid's quota. | 106 | common | `setgid` | `sys_setgid` | security | credentials, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/sched/src/cred/gid.rs:79` | `IMPL` | `F724-cred-syscall-audit` | `kernel/sys.c`, `kernel/cred.c:commit_creds`, `kernel/groups.c`, `security/commoncap.c:cap_task_fix_setuid/cap_emulate_setxuid`, `include/uapi/linux/limits.h:NGROUPS_MAX`, `include/linux/uidgid.h:uid_valid` | `sched::cred::tests::{setuid,setresid,groups,getres,fsid,capfix}` (67 hosted cases); `vfs::namei::group_list::tests`; x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds | Audited against `kernel/sys.c:__sys_setgid`. FIXED: `(gid_t)-1` is `EINVAL`; unprivileged target set narrowed to `{rgid, sgid}` (the effective gid was wrongly accepted). commoncap installs no `task_fix_setgid` hook, so no capability juggle runs — verified by a test asserting caps are untouched. REMAINING: user-namespace id translation (`make_kuid`/`from_kuid_munged`, `kernel/user_namespace.c`) is not modelled anywhere in-tree — every subsystem (chown/stat/getuid/setuid alike) operates on HOST ids, so inside a non-init user namespace this syscall accepts/returns host rather than namespace-relative ids. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. Closed in `B1573`: uid/gid arguments now map through `make_kuid`/`make_kgid` before any permission check (unmapped -> `EINVAL`, ahead of the EPERM ladder and the `setres*id` no-op short circuit) and results leave through `from_kuid_munged`/`from_kgid_munged` (unmapped -> overflow id); `setfsuid`/`setfsgid` report the previous id in namespace numbering and treat an unmapped argument as the invalid id; `setgroups` validates per element EFAULT-then-EINVAL left to right and sorts like `groups_sort`. `cap_emulate_setxuid` compares against `make_kuid(ns, 0)` rather than the literal 0, so a namespaced root drops capabilities on leaving root and a host-uid-0 task in a namespace that cannot name it is not root. `ns_capable_setid`'s target namespace is the caller's own, so it reduces exactly to an effective-set check. Harness: `sched::cred::tests::userns` (20 cases), `user_namespace::resolve::tests` (6); `cargo test -p sched` 678/678, `-p user-namespace` 28/28; both arches build and boot to `basic.target`. |

| 109 | common | `setpgid` | `sys_setpgid` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:53` | `IMPL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1114 SYSCALL_DEFINE2(setpgid)`, `include/linux/sched/signal.h same_thread_group`, `fs/exec.c begin_new_exec (PF_FORKNOEXEC)` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Rewrote as an ABI shim over `sched::session::setpgid`. Was a bare `pgid.store` with NO validation. Now the full Linux ladder in Linux's order: `pgid<0` EINVAL (after the pid/pgid 0-aliases, so `setpgid(-5,0)` is EINVAL not ESRCH); unknown/negative pid ESRCH; non-thread-group-leader target EINVAL; child in a different session EPERM; child that already exec'd EACCES (Linux returns EACCES here, not EPERM) via the new `Task::forknoexec` (`PF_FORKNOEXEC`, set at fork, cleared in `reset_per_execve_state`); target neither self nor child ESRCH; session-leader target EPERM; destination pgrp absent or in another session EPERM. `pid==0` now resolves the caller's PROCESS (was the calling thread). pgid moved from `Task` to `ThreadGroup`, so a move applies to every thread of the process (Linux `task->signal`) instead of only the caller. Harness: 27 hosted tests in `crates/kernel/sched/src/tests/session.rs`; `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; x86_64+aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86`/`smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target`. |
| 110 | common | `getppid` | `sys_getppid` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1016 SYSCALL_DEFINE0(getppid)` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | ABI shim over `sched::session::getppid` = Linux `task_tgid_vnr(real_parent)`. Resolves the parent's thread-group LEADER before taking the namespace-visible pid, so a child forked by a worker thread reports the parent PROCESS, not the parent thread; falls back to the process vpid when no namespace mapping exists (previously returned 0 in that case). Parent gone / outside the pid namespace still reports 0, as Linux does for pid 1. Harness: `tests/session.rs` getppid cases; `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; both arches build + boot. |
| 111 | common | `getpgrp` | `sys_getpgrp` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:26` | `IMPL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1222 SYSCALL_DEFINE0(getpgrp)`, `kernel/sys.c:1187 do_getpgid` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Linux defines getpgrp as `do_getpgid(0)`; the shim now routes to the same `sched::session::getpgid` work fn with `pid==0` instead of reading the field directly, and reports the process-wide pgid from `ThreadGroup`. The old `unwrap_or(1)` fallback (a fabricated pgid 1 with no current task) is gone. Harness: `tests/session.rs`; `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; both arches build + boot. |
| 112 | common | `setsid` | `sys_setsid` | sched | namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, tty, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:54` | `IMPL` | `F725-session-pgid-audit` | `kernel/sys.c:1268 ksys_setsid`, `kernel/sys.c:1257 set_special_pids`, `drivers/tty/tty_jobctrl.c proc_clear_tty` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | ABI shim over `sched::session::setsid`. Was unconditional success. Now both Linux EPERM gates: already a session leader (new `ThreadGroup::session_leader`, Linux `signal_struct::leader`, latched atomically so a failed call never latches), and a process group already numbered with the caller's pid (`pid_task(sid, PIDTYPE_PGID)`) — which is the 'caller is already a process group leader' case. sid+pgid are set on the thread group, so every thread of the process joins the new session/group. REMAINING: the controlling terminal is still a per-`Task` field, so setsid clears only the calling thread's `ctty`; Linux keeps the tty in `signal_struct` and `proc_clear_tty(group_leader)` drops it process-wide. Moving `ctty` to `ThreadGroup` is a tty-lane change (console/routing, tty_ioctl, openat `/dev/tty`, clone) and is NOT done here. Harness: `tests/session.rs` setsid cases (new session+group, EPERM re-entry, EPERM as pgrp leader, EPERM on a pre-existing group number, whole-thread-group move, ctty cleared); `cargo test -p sched` 282/282; both arches build + boot. Closed in `B1573`: the controlling terminal moved to `ThreadGroup` (Linux `signal_struct::tty`), spinlock-guarded because the hangup walk clears a foreign process's terminal from another CPU. `setsid` drops it for every thread of the process, `TIOCSCTTY` claims it process-wide, fork snapshots it into the child's fresh group and `CLONE_THREAD` shares it. Harness: `sched::tests::session` (3 cases). |
| 113 | common | `setreuid` | `sys_setreuid` | security | credentials, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/sched/src/cred/uid.rs:109` | `IMPL` | `F724-cred-syscall-audit` | `kernel/sys.c`, `kernel/cred.c:commit_creds`, `kernel/groups.c`, `security/commoncap.c:cap_task_fix_setuid/cap_emulate_setxuid`, `include/uapi/linux/limits.h:NGROUPS_MAX`, `include/linux/uidgid.h:uid_valid` | `sched::cred::tests::{setuid,setresid,groups,getres,fsid,capfix}` (67 hosted cases); `vfs::namei::group_list::tests`; x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds | Audited against `kernel/sys.c:__sys_setreuid`. FIXED: the real-uid target set is `{ruid, euid}` (the saved uid was wrongly accepted); and the saved uid now follows the new effective uid ONLY when `ruid != -1` or the new euid differs from the OLD real uid — previously `setreuid(-1,-1)` clobbered the saved uid whenever real != effective, destroying the hop-back hatch. fsuid always mirrors the new euid. EPERM is reported for the real uid before the effective uid, with no partial application. REMAINING: user-namespace id translation (`make_kuid`/`from_kuid_munged`, `kernel/user_namespace.c`) is not modelled anywhere in-tree — every subsystem (chown/stat/getuid/setuid alike) operates on HOST ids, so inside a non-init user namespace this syscall accepts/returns host rather than namespace-relative ids. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. Closed in `B1573`: uid/gid arguments now map through `make_kuid`/`make_kgid` before any permission check (unmapped -> `EINVAL`, ahead of the EPERM ladder and the `setres*id` no-op short circuit) and results leave through `from_kuid_munged`/`from_kgid_munged` (unmapped -> overflow id); `setfsuid`/`setfsgid` report the previous id in namespace numbering and treat an unmapped argument as the invalid id; `setgroups` validates per element EFAULT-then-EINVAL left to right and sorts like `groups_sort`. `cap_emulate_setxuid` compares against `make_kuid(ns, 0)` rather than the literal 0, so a namespaced root drops capabilities on leaving root and a host-uid-0 task in a namespace that cannot name it is not root. `ns_capable_setid`'s target namespace is the caller's own, so it reduces exactly to an effective-set check. Harness: `sched::cred::tests::userns` (20 cases), `user_namespace::resolve::tests` (6); `cargo test -p sched` 678/678, `-p user-namespace` 28/28; both arches build and boot to `basic.target`. REMAINING: `set_user()`/`flag_nproc_exceeded()` — no per-user process accounting (ucounts) exists kernel-wide, so a setuid to a uid at `RLIMIT_NPROC` cannot arm the later execve `EAGAIN`. Fork/exit/exec accounting lane. Closed in `F772`: per-user hierarchical process accounting now exists (`crates/kernel/ucounts`), so `set_user()`/`flag_nproc_exceeded()` is real — the charge moves between accounts on a real-uid change, `PF_NPROC_EXCEEDED` is a `Task` field, and the deferred `EAGAIN` fires at the next `execve` on both arches while the `set*id` call itself still succeeds. Charges are hierarchical (a nested user namespace also charges its creator's account, bounded by the ceiling recorded at namespace creation), so `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` is not an escape from a uid's quota. Harness: `cargo test -p ucounts` 12/0, `-p sched` 691/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 and smoke-arm PASS through systemd's full fork/exec storm. |

FIXED: the limit is now `NGROUPS_MAX` (65536), not 32 — the credential holds a heap `group_info` instead of an inline 32-entry array, and the DAC snapshot (`vfs::Cred`) shares that whole list so a user in >32 groups is no longer silently denied. The list is `groups_sort`ed ascending before install (membership is now the `groups_search` binary search). Error ORDER matches Linux exactly: `EPERM` from `may_setgroups` FIRST (even for an illegal size and an unreadable pointer), then `(unsigned)gidsetsize > NGROUPS_MAX` -> `EINVAL`, then `ENOMEM` from the allocation, then per-element `EFAULT`/`EINVAL` left to right; a failed call leaves the previous list intact. `may_setgroups` now also honours the `/proc/PID/setgroups` deny policy (CVE-2014-8989) via `user_namespace::setgroups_policy`, which was previously ignored. Fork/exit/exec accounting lane. Closed in `F772`: per-user hierarchical process accounting now exists (`crates/kernel/ucounts`), so `set_user()`/`flag_nproc_exceeded()` is real — the charge moves between accounts on a real-uid change, `PF_NPROC_EXCEEDED` is a `Task` field, and the deferred `EAGAIN` fires at the next `execve` on both arches while the `set*id` call itself still succeeds. Charges are hierarchical (a nested user namespace also charges its creator's account, bounded by the ceiling recorded at namespace creation), so `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` is not an escape from a uid's quota. Harness: `cargo test -p ucounts` 12/0, `-p sched` 691/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 and smoke-arm PASS through systemd's full fork/exec storm. |

FIXED: a NULL output pointer used to be SKIPPED and the call returned 0; Linux `put_user` faults, so all three pointers now go through `uaccess::copy_to_user` in r/e/s order and the first failure returns `EFAULT` (with the earlier writes already committed, as in Linux). Raw `write_volatile` is gone, so an in-range unmapped page recovers through the exception table instead of faulting in kernel mode. REMAINING: user-namespace id translation (`make_kuid`/`from_kuid_munged`, `kernel/user_namespace.c`) is not modelled anywhere in-tree — every subsystem (chown/stat/getuid/setuid alike) operates on HOST ids, so inside a non-init user namespace this syscall accepts/returns host rather than namespace-relative ids. Closing it is a kernel-wide cred-model lane, not a cred-syscall-local fix. `ns_capable_setid` likewise degrades to a global effective-set check because capabilities are not per-user-namespace yet. Closed in `B1573`: uid/gid arguments now map through `make_kuid`/`make_kgid` before any permission check (unmapped -> `EINVAL`, ahead of the EPERM ladder and the `setres*id` no-op short circuit) and results leave through `from_kuid_munged`/`from_kgid_munged` (unmapped -> overflow id); `setfsuid`/`setfsgid` report the previous id in namespace numbering and treat an unmapped argument as the invalid id; `setgroups` validates per element EFAULT-then-EINVAL left to right and sorts like `groups_sort`. `cap_emulate_setxuid` compares against `make_kuid(ns, 0)` rather than the literal 0, so a namespaced root drops capabilities on leaving root and a host-uid-0 task in a namespace that cannot name it is not root. `ns_capable_setid`'s target namespace is the caller's own, so it reduces exactly to an effective-set check. Harness: `sched::cred::tests::userns` (20 cases), `user_namespace::resolve::tests` (6); `cargo test -p sched` 678/678, `-p user-namespace` 28/28; both arches build and boot to `basic.target`. |

| 125 | common | `capget` | `sys_capget` | security | credentials, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/sched/src/cred.rs:435` | `IMPL` | `F759-socket-cap-keyring-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c, kernel/cred.c, security/` | `cred-security-model` | F759 completed the row on top of B1439's NULL-`dataptr` probe ladder (preserved: a probe returns 0 even on bad magic, after the corrected version is written back, and returns BEFORE `cap_get_target_pid` so the header pid is never resolved). Audited against `kernel/capability.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(capget)` + `cap_validate_magic` + `security/commoncap.c` `cap_capget`. Verified correct: v1 carries one 32-bit block and v2/v3 two (v2 falls through to v3); a v1 request silently drops the upper 32 bits, which is Linux's documented fail-safe rather than -ERANGE; `pid < 0` is EINVAL; the target is resolved by namespace vpid; there is deliberately NO permission gate, matching `cap_capget`, which any task may call for any pid. FIXED: `Creds::CAP_FULL` was all 64 bits, so capget reported 23 undefined capabilities above `CAP_CHECKPOINT_RESTORE` that Linux masks out of every cred; it is now `CAP_VALID_MASK` == `BIT_ULL(CAP_LAST_CAP+1)-1`. Decision logic lives in `sched/src/cred/cap_policy.rs` with 13 hosted tests. |
| 126 | common | `capset` | `sys_capset` | security | credentials, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/sched/src/cred.rs:436` | `IMPL` | `F759-socket-cap-keyring-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c, kernel/cred.c, security/` | `cred-security-model` | F759 audited against `kernel/capability.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(capset)` and `security/commoncap.c` `cap_capset`. Verified correct: only the calling thread may be targeted (pid 0 or the caller's own vpid, anything else EPERM), bad magic is EINVAL with the version written back and has no probe exemption, `new_permitted` must be a subset of OLD permitted, `new_effective` a subset of the NEW permitted, and ambient is masked to `permitted & inheritable`. FIXED three admission errors. (1) The inheritable rule was `I subset of (old_I \| old_P) & bounding`; Linux runs TWO separate tests -- `I subset of (old_I \| old_P)` and `I subset of (old_I \| old_bset)` -- so an inheritable bit the task already holds survives a later `PR_CAPBSET_DROP` of that same bit, which a capget/modify/capset round-trip after `CapabilityBoundingSet=` depends on and which the intersected form rejected with EPERM. (2) `cap_inh_is_capped()` is FALSE for a task holding CAP_SETPCAP in effect, which lifts the first test entirely and lets such a task raise any inheritable bit inside the bounding set; that relaxation was enforced away. (3) `mk_kernel_cap` masks the incoming u32 pair with `CAP_VALID_MASK` BEFORE any subset test, so a full-capability root task writing ~0 succeeds on Linux while the unmasked comparison returned EPERM. Whole policy extracted to `cred/cap_policy.rs::capset_check` with 13 hosted tests naming each rule. |
| 127 | 64 | `rt_sigpending` | `sys_rt_sigpending` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:95` | `IMPL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `kernel/signal.c:3383 do_sigpending, kernel/signal.c:3400 SYSCALL_DEFINE2(rt_sigpending)` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `do_sigpending` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:3383`. FIXED: (a) sigsetsize rule was `!= 8` (EINVAL); Linux uses `> sizeof(sigset_t)`, so a legal shorter sigset was rejected — now `syscall::sigset::check_max` and only `sigsetsize` bytes are copied out; (b) reported ONLY the thread-private `sigpending`, so a worker thread never saw a process-directed signal — now `sigpend::all_pending` unions the thread-private set with the process-directed one (posted on the group leader, reached O(1) via `ThreadGroup::leader_task`); (c) result was not intersected with `blocked` — Linux `sigandsets(set, &current->blocked, set)`; now it is. EFAULT probes only the requested length; `sigsetsize == 0` never faults |
| 128 | 64 | `rt_sigtimedwait` | `sys_rt_sigtimedwait` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:97` | `IMPL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `kernel/signal.c:3755 do_sigtimedwait, kernel/signal.c:3814 SYSCALL_DEFINE4(rt_sigtimedwait), kernel/signal.c:collect_signal` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `do_sigtimedwait` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:3755`. FIXED: (a) SIGKILL/SIGSTOP were waitable — Linux `sigdelsetmask` strips them, else a waiter swallows a fatal signal and becomes unkillable; (b) a zero timeout returned EINTR when an unrelated signal was pending — Linux `if (!sig && timeout)` never sleeps, so the answer is EAGAIN; (c) `uinfo` was probed at entry, turning a timeout into EFAULT — Linux copies siginfo only after a successful dequeue; (d) dequeued only the thread-private set, so `sigwaitinfo` in a worker thread for a process-directed signal hung forever — now `sigpend::dequeue_signal` claims from thread-private then process-directed, atomically (one consumer wins, never a duplicate delivery); (e) a bitmap-only signal reported si_code 0 with no explicit fallback — now Linux `collect_signal`s SI_USER; (f) si_value was written but standard signals had no queue to hold it. Timespec validation + `ktime_set` clamp already correct via `syscall::time::timespec_to_ns`. The pre-existing park-before-recheck ordering (no lost wakeup) is preserved and re-verified. REMAINING: the "a signal OUTSIDE the set interrupts me" EINTR check reads only the thread-private deliverable set, so a process-directed out-of-set signal does not interrupt a non-leader waiter. The success path (waiting for and consuming a signal IN the set) does cover both sets. Closing this needs a real `signal_struct::shared_pending` that the DELIVERY path (`take_lowest_pending`) also dequeues from — a whole-signal-subsystem change, not a slot-128 one Closed in `B1584`, which deleted the parallel fault-signal path in `mm-pmm/user_as/signal.rs` (it hand-wrote a siginfo onto the user stack and rewrote the live fault frame to jump at the handler, with a ZEROED ucontext stub so a returning handler resumed on garbage registers, `si_code` always SEGV_MAPERR, and `force_sig_info`'s unblock/reset-to-SIG_DFL ladder never running). Fault classification now lives in ungated `hal::fault_class` for BOTH arches and delivers through the single `rt_sigframe` builder; ~40 raw pending-bit sites across syscalls, ptrace, seccomp, tty, net, mq, OOM, cgroup and pid-ns now route through the one enqueue (`sched::live::send`), with ungated `sched::sigsend` owning the `prepare_signal`/`sig_ignored`/`legacy_queue` decisions. Two dead aarch64 vectors (undefined-instruction, software-step) that never called `oxide_irq_exit_to_user` are fixed. Knowingly unrouted and documented in place: timer-IRQ expiry (the enqueue allocates; Linux runs it from an hrtimer callback in process context) and SIGCHLD (per-thread `child_sigq` has no shared-set slot). Harness: `cargo test -p sched --lib` 720/0 (700 before), `-p hal` 71, `-p fs --lib signalfd` 23, aggregate 1906/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS x2 and smoke-arm PASS. `rt_sigtimedwait` kept its own siginfo offset table with no `_sigfault` arm, so a fault record copied out through the `_kill` arm; it now renders through `hal::write_siginfo`, the same writer the frame builder uses. The prior remaining-note about out-of-set EINTR reading only thread-private pending was stale — `deliverable_signals` reads private OR shared. |
| 129 | 64 | `rt_sigqueueinfo` | `sys_rt_sigqueueinfo` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:98` | `IMPL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `kernel/signal.c:4202 do_rt_sigqueueinfo, kernel/signal.c:4221 SYSCALL_DEFINE3(rt_sigqueueinfo), kernel/signal.c:3543 __copy_siginfo_from_user, kernel/signal.c:4245 do_rt_tgsigqueueinfo` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `do_rt_sigqueueinfo` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:4202`. FIXED SECURITY: the si_code forgery guard ran only for signals 33..64 and only AFTER the ESRCH/EPERM checks, and compared internal tids; signals 1..32 were routed to `sys_kill` with the siginfo DISCARDED, so `rt_sigqueueinfo(other_pid, SIGUSR1, {si_code=SI_KERNEL})` returned 0 where Linux returns EPERM. Now `signum::sigqueueinfo_forgery_rejected` implements `(si_code >= 0 OR si_code == SI_TKILL) && task_pid_vnr(current) != pid` verbatim, against the pid ARGUMENT, before any other check, for every signal number. ALSO FIXED: siginfo copy-in was 32 bytes (Linux copies `sizeof(kernel_siginfo)` = 48); si_signo is now forced to `sig`; EINVAL/ESRCH/EPERM now follow `kill_proc_info` order; standard signals keep their queued siginfo — `Task::sigqueue` covers all 64 signals with Linux `legacy_queue` depth (1 record for standard, `RT_QUEUE_CAP` for RT), so `sigqueue(3)` on SIGUSR1 delivers its `sival_ptr` and glibc SIGCANCEL/SIGSETXID handlers see si_code. si_value is now written as a full 8-byte `_rt` `sigval_t` instead of a truncated 4-byte si_status. 297 `rt_tgsigqueueinfo` fixed in the same shape (EINVAL for non-positive tgid/tid; ESRCH unless tid is in tgid) |
| 130 | common | `rt_sigsuspend` | `sys_rt_sigsuspend` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:96` | `IMPL` | `F729-signal-syscall-audit` | `kernel/signal.c:4843 sigsuspend, kernel/signal.c:4862 SYSCALL_DEFINE2(rt_sigsuspend), kernel/signal.c:signal_delivered, include/linux/sched/signal.h:sigmask_to_save` | `sched::sigaltstack` (14) + `sched::tests::signals` (24) + `syscall::sigset` (4) + `hal::write_siginfo` (3); x86_64 + aarch64 kernel builds; `make smoke-x86` + `make smoke-arm` both reach `basic.target` | Full `sigsuspend` audit against `linux-master/kernel/signal.c:4843`. FIXED: the old mask was RESTORED before returning EINTR, so a handler dispatched on the way out ran under the CALLER's mask instead of the suspend mask — the exact race `sigsuspend(2)` exists to close. Now Linux `saved_sigmask` + `TIF_RESTORE_SIGMASK`: `Task::arm_saved_sigmask` installs the temporary mask and arms the restore; `Task::sigmask_to_save` (Linux `sigmask_to_save`) hands the signal frame the PRE-suspend mask so `rt_sigreturn` lands on it, while the handler runs under the suspend mask OR `sa_mask` OR the signal; `Task::restore_saved_sigmask` (Linux `restore_saved_sigmask`) puts it back at the syscall-return tail when no handler runs. The flag is one-shot, so exactly one of the two paths consumes it. ALSO FIXED: the wait was a `sti; pause; cli` + `tick_yield` busy-spin burning a CPU for the whole suspension — now a real `WaitList` park with publish-before-recheck (Linux `TASK_INTERRUPTIBLE; schedule()`). SIGKILL/SIGSTOP can never be blocked by the suspend mask. Always returns EINTR. NOTE: the wake condition is thread-private `deliverable_signals_self()`, matching what the delivery path can actually deliver; see the remaining-gap note below. REMAINING: the wake condition is thread-private `deliverable_signals_self()`, so a non-leader thread suspended for a PROCESS-directed signal does not wake. Deliberate, not an oversight: this kernel posts process-directed signals on the group leader and `take_lowest_pending` dequeues only the thread-private set, so waking on the shared set would return EINTR with no handler to run and spin the caller's `while (!flag) sigsuspend()` loop forever. Closing it needs the delivery path to dequeue process-directed signals too — a whole-signal-subsystem change, not a slot-130 one Closed in `B1584`, which deleted the parallel fault-signal path in `mm-pmm/user_as/signal.rs` (it hand-wrote a siginfo onto the user stack and rewrote the live fault frame to jump at the handler, with a ZEROED ucontext stub so a returning handler resumed on garbage registers, `si_code` always SEGV_MAPERR, and `force_sig_info`'s unblock/reset-to-SIG_DFL ladder never running). Fault classification now lives in ungated `hal::fault_class` for BOTH arches and delivers through the single `rt_sigframe` builder; ~40 raw pending-bit sites across syscalls, ptrace, seccomp, tty, net, mq, OOM, cgroup and pid-ns now route through the one enqueue (`sched::live::send`), with ungated `sched::sigsend` owning the `prepare_signal`/`sig_ignored`/`legacy_queue` decisions. Two dead aarch64 vectors (undefined-instruction, software-step) that never called `oxide_irq_exit_to_user` are fixed. Knowingly unrouted and documented in place: timer-IRQ expiry (the enqueue allocates; Linux runs it from an hrtimer callback in process context) and SIGCHLD (per-thread `child_sigq` has no shared-set slot). Harness: `cargo test -p sched --lib` 720/0 (700 before), `-p hal` 71, `-p fs --lib signalfd` 23, aggregate 1906/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS x2 and smoke-arm PASS. The prior remaining-note for `rt_sigsuspend` was stale for the same reason; the wake condition is now pinned by test (sees process-directed signals, excludes default-ignored, never hides SIGKILL). |

The old implementation recorded the stack and NOTHING ELSE — no EPERM, no EINVAL, no ENOMEM, no SS_DISABLE handling, `oss` written BEFORE `ss` was validated, and the alternate stack was never actually USED by signal delivery: a recorded-but-ignored sigaltstack that breaks every SIGSEGV crash handler. FIXED, all of it, via the new hosted-tested `sched::sigaltstack` policy module: EPERM when changing the stack while executing on it, EINVAL for an `ss_flags` mode outside {0, SS_ONSTACK, SS_DISABLE}, ENOMEM for `ss_size < MINSIGSTKSZ` (2048 x86_64 / 5120 aarch64), SS_DISABLE zeroing sp/size while keeping the flag bits, the Linux unchanged-request short circuit before the size check, `oss.ss_flags` computed by `sas_ss_flags()` (live mode + stored SS_AUTODISARM) rather than the stored word, and Linux's exact ordering (read `ss` -> validate/apply -> write `oss` last). DELIVERY now honours it: `fs::sig_dispatch::setup_altstack` applies Linux `sigsp()` and both `build_signal_frame`s place the rt_sigframe at the alt-stack top under SA_ONSTACK; `uc_stack` is populated (Linux `save_altstack_ex`) and `rt_sigreturn` re-arms it (Linux `restore_altstack`), so SS_AUTODISARM disarms for the handler and comes back after. Scope note: the SA_ONSTACK delivery path is verified by the hosted `sigsp`/`sas_ss_flags`/`apply` decision table plus both-arch build and boot to `basic.target`; it is NOT verified by an end-to-end guest program that faults onto its alternate stack B1459: the standing gap that `ss_sp` was never range-checked is closed at the point Linux closes it — `get_sigframe`'s `access_ok`, not `sigaltstack(2)` entry (Linux does not check there either). An `SA_ONSTACK` delivery now derives the frame from the alt-stack top through `sigframe_range()` and `fs::sig_dispatch` runs `validate_user_buf` on the result before writing, so `sigaltstack({ss_sp=<kernel VA>})` + `SA_ONSTACK` force-SIGSEGVs instead of giving an arbitrary kernel write. |

| 144 | common | `sched_setscheduler` | `sys_sched_setscheduler` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:60` | `IMPL` | `F731-sched-policy-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sched/syscalls.c, kernel/sched/core.c, kernel/sched/rt.c, kernel/sched/fair.c, kernel/sched/sched.h, include/uapi/linux/sched.h` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | F731 rewrote the slot as a thin shim over `crate::sched_policy` (Linux `__sched_setscheduler`). Fixed: negative pid/policy wrapped instead of EINVAL; NULL param gave EFAULT not EINVAL; the RT `1..=99` range check ran AFTER the permission check so EPERM masked EINVAL; SCHED_RESET_ON_FORK was masked off and DISCARDED (now stored in `task_struct::policy`-adjacent state, ORed back by 145, and enforced at fork); SCHED_BATCH/SCHED_IDLE collapsed onto the CFS class so `sched_getscheduler` could not round-trip them (added Linux's separate `p->policy`); SCHED_DEADLINE returned EOPNOTSUPP where mainline returns EINVAL for a `sched_param`-only call (no `__checkparam_dl` data). PARTIAL because the SCHEDULER, not the syscall, is incomplete: SCHED_RR is stored and picked as RT but has NO round-robin quantum (no tick requeue anywhere in `crates/kernel/sched`), so it runs identically to SCHED_FIFO; SCHED_BATCH is stored but scheduled identically to SCHED_NORMAL; SCHED_IDLE is honoured only as CFS `WEIGHT_IDLEPRIO`=3, with no wakeup-preemption special case; SCHED_DEADLINE has no class at all and is refused. Genuinely honoured: NORMAL (CFS weight), FIFO (RT priority buckets, preempts CFS), IDLE (weight only). Harness: `setscheduler_round_trips_every_implemented_policy`, `invalid_priority_is_einval_even_when_the_caller_would_be_denied`, `reset_on_fork_bit_is_recorded_and_reported`, `deadline_is_rejected_never_silently_run_as_normal`. B1587: policies were STORED BUT NOT HONORED — wakeup preemption was unconditional, breaking the SCHED_FIFO guarantee and making BATCH and IDLE identical to NORMAL; the RR quantum enforced 10x the reported value; `rt_time_slice` was never reloaded on policy entry; and the resched IPI ran the periodic tick. Also `put_prev_task` pushed to the tail unconditionally, so every involuntary preemption demoted a FIFO task behind its equal-priority peers — FIFO was RR with a scheduler-chosen quantum. Requeue position is now the caller's choice (wakeup -> tail, preempted -> head, gave-up-turn -> tail), where the gave-up-turn bit has exactly two producers (a spent RR quantum and `sched_yield`) and FIFO never sets it. REMAINING: no SCHED_DEADLINE scheduling class — validated then refused with EOPNOTSUPP; a real EDF/CBS class needs its own lane. Harness: ungated `sched_enc/{wakeup,requeue}.rs` and `syscalls::priority_target`; `cargo test -p sched --lib` 789/0 (697 before), `-p syscalls --lib` 873/0, `-p block --lib` 47/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 86s. Closed in `F774`, which BUILT the class: `sched_setattr` had validated a deadline request in full and then answered EOPNOTSUPP — there was no EDF ready set, no budget, no admission control, no throttle, no replenishment. Now `sched/src/deadline/` (params/cbs/bw/entity/clock/replenish/live) plus the EDF set in `sched/src/dl.rs`. CBS charges budget from a per-stint monotonic stamp at schedule-out AND tick (so a task blocking between ticks is still accounted); `runtime <= 0` throttles; overrun is kept as signed debt and replenish trades one period per budget, so an N-budget overrun costs N postponed deadlines; wakeup takes original-CBS restart or the revised laxity-scaled rule. Admission is a fixed-point ledger comparing undivided `total_bw` against a per-CPU cap scaled by the LIVE online-CPU capacity — one source of truth — returning EBUSY after every argument and permission answer, with a re-issue or shrink judged against the bandwidth already held so it can never spuriously EBUSY. Deadline outranks RT outranks fair, compared wrap-safe and STRICT so an equal deadline neither preempts nor reorders. A throttled entity leaves every tree at one chokepoint and parks on a replenishment queue folded into the one-shot timer, requeued through the DEFERRED wake path because the timer IRQ must not spin on the rq lock. A DL parent's fork is EAGAIN, with `SCHED_RESET_ON_FORK` the supported route. A `sched_param`-shaped deadline request stays EINVAL (it carries no runtime/deadline/period), which is Linux's answer and is now asserted rather than a side effect of refusal. Harness: `cargo test -p sched --lib` 1021/0 (was 789; +90 in `deadline::*`), `-p syscalls --lib` 986/0, `-p procfs --lib` 124/0; the throttle is PROVEN not asserted — `a_task_that_overruns_its_budget_is_thrown_off_the_ready_set` runs a real task on a real `RunqueueInner` against a settable clock and shows the CPU idling rather than overrunning. smoke-x86 PASS 282s, smoke-arm PASS 378s; stack-gate PASS both arches. |

| 153 | common | `vhangup` | `sys_vhangup` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/153_vhangup.rs; dispatch/route_c.rs:32` | `IMPL` | `F764-admin-tty-compat-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | F764: REWROTE. Was `sigpending \|= SIGHUP` on every task sharing the caller's sid, with no tty touched at all — a caller with no controlling terminal SIGHUP'd its whole session, and a process ignoring SIGHUP kept a live handle on the next login's terminal. Now `fs/open.c:1530-1537`: CAP_SYS_TTY_CONFIG -> EPERM; no ctty -> silent 0 (`tty_vhangup_self`, tty_io.c:701-708); else `__tty_hangup(tty, 0)` — revoke the line (reads EOF / writes EIO), clear `tty->ctrl.session`+`pgrp`, and per `tty_signal_session_leader` (tty_jobctrl.c:196-238) clear ctty on every session member holding THIS tty while SIGHUP+SIGCONT go to session LEADERS only (`exit_session=0` so no `kill_pgrp` on the fg group). Added `clear_bit(TTY_HUPPED)` on tty_open (tty_io.c:2161) — without it one vhangup wedged the singleton console forever. Fixed TIOCNOTTY in the same pass (`no_tty` = `disassociate_ctty(0)` + `proc_clear_tty`, tty_jobctrl.c:578-582): it never cleared `task.ctty`, so agetty's TIOCNOTTY->close->vhangup sequence hung up a console Linux leaves alone. Rules in `tty::hangup::{decide,live}` + `TtyStruct::hangup(HangupKind)`; 10 hosted tests (tty::hangup::*, core::tests::hangup::*), 5 fail without the fix. |
| 154 | common | `modify_ldt` | `sys_modify_ldt` | enosys-contract | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/unconfigured.rs:47; dispatch/route_c.rs` | `LINUX-ENOSYS` | `F757-compat-privileged` | `arch/x86/kernel/ldt.c, arch/x86/kernel/Makefile:73, kernel/sys_ni.c:291` | `enosys-abi-harness` | F757: was blanket EPERM in compat.rs, which told a root caller it lacked a privilege that could never help. Linux builds `ldt.o` only under CONFIG_MODIFY_LDT_SYSCALL and otherwise resolves the slot through `COND_SYSCALL(modify_ldt)` to `sys_ni_syscall` = -ENOSYS. Oxide has no per-mm LDT, no GDT LDT descriptor and no `lldt` on address-space switch, and aarch64 has no such slot in the generic ABI, so ENOSYS is the honest answer on both arches. Membership pinned by `unconfigured::tests::set_matches_the_pinned_linux_slot_numbers`. |
| 155 | common | `pivot_root` | `sys_pivot_root` | vfs | dcache, fs-context, mntns, permissions, procfs, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:29; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/155_pivot_root.rs; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/pivot_root_policy.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/src/mount/pivot_check.rs; crates/kernel/vfs/src/mount/namespace.rs` | `IMPL` | `B1650` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/namespace.c, fs/fs_struct.c, fs/internal.h` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | F739 rebuilt slot 155 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master fs/namespace.c `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(pivot_root)` + `path_pivot_root()`. FIXED, all four previously-collapsed behaviours: (1) both lookups now report their real errno -- `Err(_) => EINVAL` swallowed ENOENT/ENOTDIR/ELOOP/EACCES/ENAMETOOLONG, so a caller naming a missing directory was told its arguments were malformed; (2) `LOOKUP_DIRECTORY` is enforced, so a non-directory argument is ENOTDIR; (3) CAP_SYS_ADMIN moved AFTER both lookups, matching Linux's split between the syscall wrapper and `path_pivot_root`, so a bad path no longer reports EPERM; and it is now `may_mount()` proper -- `ns_capable(mnt_ns->user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` via `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for` rather than a flat effective-set test; (4) the previously-absent rungs are implemented -- `check_mnt(root_mnt)`/`check_mnt(new_mnt)` EINVAL, `d_unlinked(new->dentry)` ENOENT, `new_mnt == root_mnt` or `old_mnt == root_mnt` EBUSY, `path_mounted(&root)` EINVAL (a task chrooted into a plain directory), `path_mounted(new)` EINVAL -- in Linux's exact order, extracted into `vfs::mount::pivot_check` so the order is a hosted unit test. The caller's root now comes from its own `fs_struct` (`get_fs_root`), not an assumed namespace root. The dead `NR_PIVOT_ROOT` blanket-EPERM arm in `sched/src/compat.rs` (shadowed by route_c since F110) is removed. Two Linux lines are structurally inapplicable and documented as such rather than skipped silently: `!mnt_has_parent(new_mnt)` is implied by the EBUSY rung in a tree whose only self-parented mount is the namespace root, and `!mnt_has_parent(root_mnt)` guards Linux's `attach_mnt` into the old root's slot under `rootfs`, which this tree does not have. PARTIAL for one disclosed divergence: when the caller has chrooted into a DIFFERENT mount, Linux relocates that mount and leaves the namespace root alone, whereas `commit_retree` re-roots the whole namespace; the common container/systemd path (`chdir(new); pivot_root(".", ".")`, no chroot) is unaffected. Harness: `cargo test -p vfs` 1281 (11 new `mount::pivot_check` order tests + the pre-existing `mount_pivot_validation`, `mount_propagation_pivot`, `mount_root_truth` suites still green, including the systemd-shaped `full_service_setup_pivot_and_switch_root_detach`); `cargo test -p syscalls` 357 in the lib target (7 new `pivot_root_policy` sequence tests). Both arches build. `B1634` implements the real attachment swap on the caller's own root, plus four gaps the prior note omitted: `MNT_LOCKED` was never transferred to the root slot, the new root stayed on its expire list, a covered `put_old` wrongly returned EBUSY, and BOTH reachability rungs were hardcoded `true` behind a string heuristic. REMAINING: in the namespace-root re-root branch a covered `put_old` attaches on a descended dentry rather than the covering mount's root. Harness: 4622 passed / 0 failed across vfs (1630, was 1100), ext4 (559, was 500), syscalls (1287), fs (978), user-namespace (34), procfs (134); workspace check clean; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. B1650: covered-put_old residual real (fixed, causality-proven by reverting); the chroot-relocate residual was STALE (already done). The discipline actually STEERS the clock: `NtpState::advance()` runs `second_overflow` for each elapsed wall second (leap-second state machine, dispersion growth, phase draining, MAX_TICKADJ-capped adjtime slew) and integrates `tick_length` against elapsed MONOTONIC time -- not tick count, because this kernel programs a one-shot timer and its ticks are not evenly spaced -- applying the result through `ClockState::slew`, which deliberately does not bump the step generation because a slew must not invalidate absolute deadlines. Wired at the kmain tick hook ahead of the vvar publish; an undisciplined system costs one relaxed atomic load. Return value is the `TIME_*` state (TIME_OK/INS/DEL/OOP/WAIT/ERROR) including the leap-second rewrite of `time` and `tai`, matching the contract `crates/user/glibc/src/time/adjtime.rs` already documented. Copy-back is unconditional, as in time.c. Harness: `cargo test -p timekeeper` 46 (was 3) covering the validation ladder, every mode, leap seconds, and the slew rate; `cargo test -p syscalls` timex_policy 16 + timex_abi 6. Both arches build. |

FIXED, three divergences: (1) the capability test ran FIRST, before the path was looked up, so an unprivileged caller naming a missing or non-directory path was told EPERM where Linux says ENOENT/ENOTDIR -- the wrong errno for every `RootDirectory=` failure; the lookup's own errno is now reported verbatim. (2) The `MAY_EXEC` search-permission test on the new root was skipped entirely, so a caller could chroot into a directory it had no right to traverse; a non-searchable target is now EACCES, decided before the capability test exactly as Linux orders it. Harness: the ladder is one work-fn, so its order is a single readable sequence rather than three call sites; `cargo test -p fs` and `cargo test -p syscalls` green. Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |

REMAINING unchanged: no global superblock list, no `sync_bdevs` pass, `sync_filesystem` still calls `sync_fs` twice, and only fsync/sync feed `s_wb_err`. Harness: `vfs --test lazytime_writeback` 13 (each forcing point READS THE TIMESTAMP BACK from a store reachable only via `i_op->update_time`/`s_op->write_inode`, not flag assertions) + `writeback_policy` 13, revert-proved both ways; `-p vfs -p fs -p ext4 -p syscalls` 4239/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 60s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1970 closes all four remainders. `sync(2)` walked the MOUNT table, so a live-but-unmounted superblock (lazy detach with open fds) was unreachable; it now walks the superblock registry through a new iteration primitive that snapshots under the lock, releases it before calling, holds a reference across the call and skips unpublished and dying instances (5 hosted tests; positive controls restoring the mount-table walk and a skip-nothing predicate each RED 3/5). And a metadata writeback failure at inode eviction was discarded outright while the background pass latched only the filesystem half — so a later `fsync` on the failing inode returned SUCCESS; both now flow through one choke point that latches inode and filesystem together (6 tests; two positive controls RED 2/6 and 5/6). Closed in `B1634`: `Constructor::{Basic,Flags}` was a SPLIT SOURCE OF TRUTH — only ext4 took the flag word, so ~15 filesystem types produced `s_flags == 0` and every `SB_RDONLY`/`SB_NOATIME` consumer read them as unrestricted. Two further defects found: `MS_REMOUNT` had no mount-root rung, so `mount -o remount,ro /var/log` silently remounted the CONTAINING mount; and `MS_BIND` ignored unbindable mounts. Harness: 4622 passed / 0 failed across vfs (1630, was 1100), ext4 (559, was 500), syscalls (1287), fs (978), user-namespace (34), procfs (134); workspace check clean; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. |

Propagation flows across peer groups and slave chains, which the detach choke point walks, and those chains are not namespace-scoped — a mirror living in another mount namespace is found and detached. The row's own preceding sentence (B1650 adding the propagation half) already contradicted the gap line. The phrasing was also imprecise: the reference propagates across the propagation tree, not across namespaces as such. |
