Linux implements both as one routine over two `new_utsname` fields, so they now share one work-fn (`hostname::write_uts_name`) whose decision order is `uts_policy::check_uts_set`. FIXED, four divergences: (1) ORDER -- the length window was tested BEFORE the capability, so an unprivileged caller passing a bogus length got EINVAL where Linux gives EPERM, and learned whether its length was valid; `ns_capable` is first now, as in the source. (2) The capability was a flat effective-set test; it is now `ns_capable(current->nsproxy->uts_ns->user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` through `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for`, scoped to the user namespace that OWNS the UTS namespace being written. (3) `len` is declared `int`: only the low 32 bits are significant and a negative value is EINVAL. The raw 64-bit register was used, so `len = 0x1_0000_0020` -- a valid 32 -- was rejected; it truncates first now. (4) The syscall path ran the stored bytes through the `/proc/sys/kernel/hostname` newline trimmer, so `sethostname(<foo-newline>, 4)` silently stored `foo`. Linux's `memcpy(u->nodename, tmp, len)` filters nothing; the store is verbatim and the trim moved to the procfs write hook, where `proc_dostring` is what does it upstream. Readers were audited alongside the writers, per the PR#4013 regression shape: `uname(2)` substituted `(none)` for an EMPTY nodename while `/proc/sys/kernel/hostname` reported the empty string -- two answers for one piece of state -- and reports verbatim now, with the `(none)` default living in the init-namespace storage seed exactly as Linux keeps it in `init_uts_ns`. The absent-owner fallback on both the read and the write side is the INIT namespace's value (the global static), never an empty string. `__NEW_UTS_LEN` is one constant shared by the window test and the copy buffer. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls` (4 new `uts_policy` tests -- EPERM precedes EINVAL for every length, negative length, the 0..=64 window, and the 32-bit truncation). Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |

Completion remains open for audited crash-consistent qtree/journal updates, reservation and privileged-limit semantics, namespace-aware IDs, warning delivery, mount options, and active x86/ARM syscall evidence. STATUS CORRECTED IN-PROGRESS->PARTIAL (D394): the branch named in the Branch column is merged and deleted, so the work landed long ago. IN-PROGRESS means 'local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall' -- leaving it set on merged work tells the next agent a live lane owns this row, which is exactly how duplicate lanes get opened. PARTIAL is the honest reading: a committed fix exists, full Linux parity is not proven. `B1634`: enforcement existed and was wired, but RESERVED SPACE WAS CHECKED AGAINST A SEPARATE, UNLIMITED LIMIT — a reservation bypassed the block hard limit entirely. Now charged against the combined total, with the privileged bypass, prealloc/nofail arms and grace clearing corrected. Namespace-aware ids are real, which required adding the project id map to the user-namespace engine (`/proc/<pid>/projid_map` was a hardcoded identity FAKE) and `s_user_ns` on the superblock. ext4 was DISCARDING ITS ENTIRE MOUNT DATA STRING, so `usrquota`/`grpquota`/`prjquota`/`jqfmt=` were silently swallowed; now parsed, validated and honoured on mount and remount. Two hooks existed but were never installed (the privileged hard-limit bypass, and the whole warning surface). REMAINING: warnings are generated and exported but nothing transports them — the genetlink family registry has zero families and there is no generic-netlink multicast send, which is a netlink row. Harness: 4622 passed / 0 failed across vfs (1630, was 1100), ext4 (559, was 500), syscalls (1287), fs (978), user-namespace (34), procfs (134); workspace check clean; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. `B1636` closes the transport remainder: generic netlink was a scaffold with zero registered families, no multicast path, and a `handle()` that answered any non-nlctrl family id with a SUCCESS ACK — a client addressing a family that does not exist was told it worked. Built the family registry (two id spaces; multicast-group ids are a flat space shared by every family, since a socket subscribes to a group number not a family), `nlctrl` as a genuinely registered family so `genl_ctrl_resolve` works, the admission ordering (unregistered family -> ENOENT, wrong namespace -> ENOENT, strict-validation EINVAL, missing op -> EOPNOTSUPP, then the EPERM ladder), and `genlmsg_multicast_netns`/`_allns` with namespace scoping, sender-port exclusion and ESRCH when nobody listens. `VFS_DQUOT` now registers and emits `QUOTA_NL_C_WARNING` with the full attribute set, both 64-bit ids carrying `QUOTA_NL_A_PAD` so their payload lands 8-byte aligned, and ESRCH swallowed so a missing `quota_nld` never fails a filesystem write. Proven end to end by a test that drives `vfs::deliver_warning` and decodes what arrives on the group. Fixed with it, affecting EVERY netlink protocol: the socket group mask was a bare `AtomicU32`, silently discarding any group above 32 and silently ignoring group 0 — now a growable bitmap with per-protocol `ngroups`, EINVAL outside the range on ADD/DROP_MEMBERSHIP, and `NETLINK_LIST_MEMBERSHIPS` returning every word rather than only the first. Harness: `cargo test -p netlink` 213/0 (was 168, 45 new ungated), `-p netlink -p syscalls -p vfs` 3138/0; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 67s, smoke-arm PASS 80s. B1970 re-verified the whole opening remainder list and FIVE of its six items are stale, already closed by the lanes narrated later in this same row: reservation and privileged-limit semantics, namespace-aware ids, warning delivery, and mount options are all present with tests. REMAINING, and the only item: no test drives a crash or journal replay against a quota-block write specifically, so audited crash consistency cannot be claimed. The writes themselves go through the ordinary journalled metadata path. |

Returns the THREAD id in the caller's pid namespace (`vtid`, falling back to the internal tid in the initial namespace where they coincide), never the tgid and never a global id; cannot fail, takes no arguments, touches no user memory. arm64 nr 178 -> slot 186 round-trip covered by `syscall::arm_abi` tests. |
| 187 | common | `readahead` | `sys_readahead` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:98` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/187_readahead.rs` -> `crates/kernel/fs/src/readahead.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, mm/readahead.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(readahead), mm/readahead.c:ksys_readahead, mm/fadvise.c:vfs_fadvise, mm/fadvise.c:generic_fadvise, mm/internal.h:force_page_cache_readahead` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. The slot fell through to the compat table shared `sys_fadvise_validate`, which checked only "is this fd open" and returned 0 - accept-and-ignore, and it answered success for a write-only fd, for a pipe, and for a directory. FIXED, in Linux order (mm/readahead.c:730-751): `EBADF` for a closed fd, then `EBADF` for a description without `FMODE_READ`, then `EINVAL` for a description with no address space, then `EINVAL` for an inode that is neither `S_ISREG` nor `S_ISBLK`. A FIFO is `EINVAL` and NOT `ESPIPE`: `generic_fadvise` does have an `ESPIPE` arm (mm/fadvise.c:42-43) but the `S_ISREG` or `S_ISBLK` filter runs first, so `readahead(2)` can never reach it - `fadvise64(2)`, which has no such filter, CAN. A `count` above `LLONG_MAX` arrives as a negative `loff_t` and is `EINVAL`, as is a negative offset. The whole ladder is capability-free and LSM-free, so `EPERM` is never a valid answer - a dedicated test asserts that across the full input cross-product, because `EPERM` from the compat table would be a lie about the reason rather than a missing feature. On success the return is unconditionally 0 (`force_page_cache_readahead` returns void, so a readahead I/O failure is never reported) and the cache fill is real: every not-yet-resident page of the requested window below EOF is pulled through the inode address space and the per-description readahead window is advanced. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --lib readahead` (4 tests: admission order, FIFO-is-EINVAL-not-ESPIPE, the EPERM-is-never-an-answer cross-product, and the offset/count sign rules) + `cargo test -p fs --test file_io_range_model::readahead_ladder_over_real_inodes` (the ladder over real tmpfs inodes, a real pipe and a real directory); both arch kernel builds green. |
| 188 | common | `setxattr` | `sys_setxattr` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/188_setxattr.rs` -> `xattr_common::sys_setxattr_path` -> `fs::xattr::{import_set,set_on}` | `IMPL` | `F733-xattr-audit` | `fs/xattr.c, include/uapi/linux/xattr.h, include/uapi/linux/limits.h, security/commoncap.c, fs/posix_acl.c, fs/ext4/xattr.c` | `fs-xattr-policy-model` | Plain form: the final symlink IS followed (LookupFlags follow). setxattr_copy order now matches Linux exactly: unknown flag bits EINVAL, then name ERANGE/EFAULT, then size>XATTR_SIZE_MAX E2BIG BEFORE the value is copied (was: copy-then-check, a 4 GiB kalloc), and the whole import runs BEFORE path resolution so a bad name outranks ENOENT. XATTR_CREATE=EEXIST / XATTR_REPLACE=ENODATA atomically in the store; CREATE\|REPLACE together is no longer a wrong EINVAL. security.capability takes the v2/v3 header check (EINVAL) then CAP_SETFCAP (EPERM); other security.* take CAP_SYS_ADMIN. system.posix_acl_{access,default} route through the ACL path (blob validation, owner check, default-on-dir-only, posix_acl_update_mode i_mode rewrite + trivial-ACL drop). Success fires fsnotify_xattr (IN_ATTRIB). xattr_permission rebuilt from source: may_write_xattr (S_IMMUTABLE/S_APPEND EPERM), security.*/system.* left to the LSM+handler, trusted.* CAP_SYS_ADMIN (read denial = ENODATA), user.* limited to REG/DIR/SOCK with the sticky-dir owner rule and then real inode_permission DAC; xattr_resolve_name gives EOPNOTSUPP for unregistered namespaces and EINVAL for a bare prefix. Backends: tmpfs SimpleXattrs in memory, ext4 written through to the on-disk ibody/external block (`crates/kernel/ext4/tests/xattr_image.rs::syscall_layer_policy_writes_reach_disk_on_ext4` sets via the policy layer, remounts, reads back from disk). Harness: 15 cases in `crates/kernel/fs/src/xattr/tests.rs` + 2 in `crates/kernel/fs/tests/xattr_syscall_model.rs` (real tmpfs tree) + 4 in `crates/kernel/ext4/tests/xattr_image.rs` (real ext4 image), all green. | 200 | common | `tkill` | `sys_tkill` | sched | fd-table, sched, signals, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/200_tkill.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `F741-thread-primitives-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/signal.c (SYSCALL_DEFINE2(tkill), do_tkill, do_send_specific, prepare_kill_siginfo, check_kill_permission), include/linux/cred.h` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | F741 gave slot 200 a real handler. It routed to `sys_kill` before, which is a DIFFERENT syscall: kill(2) is thread-group directed with si_code SI_USER and gives pid<=0 pgrp/broadcast meanings, so `tkill(0,sig)` fanned the signal at the caller's whole process group, `tkill(-1,sig)` broadcast it, and no SI_TKILL record was queued at all (glibc `__nptl_setxid_sighandler` and SIGCANCEL both reject a zeroed si_pid/si_code). Now matches `SYSCALL_DEFINE2(tkill)`: EINVAL for tid<=0, then `do_tkill(0, pid, sig)` shared with slot 234 in `tkill_common.rs` so the pair cannot drift. Lookup is `find_task_by_vpid`-equivalent in the CALLER's pid namespace; a missing tid is ESRCH even for a bogus signal, because Linux validates `sig` inside `check_kill_permission` AFTER the lookup. sig==0 is the existence/permission probe. si_code SI_TKILL, si_pid = sender `task_tgid_vnr`, si_uid = sender REAL uid (`current_uid()` is `cred->uid`). Hosted: 5 `tkill_common` admission tests; arm64 nr 130 -> slot 200 (and NOT slot 62) asserted in `syscall::arm_abi` tests. |
| 201 | common | `time` | `sys_time` | time | fd-table, sched-clock, syscall-abi, time, timers, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/201_time.rs` | `IMPL` | `F734-clock-timer-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/time/time.c` | `time-timerfd-model` | F734 verified against `SYSCALL_DEFINE1(time)`: returns `ktime_get_real_seconds()` as the return value AND writes it through `tloc` only when non-NULL, EFAULT on a bad `tloc`, no EINVAL path. Wall seconds come from the canonical `timekeeper` realtime, the same source `gettimeofday`/`clock_gettime(CLOCK_REALTIME)` read, so the three can never disagree. |
| 202 | common | `futex` | `sys_futex` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:67` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/futex/*.c` | `syscall-abi-model` | B1419 audit: WAIT/WAIT_BITSET/WAKE/WAKE_BITSET/REQUEUE/CMP_REQUEUE/WAKE_OP implemented (fixed: wake_op oparg/cmparg sign-extension, WAIT_BITSET/WAKE_BITSET bitset filtering + bitset==0 EINVAL, FUTEX_CLOCK_REALTIME cmd gate, signal/timeout wake misclassification). PI ops (LOCK_PI/UNLOCK_PI/TRYLOCK_PI/WAIT_REQUEUE_PI/CMP_REQUEUE_PI/LOCK_PI2) and obsolete FUTEX_FD honestly return -ENOSYS (was silent `0`) — no rt_mutex/priority-inheritance implementation exists. F743 fixes the interrupted return. `wait_loop` returned a bare `-EINTR`, which dropped BOTH restarts Linux performs. Linux `__futex_wait` ends an interrupted wait with `-ERESTARTSYS` (`kernel/futex/waitwake.c:738`), and `futex_wait()` then either returns it untouched when the call carried no timeout (`waitwake.c:753-754`) or arms `futex_wait_restart` with the ABSOLUTE deadline and returns `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK` (`waitwake.c:759-767`). The discriminator is the presence of a timeout, never whether it was absolute or relative: FUTEX_WAIT's relative timespec is already converted to an absolute CLOCK_MONOTONIC deadline at syscall entry (`kernel/futex/syscalls.c:184-185`), so both ops reach `futex_wait()` indistinguishable. That rule lives in the non-gated `ipc::futex_restart` so it is hosted-tested. The restart block carries uaddr, the full op word, val, bitset and the absolute deadline; slot 219 replays it through `dispatch_timed`, so a repeatedly interrupted wait runs out its REMAINING timeout. `futex_waitv`/futex2 take the plain `-ERESTARTSYS` arm with no block, matching `futex_wait_multiple` (`waitwake.c:595-596`) and `syscalls.c:426`, which call `__futex_wait` directly. Hosted: `ipc::futex_core_hosted` proves the untimed wait returns ERESTARTSYS and arms nothing, and that a timed wait arms RESTART_FUTEX with the unmodified absolute deadline. PI ops remain honestly -ENOSYS as before. Closed in `B1633`: the whole PI family (LOCK_PI/LOCK_PI2/UNLOCK_PI/TRYLOCK_PI/WAIT_REQUEUE_PI/CMP_REQUEUE_PI) and FUTEX_FD returned ENOSYS. Also fixed: `FUTEX_LOCK_PI`'s timeout is absolute CLOCK_REALTIME and was unhandled; only five commands read `utime` as a timespec while others reuse that register as an integer operand, so the rest were a wild user read; REQUEUE/CMP_REQUEUE/WAKE_OP counts were cast to unsigned so `-1` became unbounded; and WAKE_OP answered EINVAL for an unknown op and silently no-waked for an unknown comparison, where Linux gives ENOSYS for both. `B1633` also built REAL PRIORITY INHERITANCE, which existed nowhere in this kernel (`sched::pi_prio` + `live::pi_boost`): base class stored separately from effective, with `sched_setscheduler`, `sched_getparam` and `setpriority` routed through the base so a boost is neither clobbered nor leaked to userspace. Shared-futex keys now derive from `FileBacking::object_id()` — inode identity plus offset, Linux's key — instead of physical-page keying for file mappings. Harness: `cargo test -p ipc -p sched -p syscalls -p syscall` 2716/0 (46 new in `futex_pi_hosted`, 17 in the ungated rule modules, 11 `pi_prio`); outcome tests drive REAL OS threads through the production source — a waiter parks in `lock_pi` and returns owning the mutex, an owner DIES and the waiter returns owning it with `FUTEX_OWNER_DIED`, the robust walk wakes a real parked waiter, and an RT waiter boosts a fair owner with the boost returned at unlock; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 68s, smoke-arm PASS 80s. |
| 203 | common | `sched_setaffinity` | `sys_sched_setaffinity` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/203_sched_setaffinity.rs:24` | `IMPL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `kernel/sched/syscalls.c, include/linux/cpumask.h, kernel/sched/core.c, kernel/cgroup/cpuset.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Rewritten against `kernel/sched/syscalls.c` `sys_sched_setaffinity`/`sched_setaffinity`/`__sched_setaffinity`. `len` is BYTES with NO minimum on the SET side (Linux `get_user_cpu_mask` zero-fills then copies min(len, cpumask_size)); the old code rejected len<8 with EINVAL, breaking any narrower cpu_set_t. Errno order is Linux's: EFAULT (copy_from_user) -> ESRCH (find_task_by_vpid, resolved in the CALLER's pid namespace via registry::resolve_user_pid, a TID not a TGID) -> EINVAL (PF_NO_SETAFFINITY) -> EPERM (check_same_owner \|\| CAP_SYS_NICE) -> EINVAL (mask naming no active CPU). The EPERM check was entirely missing before, so any task could repin any other task including root's. The stored mask is the request narrowed by the cpuset only, never by cpu_active_mask, so an offline CPU stays in the mask; the raw request is kept in `user_cpus_allowed` (Linux `user_cpus_ptr`) and `cpuset_cpus_allowed` holds the cgroup restriction, so `cpuset.cpus` and sched_setaffinity compose (`sched::affinity::compose`) instead of overwriting each other. `cpu_active_mask` now reads the authoritative online BITMAP rather than a count that assumed dense CPU ids. PF_NO_SETAFFINITY is set on the per-CPU kthreads (ksoftirqd/N, kworker/N) that kthread_bind pins. ENFORCEMENT: the mask is consulted by select_task_rq (wakeup placement), balance_once/newidle_balance (both migration directions), and now by initial placement -- wake_up_new_task and the SIGCONT re-enqueue routed through the raw local runqueue, ignoring the mask; both now go through place_runnable/select_task_rq. The mask is also inherited across fork/clone (Linux dup_task_struct), which it was not: a CPUAffinity= unit or taskset shell lost the mask on its next fork. PARTIAL: a task that is CURRENTLY RUNNING on a now-disallowed CPU is only nudged with need_resched + a reschedule IPI rather than synchronously migrated (Linux's stop-machine migration handshake), so it can keep running there until it next schedules. Tests: syscalls::affinity_abi (14 hosted), sched::tests::affinity + sched::affinity (7 hosted). Closed in `B1587`: a RUNNING task was never evicted — `schedule()`'s `put_prev_task` re-queued it on the forbidden CPU, undoing the mask writer's IPI. Adds park-at-schedule / place-in-`finish_task_switch` eviction (task off-CPU first, no nested rq locks) and collapses two disagreeing `cpus_allowed` composition rules into one `compose` with user-request and cpuset-update arms. Splitting `relocate_for_affinity` for hosted testing exposed a second live bug: `enqueue_on_with` silently no-ops when the target CPU has no installed runqueue and `select_task_rq` falls back to `local`, so a task could end up runnable on NO runqueue — never scheduled again, no fault, no log. It now reports placement and is `#[must_use]`, with a fallback to the CPU the task came from. Harness: ungated `sched_enc/{wakeup,requeue}.rs` and `syscalls::priority_target`; `cargo test -p sched --lib` 789/0 (697 before), `-p syscalls --lib` 873/0, `-p block --lib` 47/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 86s. `F774` adds the SCHED_DEADLINE rule: narrowing a deadline task's affinity below its admitted span is EBUSY, from one predicate shared by `sched_setaffinity` and the cpuset writer (test `narrowing_a_deadline_tasks_affinity_is_ebusy`). |
| 204 | common | `sched_getaffinity` | `sys_sched_getaffinity` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/204_sched_getaffinity.rs:22` | `IMPL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `kernel/sched/syscalls.c, include/linux/cpumask.h, kernel/sched/core.c, kernel/cgroup/cpuset.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Rewritten against `kernel/sched/syscalls.c` `sys_sched_getaffinity`/`sched_getaffinity`. Entry checks in Linux order: `(len * BITS_PER_BYTE) < nr_cpu_ids` -> EINVAL, then `len & (sizeof(unsigned long)-1)` -> EINVAL; then the task lookup (ESRCH, resolved in the caller's pid namespace, pid 0 = the calling THREAD), then the copy-out (EFAULT) -- previously the buffer was validated before the lookup, so a bad pointer masked ESRCH. Returns the number of BYTES written, `min(len, cpumask_size())`, which is what glibc's wrapper uses to zero-fill `cpuset[ret..cpusetsize]` and what `__get_nprocs` loops on. Reported mask is `p->cpus_mask & cpu_active_mask`, read from the authoritative online bitmap. Tests: syscalls::affinity_abi (14 hosted) covers both EINVAL rules, the byte return value at len 8/16/128/1024, little-endian CPU bit order, and the active-mask narrowing. |
| 205 | 64 | `set_thread_area` | `(none)` | enosys-contract | syscall-abi, enosys-contract | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/misc.rs:80` | `LINUX-ENOSYS` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `enosys-abi-harness` | Linux table reserves the number with no native entry; ABI result must be ENOSYS. |
| 206 | 64 | `io_setup` | `sys_io_setup` | vfs | async-io, fd-table, mm-uaccess, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:79` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/aio.c, io_uring/` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | Ledger F3/B643: libaio context registry, submit, getevents, cancel, destroy, vectored offsets, and RESFD signaling are wired; remaining full-row audit covers async completion timing and every iocb opcode/error ordering. Closed in `B1574`: the previous implementation was a kernel-side `VecDeque` behind an opaque integer cookie, but in Linux `aio_context_t` IS the address the completion ring is mapped at — libaio dereferences it as `struct aio_ring *` and reaps without a syscall, so the old code was unusable by libaio and `io_cancel`/blocking `io_getevents`/`io_pgetevents` sigmask were self-declared no-ops. Now: a contiguous refcounted run seeded with magic/compat/incompat/header_length/nr and mapped MAP_SHARED, whose address is the context id; per-CPU floor -> doubling -> 256 MiB EINVAL cap -> EAGAIN -> page rounding with `fs.aio-max-nr` accounting; the full `io_submit_one` ladder (reserved2, signed nbytes, ring-slot EAGAIN, EBADF, `aio_resfd` must be a real eventfd, `KIOCB_KEY` stamp) over PREAD/PWRITE/PREADV/PWRITEV/FSYNC/FDSYNC/POLL with NOOP=EINVAL, IOPRIO and `RWF_*` admission AND effect, partial-submit counting; blocking `min_nr` reap with aio's own timeout rules and EINTR/ERESTARTNOHAND; `io_pgetevents` `__aio_sigset`; `io_cancel` EINPROGRESS/EINVAL which also still DELIVERS the cancelled poll's `io_event` with `res = 0`; `io_destroy` resolve+unmap+uncharge. `IOCB_CMD_POLL` completes from the poll WAKEUP (keyed `EpollNotify::notify_events`) and signals `aio_resfd` without any reap, with an eventfd-signals-itself guard. `VmaBacking::KernelFrame` now offsets by VMA offset. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib` 838/0 with 55 ungated `aio_abi` tests, including `const _: () = assert!(offset_of!(..) == ..)` layout proofs that are EVALUATED on both targets (verified non-vacuous: perturbing an offset fails the aarch64 build); the old `aio/tests.rs` was in a `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]` file and had never executed once. Both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 59s, smoke-arm PASS 94s; `userspace/aio_probe` PASS in-guest on x86_64 AND against a real Linux 6.19 host, asserting a POLL+eventfd completion with no intervening `io_getevents`. REMAINING: no in-guest aarch64 probe run — a harness gap (the ARM lite image has no interpreter, and the GRUB cmdline does not reach systemd on the aarch64 EFI-stub path), not a code gap. |

| 217 | common | `getdents64` | `sys_getdents64` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/217_getdents64.rs:57` | `IMPL` | `F736-affinity-getdents-audit` | `fs/readdir.c, include/linux/dirent.h, include/linux/fs.h dir_emit_dots, fs/ext4/dir.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rewritten against `fs/readdir.c` `SYSCALL_DEFINE3(getdents64)`/`filldir64`. `linux_dirent64` layout byte-exact (d_ino@0, d_off@8, d_reclen@16, d_type@18, d_name@19) and distinct from the legacy `linux_dirent`; reclen = ALIGN(offsetof(d_name)+namlen+1, sizeof(u64)). Return rule is Linux's tail: bytes already packed win over a late backend error, EINVAL only when the buffer cannot hold the FIRST record, 0 only at end-of-directory; `iterate_dir` stores f_pos error-or-not; last record's d_off sealed with the final ctx.pos; count truncated to unsigned int; ENOTDIR decided before any user access; `verify_dirent_name` EIO on empty/`/`/>=PATH_MAX names; signal_pending abandons the walk after the first record. `.`/`..` now emitted for EVERY filesystem (`vfs::readdir_dots`, cursors 0/1, child cookies shifted by 2) with an opt-out for ext4/FUSE which carry their own; before this they were missing on tmpfs, kernfs, devfs, devpts, procfs, sysfs, cgroupfs, tracefs, binfmt_misc and autofs. d_type is honest per backend: tmpfs/kernfs/procfs/sysfs/cgroupfs report the real per-inode type; ext4 decodes the on-disk file_type byte but only when EXT4_FEATURE_INCOMPAT_FILETYPE is set (otherwise DT_UNKNOWN, previously DT_REG for every entry including directories); FUSE passes the daemon's DT_* through including DT_UNKNOWN. ext4 readdir cookies are now byte offsets (Linux `ext4_readdir`) so lseek(d_off) is stable and one listing is O(N) not O(N^2), and a block read error or corrupt rec_len surfaces EIO instead of looking like end-of-directory. PARTIAL: the synthetic filesystems still use ordinal cursors, so an insert/unlink between two getdents calls shifts the cookies of later entries (Linux uses cursor dentries / stable hashes); the f_version stale-cursor restart limits this to a restart rather than a torn listing. Tests: syscalls::getdents_abi (20 hosted), crates/kernel/fs/tests/getdents_dots_tmpfs.rs (6, real tmpfs), crates/kernel/ext4/tests/getdents_dtype_image.rs (5, real ext4 image). Closed in `F775`: the three stated remainders are gone. Synthetic filesystems used ORDINAL cursors — not `d_off` cookies — so entries added or removed mid-listing shifted the position and `seekdir(3)` duplicated or skipped; the shared `kernfs::PseudoDir` loop and all ~50 hand-rolled loops in sysfs, procfs, cgroupfs, tmpfs, binfmt_misc and tracefs/debugfs now use `vfs::readdir_cookie` (name-hash ordered, stable per entry, collisions separated). All 56 `d_ino == 0` sites are gone — a vanished name is omitted from the listing rather than emitted as a deleted placeholder. FUSE re-`OPENDIR`ed inside every getdents call, discarding the daemon's cursor; the new Linux-shaped `FileOps::iterate_file` carries the open description so FUSE reuses its tracked `fh`. Incidental: sysfs `net_class` and `tty` never advanced past their second-to-last entry, so `subsystem` was reachable only by resuming exactly there. `/proc/self` and `/proc/thread-self` were already DT_LNK from the `B1603` symlink work. Harness: `atime_touch` 16, `atime_read_tmpfs` 7, `readdir_cursor` (kernfs 9, procfs 9, cgroup 6, tmpfs 5), `sysfs::readdir` 75->81; 2717 passed / 0 failed across vfs/kernfs/sysfs/procfs/fs, with every mutation test revert-proved (restoring the ordinal loop makes it FAIL); both arches build; stack-gate PASS; both smokes PASS. |

| 219 | common | `restart_syscall` | `sys_restart_syscall` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/219_restart_syscall.rs:38` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/signal.c:3185 (SYSCALL_DEFINE0(restart_syscall)), kernel/signal.c:3191 (do_no_restart_syscall), include/linux/restart_block.h:26-57, include/linux/thread_info.h:83-89 (set_restart_fn), arch/x86/kernel/signal.c (handle_signal, arch_do_signal_or_restart, get_nr_restart_syscall), arch/arm64/kernel/signal.c, kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2425-2462 (hrtimer_nanosleep, do_nanosleep, hrtimer_nanosleep_restart), fs/select.c:1042-1058 (do_restart_poll), kernel/futex/waitwake.c:741-785 (futex_wait, futex_wait_restart)` | `syscall-abi-model` | F741 replaced the `NR_RESTART_SYSCALL => Some(EINTR)` compat stub with Linux's real mechanism. `Task::restart_block` is `struct restart_block` with the indirect call docs/07 5 bans replaced by a kind discriminant plus payload; slot 219 dispatches it and falls back to `do_no_restart_syscall` (EINTR) for a stale or unarmed block, exactly as Linux does. The signal-delivery decision lives in one hosted-tested owner, `syscall::restart::signal_restart_action`, mirroring `handle_signal` (handler frame built: ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK and ERESTARTNOHAND -> EINTR, ERESTARTSYS -> restart only under SA_RESTART, ERESTARTNOINTR -> always restart) and `arch_do_signal_or_restart` (no handler frame -- SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, or `get_signal()` returning nothing: every code restarts the same call except ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK, which rewrites the syscall-number register to this slot). Both arches carry the frame rewrite: x86 `regs->ax = get_nr_restart_syscall(regs); regs->ip -= 2`, arm64 the same against x8 (which `oxide_lower_sync_restore` reloads) and ELR_EL1. F743 completes the client set, so every Linux `restart_block` payload now has its counterpart here: `nanosleep` (`RESTART_NANOSLEEP`, the absolute expiry plus rmtp, shared by nanosleep(2) and the RELATIVE form of clock_nanosleep(2)), `poll` (`RESTART_POLL`, ufds/nfds/has_timeout/end_time, Linux `do_restart_poll`, self-re-arming) and `futex` (`RESTART_FUTEX`, uaddr/op/val/bitset plus the absolute deadline, Linux `futex_wait_restart`). The syscalls that Linux deliberately leaves blockless now say so with the right code instead of EINTR: ppoll/pselect6/select return `-ERESTARTNOHAND` and restart in place off the residual timeout they wrote back, `TIMER_ABSTIME` clock_nanosleep returns `-ERESTARTNOHAND`, and an untimed futex wait returns `-ERESTARTSYS`. F743 also repaired the syscall-return tail's job-control-stop arm, which returned the raw retval after `stop_until_cont_sig` -- it would have leaked the internal -512/-514/-516 sentinels to userspace as bogus errnos, and it skipped the restart Linux performs there (a stop builds no handler frame, so `arch_do_signal_or_restart` applies once SIGCONT resumes the task). Hosted: 7 `syscall::restart` decision tests, 4 `sched::task::restart` block tests, 5 slot-219 continuation-table tests, arm64 nr 128 round-trip, plus the per-client payload tests on rows 7, 35, 202 and 230. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL: **MATCHES** on x86 and aarch64 since B1456. The stop/cont continuation was reaching `rc=0` by the wrong mechanism — the sleep took the stop inside its own park loop and resumed against the same deadline, so `rem` stayed untouched (`wdiff\|sleep\|stopcont_restart_block`, `rem_written=0` vs oracle `1`) and the restart block was never armed. B1456 makes the stop unwind to the syscall-return tail, which is where this row's `RestartBlockCall` rewrite already lived, so the continuation is now genuinely exercised end-to-end by the differential. |
| 220 | common | `semtimedop` | `sys_semtimedop` | vmm | fd-table, ipc, permissions, sched, syscall-abi, usercopy, vmm | `crates/kernel/ipc/src/sysv/sem/op.rs` | `IMPL` | `F760-mqueue-semtimedop-audit` | ipc/sem.c:__do_semtimedop, ipc/sem.c:do_semtimedop | `sysv-sem-model` | Audited against `__do_semtimedop`; shares one `semop_in` body with `semop` (220 only adds the relative timeout), so SEM\_UNDO, `perform_atomic_semop` rollback and the permission ladder are not forked. Order matches Linux: timespec copied first (EFAULT), then `nsops > semopm` E2BIG and `nsops < 1` / `semid < 0` EINVAL, then `timespec64_valid` EINVAL, then the id lookup EINVAL, EFBIG on `sem_num >= nsems`, EACCES from `ipcperms`. Timeout is RELATIVE and saturating (`ktime_add_safe`); an already-expired deadline is EAGAIN without parking; a wake that raced an IPC\_RMID reports EIDRM over its own reason; a signal is EINTR (semop is never restarted); IPC\_NOWAIT is read from the BLOCKING op only. 44 hosted tests in `ipc::sysv::sem`. |
| 221 | common | `fadvise64` | `sys_fadvise64` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/221_fadvise64.rs:39; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:30` | `PARTIAL` | `F756-compat-mm-fs` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, mm/fadvise.c` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F756: was the shared compat `sys_fadvise_validate`, which checked `len < 0` BEFORE the fd (inverting Linux EBADF-first), never checked the advice value, and never returned ESPIPE — so every bad-advice call reported success. Now `generic_fadvise`'s ladder exactly: EBADF, then ESPIPE for a FIFO, then EINVAL for negative offset/len, then EINVAL outside the POSIX six. Effects are real: NORMAL/SEQUENTIAL/RANDOM move the per-open `f_ra.ra_pages` ceiling `File::ra_ondemand` reads on every buffered read; DONTNEED runs `writeback_range` then `invalidate_range` with Linux's whole-page rule; WILLNEED populates the range through the inode mapping, skipping resident pages. Held PARTIAL for two mechanism divergences: WILLNEED populates synchronously where Linux submits async readahead, and NOREUSE carries no reclaim bias because the LRU has no folio-activation hook to read one. 6 hosted tests in `syscalls::fadvise_policy`. B1600 corrected this row's central claim, which was FALSE: `File::ra_ondemand` computes Linux's window faithfully but all three call sites bound the result to `let _`, so nothing ever turned it into I/O — the hints moved `ra_pages`, the window sized itself off `ra_pages`, and ext4 then read a hardcoded 16 pages per miss regardless. Adds `AddressSpaceOps::readahead` (Linux `page_cache_ra_unbounded`), wires the read path to submit, overrides it in ext4 for coalesced window reads, and replaces WILLNEED's and `readahead(2)`'s per-4KiB `read_at` loop (which memcpy'd every page into a scratch it discarded). REMAINING: WILLNEED is still synchronous (no workqueue deferral); NOREUSE records nothing deliberately, since its consumer `mark_lru_referenced` has zero call sites and storing the flag would recreate the same defect; DONTNEED drops still-mapped frames with no mapcount guard and misses Linux's EOF trailing-partial-page discard. Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1970 closes two of the three remainders and withdraws the first. WILLNEED: the claimed divergence does NOT exist — the reference's arm is inline synchronous readahead in the calling thread, not a deferred submission, so there is nothing to change. DONTNEED had two real defects, both fixed: it evicted pages that were still mapped or still dirty, which is not a use-after-free here (a live mapping holds an object reference) but a cache-identity break — the index entry vanished while a shared mapper kept the frame, so the next reader filled a NEW frame and the two stopped aliasing, letting writes diverge silently; and it had none of the index rounding, so no trailing partial page was retained, the end-of-file exception was absent, and an index underflow let a few-byte request evict the whole file. Evidence: 9 hosted range tests with five independent positive controls, 6 mapping-op tests, and an ext4 evictability test whose mapped-guard and dirty-guard controls each go RED. Also fixed: the end-byte overflow comparison was unsigned where the ABI is signed. REMAINING: NOREUSE still records nothing. The reference stores a per-description bit whose only consumer biases reclaim of MAPPED pages; the equivalent hook here has zero call sites, so storing the bit would recreate the stored-but-unconsumed defect this project treats as the gap. Closing it needs the reclaim-side consumer, which is memory-management work. |

Fixed: SIGEV_THREAD (2) was EINVAL and is now accepted and delivered exactly like SIGEV_SIGNAL (the kernel switch falls through); CLOCK_REALTIME_ALARM/CLOCK_BOOTTIME_ALARM were EOPNOTSUPP and now create timers on their base clock gated on CAP_WAKE_ALARM (`alarm_timer_create`); MONOTONIC_RAW/COARSE now EOPNOTSUPP (no `timer_create` callback) rather than sharing an errno with unknown ids; CLOCKFD ids EOPNOTSUPP; the timer-id copyout now precedes the RT-signal reservation so an EFAULT cannot leak one; the slot table was a fixed 8-entry array that EAGAINed a legitimate 9th timer and now grows on demand (`timers/slots.rs`, EAGAIN only at a 1024 interrupt-latency cap, Linux errno for an allocation failure). NULL sigevent defaults to SIGALRM with the timer id as si_value; SIGEV_THREAD_ID requires a thread of the callers own group; signo outside 1..SIGRTMAX is EINVAL. REMAINING GAP: a timer notifying with a STANDARD signal (including the NULL-sigevent SIGALRM default) delivers a synthesized siginfo without si_value, si_overrun or si_code=SI_TIMER, because `sched::sigqueue` only queues siginfo records for RT signals (33..64) by design; carrying siginfo on standard signals is a signal-subsystem change owned outside this row. Hosted: `sched::timers::sigevent::tests` (6), `sched::timers::slots::tests` (3), `sched::timers::tests` (10). Closed in `B1588`: `SI_TIMER` records now carry `si_code=SI_TIMER`, `si_tid` (timer id) and `si_value` for ANY signal number (only RT signals carried a record before), and `si_overrun` is stamped at dequeue off the same `overrun_last` accumulator `timer_getoverrun(2)` reads, so the two cannot disagree — `ssi_tid`/`ssi_overrun` in a signalfd read were 0 for every timer previously. The record slot is now reserved at `timer_create` ON THE SET THE EXPIRY POSTS TO (it previously reserved on the leader's private queue while expiry posted to whatever thread the tick interrupted, so the reservation missed and the IRQ allocated); expiry is `PIDTYPE_TGID` unless `SIGEV_THREAD_ID` named a thread, and runs the same ungated `sigsend` ladder with no reserve, registry walk, or rq lock. Harness: `cargo test -p sched` 753/0 (733 before); both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 60s, smoke-arm PASS 84s. |

Order is now Linux `clock_settime` -> `do_sys_settimeofday64`: EINVAL for a clock with no `clock_set` callback (everything except CLOCK_REALTIME and the CPU clocks), then EFAULT on the copy-in, then `timespec64_valid_settod` EINVAL (negative tv_sec, tv_nsec outside 0..1e9, and now also a tv_sec at or past KTIME_SEC_MAX, which was previously accepted and silently saturated), then CAP_SYS_TIME EPERM. A valid CPU clock id is EPERM and an unresolvable one EINVAL, per `posix_cpu_clock_set`. Hosted: `syscalls::clock_policy::tests` settime ordering, non-settable, CPU-clock and `settod_ns` cases. |

MONOTONIC_RAW equals MONOTONIC and BOOTTIME equals MONOTONIC by construction, which is accurate rather than a shortcut: the timekeeper applies no NTP frequency slew and no suspend has occurred. CLOCK_TAI is structurally distinct but its offset is always 0 because nothing sets it — `adjtimex` (159) and `clock_adjtime` (305) have no route, which is a gap in THOSE rows. CLOCKFD ids are EINVAL, matching `pc_clock_gettime` on a system with no `posix_clock` character device. EINVAL precedes EFAULT. | 230 | common | `clock_nanosleep` | `sys_clock_nanosleep` | time | fd-table, sched-clock, syscall-abi, time, timers, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:77` | `PARTIAL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/time/posix-timers.c, fs/timerfd.c, kernel/time/time.c` | `time-timerfd-model` | Ledger G2/B638: nanosleep and clock_nanosleep now check pending unmasked signals and return EINTR/rem semantics; remaining full-row audit covers absolute timers, restart behavior, and invalid timespec ordering. F743 closes the restart and signal-triage gaps. The slot no longer carries its own signal check: it used the RAW `sigpending & !sigmask`, so a signal Linux drops at SEND time (`kernel/signal.c:1051` `prepare_signal` -> `sig_ignored`; SIG_IGN, or SIG_DFL over SIG_KERNEL_IGNORE_MASK = SIGCHLD/SIGCONT/SIGWINCH/SIGURG) truncated the sleep -- a terminal resize cut every `clock_nanosleep` short. It now shares `nanosleep(2)`'s engine and `Task::sleep_wake`. The ABS/REL split is Linux's: `TIMER_ABSTIME` forces `rmtp = NULL` at the syscall entry (`kernel/time/posix-timers.c:1400-1401`), which makes `restart->nanosleep.type` TT_NONE so no remaining time is ever copied out, and `hrtimer_nanosleep` then returns `-ERESTARTNOHAND` and arms NO block (`kernel/time/hrtimer.c:2449-2453`); the relative form copies the remainder to `rmtp`, arms `hrtimer_nanosleep_restart` with the ABSOLUTE expiry, and returns `-ERESTART_RESTARTBLOCK`. `restart_block.fn = do_no_restart_syscall` is set on entry per `posix-timers.c:1402`. The wait also parks on the scheduler wait list with an absolute deadline instead of busy-yielding. Clock admission already matched `k_clock.nsleep` presence (EINVAL for unknown, EOPNOTSUPP for CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID and the coarse/raw clocks, CAP_WAKE_ALARM for the alarm clocks). Next audit: the CPU-time clocks. Linux sleeps `CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID` against consumed CPU time via `do_cpu_nanosleep`/`posix_cpu_nsleep_restart` (`kernel/time/posix-cpu-timers.c:1630-1665`), while this slot converts every clock into a wall-clock monotonic deadline, so a process-CPU sleep expires on elapsed time rather than CPU time. F751 implements CPU-time sleeping, closing the gap this row named. `clock_nanosleep` on a CPU clock no longer converts to a wall deadline: it arms a timer ON THE CPU CLOCK in the thread group's slot table with a wake-instead-of-signal notification, mirroring Linux `do_cpu_nanosleep`'s temporary `k_itimer` with `it.cpu.nanosleep = true` (`posix-cpu-timers.c:1537-1626`) and `cpu_timer_fire`'s wake branch (`:682-688`). It must be event-driven because a sleeping task accrues no CPU time, so only a RUNNING sibling advances the clock — which is also why a per-thread clock naming the caller is rejected with EINVAL up front (`:1639-1642`). The ABS/REL split is Linux's (`:1646-1653`) and `RESTART_CPU_NANOSLEEP` carries the ABSOLUTE CPU expiry so a resumed sleep owes only its remainder, per `posix_cpu_nsleep_restart` (`:1657-1665`). Residual: a DYNAMIC per-thread clock naming pid 0 reports EOPNOTSUPP where Linux reports EINVAL — `classify_clock` maps it to the same `CpuEncoded` value as the static CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID, which Linux separates by k_clock table, so telling them apart needs static-vs-dynamic provenance on the clock spec. `CpuMeasure::Sched` is still `utime+stime` where Linux `CPUCLOCK_SCHED` is `task_sched_runtime()`. No guest differential yet. F753 GUEST DIFFERENTIAL (x86): **DIVERGES** on three counts. `TIMER_ABSTIME` interrupted returns `rc=0` where Linux returns EINTR, and returned `ENOSYS` on two of three boots — UNSTABLE (`wdiff\|sleep\|abs_sarestart`). `rem` suppression under `TIMER_ABSTIME` is correct. CPU-clock sleep still behaves as a WALL-CLOCK sleep: `clock_nanosleep(CLOCK_PROCESS_CPUTIME_ID)` in a single-threaded process COMPLETES (`outcome=ok`) where Linux blocks until signalled (`outcome=eintr`), because nothing can advance the clock while the only thread sleeps — F751's fix is not observable at runtime (`wdiff\|cputime\|single_thread_no_progress`). `CLOCK_THREAD_CPUTIME_ID` EOPNOTSUPP admission MATCHES. Plan §17 D3. B1972 re-verified this row's four named divergences. The TIMER_ABSTIME one is UNREPRODUCED and its own source reports instability (two of three boots reporting a value that path cannot produce): the absolute arm returns the no-restart-handler sentinel, and the signal-return decision maps that to EINTR when a handler ran and to a restart of the same call when none did, both hosted-tested in `syscall::restart` and `fs::sys_nanosleep_shape`; the only path returning 0 is a deadline already passed, which is the reference's answer too. Treat it as a measurement fault until re-run. The static-vs-dynamic thread CPU clock divergence is REAL and confirmed against the reference: the reference splits the two by table -- the static per-thread id reaches a kclock with no sleep callback and is EOPNOTSUPP, while a negative per-thread encoding reaches the CPU kclock, which HAS one and then rejects a self-naming clock with EINVAL -- and `sched::timers::clockid::classify_clock` maps both onto one value, so the dynamic form inherits the static form's answer. The fix belongs to the clock-id decode in sched, not to this slot: the slot already routes a supported CPU clock into the self-naming EINVAL. `CpuMeasure::Sched` remains the second real item. Both owned by the sched lane; see `scratch/issues.d/B1972-syscall-abi-partial-rows.md`. |

Exit code truncated `& 0xff` per `SYSCALL_DEFINE1(exit_group)`. Plain `exit(2)` verified to leave the group alive. Same `disassociate_ctty(1)` gap as row 60. F794's `exit_shm` runs from the shared `do_exit` tail, so a group exit reclaims the creator's SysV shm segments identically. Harness: `cargo test -p sched -p syscalls -p syscall -p tty` 2280/0; ungated `clone_abi` tests 24; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 100s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. |

Positive controls: dropping the session copy from `fork` makes it fail with -126, and divesting the authority on `exec` makes it and two sibling cases fail. THE REAL REMAINING BLOCKER IS COVERAGE, NOT FUNCTION: there is not one EFAULT assertion in the whole keyring tree - every hosted test calls the core directly with kernel-owned data, so the iovec reader's validate-the-whole-array-before-copying-any-of-it invariant is asserted only in a doc comment - and there is no user-namespace dimension in the key store at all while CAPABILITIES advertises the two namespace bits. (1) The EFAULT coverage hole is closed by moving `KEYCTL_INSTANTIATE_IOV`'s gather into an ungated module that takes the user-memory primitives as a trait, so the ordering is drivable: seven tests now assert that an unreadable array is EFAULT, that a bad LAST segment copies NOTHING (the invariant that was only a doc comment), that the combined-length ceiling is EINVAL ahead of any pointer test, and that a zero-length segment is legal — with the positive control (copy each segment as it is validated) turning the invariant test RED. The boot probe asserts the same ordering against real user memory on both arches, unprivileged: EFAULT for an unreadable array, EFAULT for an unreadable segment, EPERM for a valid vector. (2) The user-namespace dimension is now IN the key store rather than only in the advertisement. `Key` carries the user namespace it was published in and the index-key domain tag; the named-keyring lookup filters on the caller's namespace and applies `kuid_has_mapping`; the per-uid user and user-session keyrings are keyed `(user_ns, uid)` and named after the uid the way the reference names them; the persistent register is one per namespace. The domain tag is consumed by a registered network-scoped type (`dns_resolver`), and search plus `add_key`'s update lookup match on it, so a cached answer from one network namespace is not found from another while an ordinary type stays one key kernel-wide. Seven namespace tests, each with a positive control that flattens exactly one dimension and turns exactly the matching test RED. (3) The RESTRICT_KEYRING clause was STALE — the restriction string has been copied and parsed since the trust-chain work landed. Pinned now by `the_restriction_string_selects_the_rule`, which shows each accepted spelling producing a rule and each rejected one producing EINVAL, so a string that never reached the parser would answer the same for all of them; the argument admission moved to a tested `vet_restrict_args`. 192 keyring tests pass (175 before). |

Closed in `B1575`: `vfs::fsnotify` per-user ucount table with the six live tunables bound to the `/proc/sys/fs/inotify/*` leaves (previously Const leaves bound to nothing) — instances charge EMFILE, watches charge ENOSPC, group queue depth snapshots the sysctl, and `Drop` returns every charge (fixing a leak that left the event fast path permanently armed after a watcher exited). `IN_EXCL_UNLINK` is now consulted (suppresses path-carrying events on an unlinked-but-open file, non-path events still reach the mark). `IN_UNMOUNT` now fires on last-mount detach, followed by `IN_IGNORED` with the wd freed. Harness: `fs` inotify filter 70 -> 92, `cargo test -p fs --lib` 410/0, `-p vfs --lib` 183/0, `-p procfs --lib` 102/0. |

Remaining: mark destroy/unmount lifetime parity, watch-count/sysctl accounting, and hosted syscall-context fd-table coverage. STATUS CORRECTED IN-PROGRESS->PARTIAL (D394): the branch named in the Branch column is merged and deleted, so the work landed long ago. IN-PROGRESS means 'local uncommitted work is currently touching this syscall' -- leaving it set on merged work tells the next agent a live lane owns this row, which is exactly how duplicate lanes get opened. PARTIAL is the honest reading: a committed fix exists, full Linux parity is not proven. B1463: `read()` now emits the full Linux `struct inotify_event` — the dir-entry name NUL-padded to `roundup(name_len + 1, 16)` with `len` set to that PADDED length, `IN_ISDIR` when the affected object is a directory (masked out of `IN_DELETE_SELF`/`IN_MOVE_SELF` as Linux does), and `EINVAL` when the reader's buffer cannot hold the next whole event (`get_one_event`). `fsnotify_parent` added, so a watch on a DIRECTORY now receives named open/access/modify/close events for entries inside it. Layout rules live in the ungated `crates/kernel/fs/src/inotify/layout.rs` with hosted tests, and the wire bytes are diffed against the host kernel by `userspace/wait_diff/inotify.c`. Remaining for this slot: blocking `read()` still returns `EAGAIN` on an empty queue regardless of `O_NONBLOCK`, and the `/proc/sys/fs/inotify/*` limits are not enforced. Closed in `B1575`: `vfs::fsnotify` per-user ucount table with the six live tunables bound to the `/proc/sys/fs/inotify/*` leaves (previously Const leaves bound to nothing) — instances charge EMFILE, watches charge ENOSPC, group queue depth snapshots the sysctl, and `Drop` returns every charge (fixing a leak that left the event fast path permanently armed after a watcher exited). `IN_EXCL_UNLINK` is now consulted (suppresses path-carrying events on an unlinked-but-open file, non-path events still reach the mark). `IN_UNMOUNT` now fires on last-mount detach, followed by `IN_IGNORED` with the wd freed. Harness: `fs` inotify filter 70 -> 92, `cargo test -p fs --lib` 410/0, `-p vfs --lib` 183/0, `-p procfs --lib` 102/0. |

Now `kernel_migrate_pages` (`mm/mempolicy.c:1860`) in order: `get_nodes(old)` then `get_nodes(new)` (EFAULT/EINVAL) -> ESRCH -> `ptrace_may_access(PTRACE_MODE_READ_REALCREDS)` EPERM -> destination outside the target's cpuset without CAP_SYS_NICE EPERM -> `nodes_and` empty EINVAL -> `get_task_mm` EINVAL. `do_migrate_pages` (`:1336`) skips every source==destination pair, so with one node there is no pair to migrate and the result is 0 -- the real Linux answer on a one-node machine. |
| 257 | common | `openat` | `sys_openat` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:83` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/open.c:1385, fs/open.c:1371, fs/namei.c:4320, fs/namei.c:4404, fs/namei.c:4843` | `fs_syscall_model::syscall_shape_covers_udev_runtime_and_user_permissions` | Fixed missing-file O_CREAT to run parent create permission before backend create while preserving Linux existing-file O_CREAT ordering; remaining: complete open flag and final-open permission matrix. B1600: `FMODE_CREATED` is now published by openat (it was defined and never set), which `F_CREATED_QUERY` reads. Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1970 turned the vague remainder into a concrete list and closed it. THREE permission rungs were entirely absent from `may_open`: `O_NOATIME` had no owner-or-capability gate, so any user could pass it on another user's file and silently succeed; an append-only inode had no open-time gate at all, so a plain write-open of one succeeded (the flag was enforced for truncate, fallocate, setattr and unlink, but never at open); and the write reference that makes a write-open of a running executable ETXTBSY was never taken — the primitive existed and was unit-tested, but its only callers were the truncate path and the exec side. The write reference is now taken where the reference takes it and released from the description's `Drop`, not the open call frame. Ordering was also wrong: the read-only-mount rejection ran before the permission ladder for EVERY write-intent open, where the reference runs it before the ladder only for a truncating regular-file open — so a write-open the caller also lacked permission for reported EROFS instead of EACCES. Also made faithful: a just-created file no longer skips the flag rungs wholesale. Evidence: `vfs --test may_open_flag_rungs` 15, `open_write_reference` 8, `syscalls --test open_flags_ladder` 19; six positive controls, one per rung plus one that acquires and releases inside the call frame, each RED (2, 3, 4, 3, 3 and 3 failures respectively) and GREEN restored. |

The copyout is already correctly ordered — the full stat is assembled and only then is the destination buffer validated, so a faulting buffer cannot report EFAULT before a stat that would have failed. And the `AT_EMPTY_PATH` premise was wrong about the reference contract: an empty path with the current-directory dirfd resolves to the current directory and succeeds, it does not report ENOENT, and this implementation already does exactly that. Backends: ext4 gained the `ext4_empty_dir` ENOTEMPTY gate (a populated directory victim previously went straight to `Mount::rmdir`, whose contract is caller-verified-empty, freeing the destination subtree's blocks and inode with its children still linked), the cross-parent `..` repoint plus both parents' `i_links_count` fixups on plain rename AND on EXCHANGE's mixed pair, the `EXT4_LINK_MAX` EMLINK ceiling, and the four `ext4_rename` timestamp stamps; the three path-based ext4 entry points now share one core with `i_op->rename` instead of three divergent copies. tmpfs gained the same ENOTEMPTY gate, `simple_rename`/`simple_rename_exchange` parent-link accounting, and EINVAL for flags outside the three shmem implements. kernfs now answers ANY renameat2 flag with EINVAL (`kernfs_iop_rename`'s `if (flags) return -EINVAL`) instead of honouring NOREPLACE/EXCHANGE and dropping WHITEOUT. Closed in `F775`: `touch_atime` was the sole stated gap. `vfs::inode_times::atime_needs_update` existed with ZERO call sites — access times never moved anywhere in the tree. New ungated `vfs::atime` is wired at every site Linux uses (read/pread/read_iter, getdents, readlink/readlinkat, symlink follow in the walk, file mmap; execve inherits it through mmap as in Linux). Latent bug fixed with it: a new mount was created with `mnt_flags == 0`, which `relatime_need_update` reads as STRICTATIME (relatime is a set bit; strictatime is the absence of NOATIME+RELATIME), so every boot and internal mount would have stamped on every read — `new_mount` and the plain-bind graft now take `path_mount`'s `ms_to_mnt(0)` relatime default. Confirmed against the primary source and pinned by tests: `S_IMMUTABLE` is NOT an atime gate; a read-only mount blocks via write access and a read-only superblock via `IS_NOATIME`. REMAINING (kernel-wide, not this row): lazytime is parsed and stored but not deferred — `wb_writeback` clears `I_DIRTY` without calling `s_op->write_inode`, so deferring the stamp would DISCARD it; atime persists eagerly and lazytime mounts behave as non-lazytime until a timestamp writeback path exists. Harness: `atime_touch` 16, `atime_read_tmpfs` 7, `readdir_cursor` (kernfs 9, procfs 9, cgroup 6, tmpfs 5), `sysfs::readdir` 75->81; 2717 passed / 0 failed across vfs/kernfs/sysfs/procfs/fs, with every mutation test revert-proved (restoring the ordinal loop makes it FAIL); both arches build; stack-gate PASS; both smokes PASS. |

| 270 | common | `pselect6` | `sys_pselect6` | vfs | fd-table, fs-special, poll, signal, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs, waitqueue | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:45` | `IMPL` | `F743-restart-block-completion` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/select.c:780-798 get_sigset_argpack, fs/select.c:748-778 do_pselect, fs/select.c:894-916 SYSCALL_DEFINE5(ppoll), fs/select.c:283-350 poll_select_finish, fs/select.c:257-272 poll_select_set_timeout, fs/select.c:483-620 do_select, fs/select.c:621-696 core_sys_select, fs/select.c:700-745 do_poll, fs/select.c:838-885 do_sys_poll, kernel/signal.c:3282 set_user_sigmask, include/linux/sched/signal.h:541-555 restore_saved_sigmask_unless`; B854-socket-file-ownership | `fd-vfs-io-model; syscalls::pselect6_sigmask_hosted (12); syscalls::pselect_ppoll unit tests (11); fs::sys_select_shape; syscalls::select_file_ownership_hosted; x86_64 and aarch64 kernel builds` | Audited line-by-line against Linux `SYSCALL_DEFINE6(pselect6)` -> `get_sigset_argpack` -> `do_pselect`. The 6th argument is read as `struct sigset_argpack { const sigset_t *ss; size_t ss_len; }` FIRST, so a faulting pack is EFAULT even when the timespec is also malformed, and the installed mask comes from `*ss` rather than the pack word itself. The sigmask handshake is now Linux's, not a swap-and-swap-back: `set_user_sigmask` arms `Task::arm_saved_sigmask` (Linux `saved_sigmask = blocked; set_current_blocked(new); set_restore_sigmask()`) and `poll_select_finish` calls `restore_saved_sigmask_unless(ret == -ERESTARTNOHAND)`, so an interrupted wait keeps the temporary mask for the handler and `rt_sigreturn` lands on the caller's original mask via `Task::sigmask_to_save`; the earlier eager restore reopened the delivery race the syscall exists to close. A NULL sigset pointer leaves the mask untouched whatever `sigsetsize` says; a non-NULL one is size-checked through the shared `syscall::sigset::check_exact`. `poll_select_set_timeout` reads the `struct __kernel_timespec` (EFAULT), validates it through the shared `syscall::time::timespec_to_ns` `ktime_set` clamp (EINVAL for tv_sec < 0 or tv_nsec outside [0, 1e9)), and folds it into an absolute monotonic end_time before the mask is installed and before the wait. NULL timeout waits indefinitely; a {0,0} timeout makes exactly one non-blocking pass. `poll_select_finish` writes the remaining time back to the caller's timespec as the raw syscall does (glibc's wrappers hide it behind a local copy), skipping the update for a zero timeout and for a `STICKY_TIMEOUTS` persona, and swallowing a writeback fault exactly as Linux does. `crate::pselect_ppoll::wait_verdict` now owns `do_poll`/`do_select` break order for both engines, so readiness outranks a deliverable signal which outranks an expired deadline: a zero timeout with a pending signal is EINTR, matching `count = -ERESTARTNOHAND` before `if (count or timed_out) break`. `signal_pending` is `Task::deliverable_signals`, the same `sig_ignored`-filtered definition every other blocking path uses, so a default-ignored signal cannot spuriously interrupt an event loop. Register-then-recheck holds: the waiter subscribes to every polled fd before the first readiness scan, the notification generation is snapshot before the scan, and `PollWaiter::park_until` publishes Sleeping before comparing it, so a wakeup landing in the scan-to-park gap cannot be lost. Busy-polling (`POLL_BUSY_LOOP`) is a `SO_BUSY_POLL`/`net.core.busy_poll` latency path owned by the socket-option rows and is off in Linux's default configuration, where these engines take the identical path. An interrupted select leaves the caller's fd sets byte-for-byte untouched (`core_sys_select`'s `if (ret < 0) goto out`), negative `nfds` is EINVAL, and a set bit naming an unopened fd is EBADF as `max_select_fd` reports it. B854 file retention across close/reuse is preserved. F743: `core_sys_select` now surfaces Linux's `-ERESTARTNOHAND` to the syscall tail instead of a pre-folded `-EINTR`. That distinction is the whole point of the code: with a handler frame built, `handle_signal` turns ERESTARTNOHAND into EINTR (unchanged behaviour for userspace), but with NO handler frame -- SIG_DFL, SIG_IGN, a job-control stop, or a raced dequeue -- `arch_do_signal_or_restart` RESTARTS the call, which this kernel previously never did. pselect6 arms no restart block, matching `do_pselect` (`fs/select.c:739-772`): the residual timeout written back to the caller's timespec is what makes an in-place restart correct. `poll_select_finish` now also implements the `sticky:` tail (`fs/select.c:361-363`), folding ERESTARTNOHAND to EINTR exactly when that writeback could not happen (STICKY_TIMEOUTS or a faulting copyout), per the comment at `fs/select.c:353` -- 'because we are not updating the timeval, we cannot restart the system call'. Rules owned by `pselect_ppoll::{wait_verdict, timeout_writeback_plan, finish_return}` with hosted tests. |

| 272 | common | `unshare` | `sys_unshare` | sched | cgroup, fd-table, ipc, namespaces, procfs, sched, security, syscall-abi, task-lifetime | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/272_unshare.rs; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/unshare_policy.rs; crates/kernel/nscg/src/cgroup_ns.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `F742-system-control-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/fork.c:ksys_unshare+check_unshare_flags, kernel/nsproxy.c:unshare_nsproxy_namespaces, kernel/user_namespace.c:create_user_ns, kernel/cgroup/cgroup.c:copy_cgroup_ns` | `crates/kernel/syscalls/tests/namespace_ownership_hosted.rs` | F742 audited slot 272 against /home/nd/oxide/linux-master `kernel/fork.c` + `kernel/nsproxy.c`. FIXED, four divergences, one of them a security hole: (1) THERE WAS NO CAPABILITY TEST AT ALL. Linux gates the entire namespace set behind ONE `ns_capable(user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN)` in `unshare_nsproxy_namespaces`; lacking it, any unprivileged process could hand itself a private mount, network, UTS, IPC, PID, cgroup or time namespace. The gate is in, scoped through `nscg::proc_ns::has_cap_for`, carrying Linux's exemption for `CLONE_NEWUSER` alone (`CLONE_NS_ALL & ~CLONE_NEWUSER`). (2) `check_unshare_flags`'s thread rungs were absent -- `CLONE_THREAD`/`CLONE_SIGHAND`/`CLONE_VM` were accepted and dropped on the floor. Linux does NOT reject them outright (this lane's briefing had that backwards): it accepts them as no-ops when there is nothing to unshare, and returns EINVAL when `!thread_group_empty(current)`, when `sighand->count > 1` for SIGHAND/VM, or when the caller is multi-threaded for VM. All three rungs are implemented, which is also what makes `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER)` from a multi-threaded process EINVAL through its `CLONE_THREAD` implication. (3) `CLONE_SYSVSEM` and `CLONE_NEWIPC` were accepted without running Linux's `exit_sem(current)` -- 'CLONE_SYSVSEM is equivalent to sys_exit()' -- so a caller kept SEM_UNDO adjustments naming arrays it could no longer reach; the detach now runs once every replacement has succeeded. (4) `CLONE_NEWCGROUP` allocated a namespace identity nothing consulted, so a task inside it still read the host's absolute cgroup path. `nscg::cgroup_ns` now holds Linux's `copy_cgroup_ns` state -- the creating task's cgroup becomes the namespace root -- and `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` renders with `cgroup_path_ns_locked` semantics against the READER's namespace, including kernfs's `..`-relative form for a target outside the reader's root. Per-flag verification of what is genuinely created rather than recorded: NEWNS allocates a real `vfs::mntns` and copies the mount table plus the devfs view into it, remapping the caller's own fs paths; NEWUTS allocates real `nscg::uts_ns` storage seeded from the parent; NEWIPC keys real SysV sem/shm/msg and POSIX mqueue lookups; NEWNET creates a real `net::net_ns`; NEWPID is correctly for-children-only and leaves the caller's own pid namespace in place; NEWTIME clones real monotonic/boottime offsets into a for-children namespace; NEWUSER allocates a real parented user namespace that `has_cap_for` and the `/proc/<pid>/uid_map` machinery already scope against; NEWCGROUP is real as of this row. `CLONE_FILES` and `CLONE_FS` detach real private owners. PARTIAL for one disclosed gap: Linux's `create_user_ns` gives the new credentials a full capability set INSIDE the new user namespace, which is what lets a rootless caller stack `unshare(CLONE_NEWUSER \| CLONE_NEWNS)` in one call. Oxide's capability model is a single global effective mask with namespace ancestry applied at each call site, so granting that set here would make every un-scoped `has_cap()` call site in the tree answer true -- the grant needs per-user-namespace credential scoping in `sched::cred` (`docs/27`) first, and until then an unprivileged rootless `unshare` fails closed with EPERM rather than succeeding. Second disclosed gap: cgroupfs MOUNT rooting inside a cgroup namespace still exposes the whole tree; only the `/proc/<pid>/cgroup` rendering is namespace-relative. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls --test namespace_ownership_hosted` (30 tests; 7 new end-to-end ones drive the real `sys_unshare` over a real `sched::Task` -- per-flag EPERM without CAP_SYS_ADMIN, NEWUSER-alone exempt, FILES/FS/SYSVSEM uncapped, single-threaded thread-flag no-ops, unknown-bit EINVAL with no mutation, SEM_UNDO detach through the real `ipc` undo list, cgroup-namespace root pinning) plus 10 new `unshare_policy` unit tests for the implied-flag expansion and the EINVAL ladder, and 12 new `nscg::cgroup_ns` tests. Both arches build; x86 and arm boot smoke reach `basic.target`. |

| 275 | common | `splice` | `sys_splice` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:86` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/275_splice.rs` -> `crates/kernel/fs/src/splice/splice_sys.rs` | `PARTIAL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/splice.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE6(splice), fs/splice.c:__do_splice, fs/splice.c:do_splice, fs/splice.c:splice_file_to_pipe, fs/splice.c:do_splice_from, fs/splice.c:splice_pipe_to_pipe, fs/splice.c:wait_for_space, fs/splice.c:ipipe_prep, fs/splice.c:opipe_prep, fs/splice.c:splice_from_pipe_next, fs/pipe.c:get_pipe_info, include/linux/splice.h` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. The pre-fix handler had no pipe involvement at all - it ran a kernel read/write loop between ANY two descriptions, so `splice` between two regular files copied data where Linux returns `EINVAL`, and it `seek`ed the inputs to the offset arguments instead of treating them as separate cursors. Rebuilt over the pipe ring (`fs::splice` + `fs::pipe::{link_pipe,ipipe_prep,opipe_prep}`); `sched` sits below `fs` and cannot reach that ring, which is why the work-fn moved crates. FIXED: `len == 0` returns 0 before any fd or flag validation (fs/splice.c:1620); `flags & ~SPLICE_F_ALL` -> `EINVAL`; the three-case selection with `EINVAL` when neither end is a pipe and when both ends are the SAME pipe; `ESPIPE` for an offset supplied on a pipe end, raised in `__do_splice` BEFORE the offsets are copied in so it precedes any `EFAULT`; `EINVAL` for an offset on a non-seekable file end (a different errno from the pipe case, fs/splice.c:1330/1359); `EINVAL` for `O_APPEND` on the output; `EBADF` for `FMODE_READ`/`FMODE_WRITE`. `O_NONBLOCK` on EITHER description adds `SPLICE_F_NONBLOCK` (fs/splice.c:1323). The wait states keep Linux differing errno ORDER: the input side checks signal, then all-writers-closed EOF (which returns 0 even when non-blocking), then `EAGAIN`; the output side checks no-readers `EPIPE` (with `SIGPIPE`) first, then `EAGAIN`, then signal. Offsets are copied in, advanced only by bytes moved, and copied back only on success. Partial transfers report the moved count and suppress a pending `EAGAIN`/`ERESTARTSYS`/`EPIPE`. The pipe-to-file leg PEEKS the ring and consumes only what the write accepted, so a short or failing write cannot swallow queued bytes. GAP (reason for PARTIAL): `SPLICE_F_MORE` is accepted but not translated into `MSG_MORE` on a socket output, so a pipe-to-socket splice may emit an extra segment; `SPLICE_F_MOVE` is a hint Linux itself mostly ignores and `SPLICE_F_GIFT` is meaningless to `splice`. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --lib splice::flags` (7 admission tests) + `cargo test -p fs --test splice_pipe_model` (11 tests against the REAL anonymous-pipe ring: pipe-to-pipe consumes, self-splice EINVAL, two regular files EINVAL, offset-on-a-pipe ESPIPE); both arch kernel builds green. B1970 sharpened the remainder. The `SPLICE_F_MORE` gap is real and its blocker is in the splice leg, not the socket: the pipe-to-output leg calls the plain write entry points, which take no per-operation flags word, so there is no channel the hint could travel down even in principle. Closing it needs a flags-carrying write path first; the send-side hint is a second, dependent step. |
| 276 | common | `tee` | `sys_tee` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:87` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/275_splice.rs` -> `crates/kernel/fs/src/splice/tee_sys.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/splice.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE4(tee), fs/splice.c:do_tee, fs/splice.c:link_pipe, fs/splice.c:ipipe_prep, fs/splice.c:opipe_prep, fs/pipe.c:get_pipe_info, include/linux/splice.h` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. HEADLINE BUG FIXED: `tee` forwarded to the splice read/write loop, which CONSUMED the input - so the syscall whose entire purpose is to duplicate pipe contents while leaving them readable destroyed every byte it touched, and the `tee`/`dd` shell idiom silently lost data. It is now Linux `link_pipe`: a duplication that leaves the source queue intact, with both rings locked for the whole transfer in ADDRESS order so two crossing `tee(a,b)`/`tee(b,a)` calls cannot deadlock (they share one lock class, so rank alone does not order them). Also FIXED: `flags & ~SPLICE_F_ALL` -> `EINVAL` checked BEFORE the `len == 0` short-circuit, the reverse of `splice` (fs/splice.c:1979-1982); `EBADF` for `FMODE_READ`/`FMODE_WRITE` checked BEFORE the pipe test; one shared `EINVAL` for input-not-a-pipe, output-not-a-pipe and same-pipe; an input pipe whose writers have all closed is EOF -> 0, not `EAGAIN`, even when non-blocking. Only the output ring wakes readers - the input lost nothing, so its write-side waiters stay parked. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test splice_pipe_model` (a direct duplication test that reads the SAME bytes back out of BOTH pipes after the call - this test fails against the pre-fix behaviour - plus bounded-by-len, the non-pipe/self/EBADF rejections, and EOF-as-zero) + `cargo test -p fs --lib splice::flags::tee_needs_two_distinct_pipes`; both arch kernel builds green. |
| 277 | common | `sync_file_range` | `sys_sync_file_range` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:97` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/277_sync_file_range.rs` -> `crates/kernel/fs/src/sync/range.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/sync.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE4(sync_file_range), fs/sync.c:ksys_sync_file_range, fs/sync.c:sync_file_range, fs/sync.c:VALID_FLAGS, include/uapi/linux/fs.h:SYNC_FILE_RANGE_*` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. The slot was folded into `sys_fsync`, which answered a different question entirely: it committed filesystem METADATA - which Linux states outright this syscall writes none of (fs/sync.c:341-346) - and ignored `flags`, `offset` and `nbytes`, so a garbage flag word succeeded and a pipe fd returned 0. FIXED, in Linux order (fs/sync.c:232-268): fd lookup `EBADF` first, then `flags & ~VALID_FLAGS` -> `EINVAL`, then `offset < 0` / `endbyte < 0` / `endbyte < offset` -> `EINVAL` (a wrapping `offset + nbytes` is caught by the latter two, exactly as Linux computes it rather than being rejected by an early overflow guard), then `nbytes == 0` means out-to-EOF with an INCLUSIVE endbyte otherwise, then the file-type gate: anything that is not `S_ISREG`, `S_ISBLK` or `S_ISDIR` is `ESPIPE`, NOT `EINVAL`. No `FMODE_WRITE` requirement - Linux ladder never inspects `f_mode`, so a read-only description may flush a shared inode. The WRITE phase is a genuinely range-scoped `AddressSpaceOps::writeback_range` (ext4 overrides it against its own per-page dirty set; tmpfs pages ARE the store so its writeback is correctly a no-op), so this is no longer a whole-file fsync and no longer touches metadata. The WAIT_BEFORE/WAIT_AFTER phases have nothing left to await because oxide writeback is synchronous - the same position Linux reaches once `filemap_fdatawrite_range` has returned - and only harvest the address space deferred error. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --lib sync::range` (4 tests: unknown flags rejected before the offset checks, the three offset sign/order rules incl. the wrapping case, zero-nbytes-means-EOF vs inclusive endbyte, the REG/BLK/DIR-only type gate) + `cargo test -p fs --test file_io_range_model` (the ladder over a real tmpfs description plus ESPIPE on a real pipe with the flag/offset checks still taking precedence); both arch kernel builds green. |
| 278 | 64 | `vmsplice` | `sys_vmsplice` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:88` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/275_splice.rs` -> `crates/kernel/fs/src/splice/vmsplice_sys.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/splice.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE4(vmsplice), fs/splice.c:vmsplice_to_pipe, fs/splice.c:vmsplice_to_user, fs/splice.c:iter_to_pipe, fs/splice.c:add_to_pipe, fs/splice.c:user_page_pipe_buf_try_steal, lib/iov_iter.c:import_iovec, include/uapi/linux/uio.h` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. FIXED: (a) only the user-to-pipe direction existed, so `vmsplice` on a READ pipe end APPENDED the caller buffer to the pipe instead of draining the pipe into it; the direction now comes from `f_mode` alone (fs/splice.c:1593-1598), with `FMODE_WRITE` winning when the description carries both. (b) A description that is not a pipe is `EBADF`, not `EINVAL` (fs/splice.c:1512, :1545); an `O_PATH` fd with neither access bit is also `EBADF`. (c) `flags & ~SPLICE_F_ALL` -> `EINVAL` before the fd lookup. (d) SECURITY: the old loop read each `iov_base` with `read_volatile` having validated ONLY the iovec array entry, never the segment it pointed at - an arbitrary kernel-address read driven by a userspace pointer. Every segment is now range-validated in the direction the transfer needs (writable for the pipe-to-user leg). (e) `nr_segs > UIO_MAXIOV` -> `EINVAL`, a segment whose length is negative as `ssize_t` -> `EINVAL`, and the running total truncates at `MAX_RW_COUNT` rather than erroring, matching `lib/iov_iter.c:1389-1404`. A zero-length vector returns 0. The output side runs `wait_for_space` once up front, so `EPIPE` (with `SIGPIPE`), `EAGAIN` and `ERESTARTSYS` keep Linux ordering; the input side returns 0 at EOF. GAP (reason for PARTIAL): `SPLICE_F_GIFT` is accepted and the bytes are copied, where Linux marks the pipe buffers `PIPE_BUF_FLAG_GIFT` so a downstream consumer MAY steal the page instead of copying. The oxide pipe ring is a byte array with no page identity to hand over, so the zero-copy optimisation has nothing to attach to; the delivered bytes are identical either way, and Linux also falls back to copying whenever the consumer declines the steal. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --test splice_pipe_model` (direction-follows-fmode round trip through a real ring, non-pipe EBADF for both directions, zero-length) + `cargo test -p fs --lib splice::flags::vmsplice_direction_from_fmode`; both arch kernel builds green. B1970: the stated gap is NOT a behavioural gap and the row is promoted. `SPLICE_F_GIFT` only authorises a downstream consumer to steal the page instead of copying it; it changes no delivered byte and no errno, and the reference falls back to copying whenever the steal is declined. A copying ring therefore satisfies the contract exactly — the divergence is a zero-copy optimisation, not semantics. |
