FIXED: (a) ORDERING - the fd lookup now precedes every argument check (`ksys_fallocate`, fs/open.c:355-363), so `fallocate(-1, 0, -1, -1)` is `EBADF` and not `EINVAL`; an `O_PATH` fd is `EBADF` because it carries neither access bit. (b) ERRNOS - the only `EINVAL` in the whole function is `offset < 0 // len <= 0`; every mode-combination rejection is `EOPNOTSUPP` (PUNCH_HOLE without KEEP_SIZE; COLLAPSE/INSERT/WRITE_ZEROES *with* KEEP_SIZE; any two mode bits; NO_HIDE_STALE; any bit outside `MODE_MASK/KEEP_SIZE`), and arithmetic overflow plus the `s_maxbytes` cap are `EFBIG`. The pre-fix code returned `EINVAL` for all of these. (c) MISSING GATES ADDED - append-only inode with a non-plain mode -> `EPERM`, immutable -> `EPERM`, swapfile -> `ETXTBSY`, FIFO -> `ESPIPE`, directory -> `EISDIR`, anything not REG-or-BLK -> `ENODEV`, absent backend op -> `EOPNOTSUPP`. (d) THE INODE OP was three booleans (`keep_size`/`zero_range`/`punch`) that could not express COLLAPSE_RANGE, INSERT_RANGE, UNSHARE_RANGE or WRITE_ZEROES, so the VFS had to pre-judge on the backend behalf; it is now the Linux shape `fallocate(inode, mode: u32, offset, len)` with a `Eopnotsupp` default, and each filesystem states its own answer. (e) SPLIT SOURCE OF TRUTH REMOVED - `ioctl_preallocate` (FS_IOC_RESVSP) carried a SECOND, disagreeing copy of the ladder (its own EBADF/range checks, `EINVAL` on wraparound where Linux gives `EFBIG`); it now routes through `vfs_fallocate` with `mode / KEEP_SIZE` per fs/ioctl.c:289, and the duplicate `FALLOC_FL_*` constants in the ioctl uapi module are deleted. `madvise_remove` now passes `PUNCH_HOLE / KEEP_SIZE` explicitly (mm/madvise.c:1038). (f) RLIMIT_FSIZE is enforced where Linux enforces it - in the filesystem via `inode_newsize_ok` (SIGXFSZ then `EFBIG`), unconditionally for tmpfs even under KEEP_SIZE (mm/shmem.c:3651-3654) and only for a size-growing non-KEEP_SIZE call on ext4 (fs/ext4/extents.c:4823-4829). (g) tmpfs `F_SEAL_GROW` was gated on `!KEEP_SIZE`; Linux does not gate it (mm/shmem.c:3657) - fixed. CORRECTIONS to the audit brief, verified in source: NO_HIDE_STALE never reaches the mode `switch` because step 2 `mode & ~(MODE_MASK/KEEP_SIZE)` catches it first (same errno, different arm); and the `s_maxbytes` `EFBIG` arm is unreachable in oxide today because every `SuperBlock` hardcodes `s_maxbytes = MAX_LFS_FILESIZE`, so anything surviving the overflow check is within it - it is implemented and its constant asserted rather than exercised. GAPS (reason for PARTIAL): ext4 COLLAPSE_RANGE and INSERT_RANGE return `Eopnotsupp` from the backend because both re-index every extent past the range and the extent tree has no shift primitive - Linux returns `EOPNOTSUPP` from filesystems lacking the feature so the errno is honest, but this is not feature-complete ext4. `S_SWAPFILE` is defined and the `ETXTBSY` gate is implemented and tested, but nothing sets the flag: the oxide swapfile pin is ext4-private (`Ext4FileData::swap_active`), so a live swapfile is refused with `EBUSY` from the backend instead of `ETXTBSY` from the VFS; wiring it needs the swap device to hold an `InodeRef`, which is a separate refactor. ext4 WRITE_ZEROES shares the eager-zeroing path with ZERO_RANGE (oxide zero-range produces initialized extents, which satisfies both contracts) because Linux gates it on `bdev_write_zeroes_unmap_sectors`, which has no oxide equivalent. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --lib fallocate::mode` (11 tests incl. an assertion that the mode decision NEVER yields `EINVAL` for any input) + `cargo test -p fs --test fallocate_vfs_ladder` (11) + `cargo test -p fs --test fallocate_tmpfs_backend` (5); `cargo test -p vfs -p ext4` 1740 passed with zero new failures; both arch kernel builds green. B1970 re-verified both gaps and both stand. The collapse-range and insert-range modes are admitted by the mode gate and have no backend arm, and there is no extent shift primitive anywhere in the crate to build one on. The swapfile gate is implemented and tested but the flag has NO producer outside test fixtures: the live swapfile pin is filesystem-private, so an attempt is refused with EBUSY from the backend rather than ETXTBSY from the VFS. The swap layer holds a block-device handle, not an inode reference, which is what wiring it would need. |

| 295 | 64 | `preadv` | `sys_preadv` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:58` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/295_preadv.rs` + `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/rwf.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE5(preadv), fs/read_write.c:do_preadv, fs/read_write.c:pos_from_hilo, fs/read_write.c:vfs_readv, fs/read_write.c:do_loop_readv_writev, lib/iov_iter.c:import_iovec, include/uapi/linux/uio.h:UIO_MAXIOV, include/linux/fs.h:MAX_RW_COUNT` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. TWO REAL BUGS FIXED. (1) The x86_64 offset used the 32-BIT COMPAT hi/lo split `(pos_l & 0xffffffff) / (pos_h << 32)`. Native 64-bit `pos_from_hilo` shifts `high` left by 32 TWICE and therefore discards it entirely (fs/read_write.c:1115-1119), and glibc `LO_HI_LONG` expands to a single argument on a 64-bit target - so `pos_h` is a register the caller never set. Every `preadv` on x86_64 therefore read at `offset / (junk << 32)` whenever that register happened to be nonzero, and any offset above 4 GiB was truncated. The same formula, and the same fix, applies to `pwritev`/`pwritev2` (slots 296/328) where it corrupted data at a wild WRITE offset. (2) The handler called `file.inode().read()` directly instead of `File::pread`, bypassing `FMODE_PREAD` (so `preadv` on a pipe DRAINED it rather than returning `ESPIPE`), `FMODE_READ` (a write-only fd succeeded instead of `EBADF`) and the directory `EISDIR` gate. Ladder now matches `do_preadv` (fs/read_write.c:1121-1140): `pos < 0` -> `EINVAL` BEFORE the fd lookup, then `EBADF`, then `ESPIPE`, then `vfs_readv` `FMODE_READ` `EBADF`, then the iovec import (`iovcnt > UIO_MAXIOV` -> `EINVAL`, `iovcnt == 0` is a legal zero-length read returning 0, a segment length negative as `ssize_t` -> `EINVAL`, the running total truncated at `MAX_RW_COUNT`), and a partial read reports the accumulated count rather than the error. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib rwf` (6 tests, including one that pins the 64-bit rule by asserting four different junk `pos_h` values leave the offset unchanged and that a > 4 GiB offset survives) ; both arch kernel builds green. |

The composition now lives in ungated `mmsg_batch::run` behind a `BatchOps` trait of one-step ABI operations; the slot file implements the trait and holds no loop, and both the unit tests and the new corpus drive the real runner. `crates/kernel/syscalls/tests/conformance_mmsg.rs` compares it against real `recvmmsg`/`sendmmsg`/`SO_ERROR` on real loopback sockets on the host: compat refusal ahead of a bad descriptor and on a ready socket, malformed-timeout-before-descriptor with a valid one falling through to EBADF, pending error ahead of the batch and an error-queue read leaving it in place, zero-length batch, whole-queue drain, empty-queue failure, a 1400-deep queue measured with plain recv(2) proving no UIO_MAXIOV clamp on the receive side, MSG_WAITFORONE draining without waiting and latching nothing, timeout writeback only after a delivery, and a partial batch reporting the count while latching EFAULT into SO_ERROR (EAGAIN latching nothing). Every one of the eight rules was mutated in turn and each mutation turns the corpus red. Remaining, unchanged: guest syscall-context differential over real uaccess. Row stays NEEDS-REWORK on those. B1942: the compat `mmsghdr` layout and the restart coupling are closed. `mmsg_batch::run` now asks `msg_layout::entry_layout` first — ahead of the timeout, the descriptor and every entry — and hands the answer to the ops through `use_layout`, so the 32-byte compat stride and the `msg_len` offset at 28 come from the same decision the guard does. The restart rules had NO test: four in `syscalls::mmsg_batch::tests` now pin that an interrupted empty batch hands the ERESTARTSYS sentinel out RAW (a flattened EINTR can never be resumed by the tail under any handler/SA_RESTART combination) and leaves the caller's timespec alone, so the restarted call sees its full timeout. There is NO restart block for a socket receive in Linux — the call restarts whole — and that is pinned rather than invented. Positive controls: flattening the sentinel, and writing the timeout back on a restart, each turn three tests red. `syscalls::mmsg_batch::tests` 27 (was 20); conformance corpus still green. B1952 closes the send/receive security-hook gap named on rows 44/46/47/299/307. There was no single decision: the generic per-operation verdict was evaluated in nine places at four depths and the sandbox's address verdict in a tenth, and they disagreed about ordering — a send was refused only AFTER its family had answered ENODATA/EOPNOTSUPP/EINVAL, and a blocking receive re-asked its verdict once per wakeup rather than once per transaction. One boundary (`net::socket_security`) now composes sandbox then module registry and is the only place either is consulted for a message; `socket::security::admit` is the one send call site (top of `send::prepare`, ahead of every family validation, plus the two out-of-band envelope paths) and `syscalls::recv_admit::admit_and_route` the one receive call site — the protocol owner is obtainable ONLY from that call, so no receive can reach a protocol unadmitted. Per-family and per-transport copies deleted from `net::sock::sendto`, `net::sock_io::recvfrom_opts`, `recvmsg::{inet,netlink,vsock}`, `unix_recv`, and `socket::send`'s four inline sites. Two behaviour fixes fall out: a STREAM send naming an address no longer asks for the connect right (it settles no new port; asking denied sends a sandboxed program may make), and an out-of-band send with no urgent channel imports its envelope before the hook so unreadable address memory reports EFAULT, not a permission answer. The sandbox snapshot now travels in `SendContext` so one batch cannot be judged against two policies. Harness: `net::socket_security::tests` 6, `syscalls::recv_admit::tests` 4, `socket::security_hooks_tests` 5 (which replace three tests that asserted on the SOURCE TEXT of `send.rs`/`address.rs` and could not fail for any behavioural regression). Positive controls, each RED then green: deleting the call from `send::prepare` reddens all five send tests; deleting it from `admit_and_route` reddens two receive tests; restoring the transport-derived send right reddens the stream test; swapping the two modules' order reddens the ordering test. Both arch target checks pass; smoke-x86 PASS 129s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. One consequence needed its own fix: removing the transport's duplicate evaluation let the raw IPv4 branch inline into `net::sock::sendto`, whose frame every other protocol's send then carried — the aarch64 worst-case send path grew 480 B and crossed the 13000 B stack-depth ceiling. The branch and `socket::send::send_inet` are `#[inline(never)]` now and the deepest path is 12960 B (main: 12880 B). B1959: the shared extended-error dequeue and encoding both batch layers use is rewritten — see row 47. `reports_pending_error` is unchanged; what a batch dequeues under `MSG_ERRQUEUE` now carries every origin, the corrected pending-errno relationship and the corrected offender/name ladders. B1963 closes this row's last item — the guest syscall-context differential over real uaccess — and it promotes. The copy-fault rules a batch inherits are not the batch's own: `recvmmsg` runs the same per-entry receive as `recvmsg`, so the fault transaction has ONE owner (`syscalls::recv_txn`, see row 47) and the batch layer adds nothing to it. What was untestable was the interaction of that transaction with the batch rules, because both the receive copyout and the slot file are target-gated. `userspace/glibc_conformance/t_recvfault.c` now drives it through the real syscall entry over real mappings: a batch whose SECOND entry cannot be delivered reports the delivered count and latches EFAULT as the socket's pending error, while the same fault on the FIRST entry reports EFAULT and latches nothing, with the remaining queue depth measured after each so what every entry consumed is pinned rather than inferred. Alongside it, the single-message cases the batch is built from: an unplaceable datagram payload reported EFAULT and retired anyway, a peeked one left queued, a half-placed one still retired, an unwritable `msg_name` failing after the payload landed, an unwritable `msghdr` failing before the transport is asked at all, and an unwritable or half-writable control buffer NOT failing the receive while publishing exactly the prefix that landed. 22 lines, compared against a live host oracle, PASS on x86_64 and aarch64. The channel's own positive control is the pre-branch kernel, which diverges on `stream_payload_split`. Manifest row added. Both arch kernel checks pass, `make stack-gate` PASS, smoke both arches PASS. |

REMAINING: `FAN_REPORT_PIDFD` and `FAN_REPORT_TID` records are validated but not emitted; permission events sit in a queue separate from the notification queue so a daemon sees them ahead of ordinary events; `FAN_MARK_MNTNS` binds no real mntns object; `FAN_MARK_EVICTABLE` and `FAN_MARK_IGNORED_SURV_MODIFY` are stored without eviction/survive-write behavior; no fsid/fid export capability check on the marked path. B1642: permission events sat in a SEPARATE queue drained before notifications, so a daemon saw perm events ahead of the events explaining them - now one queue in arrival order. A denial reported EACCES; Linux reports EPERM (all three call sites fixed). The write() response ladder did not exist (any 8-byte word accepted, floor(N/8) responses per write, unknown fd silently ignored) - now one response per write with the full EINVAL/ENOENT ladder. The accessor spun on tick_yield() with no signal check - now parks on a per-group wait list, breaks only on a fatal signal (TASK_KILLABLE), and cancels so a late verdict cannot resume a dead task. Now emitted: FAN_REPORT_PIDFD record, FAN_REPORT_TID pid selection, FAN_ONDIR echo to fid-mode groups. event_f_flags was ignored (event fd always O_RDONLY). GAPS -> B1644: FAN_REPORT_MNT/FAN_MARK_MNTNS inert (FAN_MNT_ATTACH/DETACH validated, never fired); FAN_INFO records rejected not parsed; no FAN_FS_ERROR emission or ERROR/RANGE/MNT info records. |

It shares slot 159's discipline loop and differs in exactly the three ways Linux does, each covered by a test: the buffer is copied in BEFORE `which_clock` is examined, so a bad pointer is EFAULT even for a nonsense clock id; a valid `posix_clocks[]` entry with no `clock_adj` callback is EOPNOTSUPP, distinct from the EINVAL of an id outside the table -- collapsing the two would tell an NTP client its clock id was malformed when the clock is merely undisciplined; and the copy-back is conditional on success (`if (err >= 0 && copy_to_user(...))`) where 159's is unconditional. The admission table is `sched::posix_clock::adjustable`, added alongside the existing `settable`/`timer_creatable`/`nsleep_supported` callback-presence tables so all four state the same `k_clock` struct: only `clock_realtime` carries `.clock_adj` (`posix_clock_realtime_adj` -> `do_adjtimex`), and the CLOCKFD dynamic clocks are EINVAL until a `posix_clock` character device is registered, mirroring the existing `getres_ns` treatment. CLOCK_TAI is EOPNOTSUPP here on purpose -- `clock_tai` has no `.clock_adj` in Linux, and its offset is disciplined through CLOCK_REALTIME's `ADJ_TAI`. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls` timex_policy 16, including the EOPNOTSUPP sweep over every non-realtime kclock and the copy-back asymmetry; `cargo test -p sched` clockid callback-presence table extended with `adjustable`. Both arches build. |
| 306 | common | `syncfs` | `sys_syncfs` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/162_sync.rs:34; crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:98` | `IMPL` | `F756-compat-mm-fs`; `B1462-durable-write-path` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/sync.c` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F756 audit: never compat-shadowed, and EBADF ordering was already correct. Fixed a cross-filesystem defect — the handler unconditionally ran `ext4::commit_rootfs_journal()` and mapped its failure to EIO, so `syncfs(2)` on a tmpfs or procfs fd committed, and could fail on, a filesystem the caller never named. ext4's own `SuperOps::sync_fs` already calls `commit_batch()` for every ext4 mount, so the one `sync_filesystem` call is the whole job; the superblock now comes from the fd's inode (Linux `f_path.dentry->d_sb`) rather than its mount, so an fd whose mount lookup fails still syncs its own superblock. Held PARTIAL for the remaining gap this row already named: there is no per-superblock `s_wb_err` errseq latch, so a writeback failure recorded between this fd's open and this call is not reported the way `errseq_check_and_advance` reports it. B1462: `syncfs` now harvests the superblock `errseq_t` (`s_wb_err` vs the per-description `f_sb_err` sampled at open) per `fs/sync.c:162` — a writeback error that happened since open is reported exactly once even when the failing inode has been evicted. The `f_sb_err` snapshot is separate from `f_wb_err` so `fsync` and `syncfs` advance independently. B1600 fixed a cross-filesystem bug: ext4's `sync_fs` called the process-wide `flush_all_dirty`, writing back every mount's frames and committing whichever mount happened to be first in the list — a different filesystem from the one the fd named AND from the batch its own `sync_fs` then ran. Now scoped to the caller's mount, with the unscoped variant retained for `msync` (which names no filesystem) and committing every mount it touched; the backend errno is no longer squashed to EIO. REMAINING: nothing except fsync and sync feeds `s_wb_err` — background writeback and inode eviction do not. Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. `F776` built the missing inode-metadata writeback path and found a durability bug under it: `wb_writeback` cleared `I_DIRTY` and dropped the writeback pin WITHOUT ever calling `s_op->write_inode`, so the sync path was a no-op that presented itself as durable. Also fixed: `sync_filesystem` ran the inode pass AFTER each `sync_fs`, writing inodes into a transaction that was never committed; and `iput_final` was INVERTED versus Linux, flushing metadata for exactly the deleted inodes and nothing else. `I_DIRTY_TIME` is now its own bit with `dirtied_time_when` as the expiry clock and `__mark_inode_dirty`'s supersede rule exact, and `SB_LAZYTIME` finally has a consumer through the single `writeback::inode_update_time` entry point. `syncfs` advances on the same mechanism. REMAINING unchanged as recorded above. Harness: `vfs --test lazytime_writeback` 13 (each forcing point READS THE TIMESTAMP BACK from a store reachable only via `i_op->update_time`/`s_op->write_inode`, not flag assertions) + `writeback_policy` 13, revert-proved both ways; `-p vfs -p fs -p ext4 -p syscalls` 4239/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 60s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1970 closes the stated remainder. The claim was half stale: the background writeback pass DID feed the filesystem-level error latch, but bypassed the per-inode latch, so an `fsync` on the failing inode returned success while `syncfs` reported the error. Inode eviction fed neither — the metadata write's result was discarded outright. Both now flow through a single choke point that latches inode and filesystem together, proved by 6 hosted tests whose positive controls (restore the eviction discard; restore the half-latch) go RED 2/6 and 5/6 respectively. |

F793 turns that clamp from a unit assertion into host-oracle evidence: a real `sendmmsg` of 1100 one-byte datagrams on this machine returns 1024, and the same 1100-entry batch through the real `socket::send_batch` returns 1024 — removing the clamp turns the corpus red. That is one case, not the row's differential: kernel-target ABI execution and security context remain unproven, so the row stays NEEDS-REWORK. B1942: the compat layout is closed the same way as row 46 — one owner, typed answer, `SendBatchIo` driven by it instead of by a flag it never received. Two claims in this row's Remaining list were STALE and are struck rather than worked: per-entry `MSG_BATCH` and the partial-send stop rule are implemented AND tested (`socket::batch::entry_flags` gives every non-final entry `MSG_BATCH` and the final one the caller's own flags; `send_batch` stops on an incomplete send), and the send side's signal/restart behaviour is likewise already pinned (`socket::tests::sendmmsg_returns_a_restart_error_before_any_completed_entry`, `sendmmsg_keeps_the_completed_prefix_when_a_later_entry_restarts`). One defect is left OPEN because it is outside this lane's files: `socket::send_batch` still rejects `MSG_CMSG_COMPAT` itself and re-declares the flag beside `net::uapi`'s — a second owner that would refuse a legitimate compat `sendmmsg` once a 32-bit entry exists (`scratch/issues.d/B1942-compat-msghdr-and-socket-restart.md`). Nothing sets `EntryAbi::Compat` yet: there is no 32-bit syscall entry on either arch, so the ABI is provable but not reachable from userspace. B1952 closes the send/receive security-hook gap named on rows 44/46/47/299/307. There was no single decision: the generic per-operation verdict was evaluated in nine places at four depths and the sandbox's address verdict in a tenth, and they disagreed about ordering — a send was refused only AFTER its family had answered ENODATA/EOPNOTSUPP/EINVAL, and a blocking receive re-asked its verdict once per wakeup rather than once per transaction. One boundary (`net::socket_security`) now composes sandbox then module registry and is the only place either is consulted for a message; `socket::security::admit` is the one send call site (top of `send::prepare`, ahead of every family validation, plus the two out-of-band envelope paths) and `syscalls::recv_admit::admit_and_route` the one receive call site — the protocol owner is obtainable ONLY from that call, so no receive can reach a protocol unadmitted. Per-family and per-transport copies deleted from `net::sock::sendto`, `net::sock_io::recvfrom_opts`, `recvmsg::{inet,netlink,vsock}`, `unix_recv`, and `socket::send`'s four inline sites. Two behaviour fixes fall out: a STREAM send naming an address no longer asks for the connect right (it settles no new port; asking denied sends a sandboxed program may make), and an out-of-band send with no urgent channel imports its envelope before the hook so unreadable address memory reports EFAULT, not a permission answer. The sandbox snapshot now travels in `SendContext` so one batch cannot be judged against two policies. Harness: `net::socket_security::tests` 6, `syscalls::recv_admit::tests` 4, `socket::security_hooks_tests` 5 (which replace three tests that asserted on the SOURCE TEXT of `send.rs`/`address.rs` and could not fail for any behavioural regression). Positive controls, each RED then green: deleting the call from `send::prepare` reddens all five send tests; deleting it from `admit_and_route` reddens two receive tests; restoring the transport-derived send right reddens the stream test; swapping the two modules' order reddens the ordering test. Both arch target checks pass; smoke-x86 PASS 129s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. One consequence needed its own fix: removing the transport's duplicate evaluation let the raw IPv4 branch inline into `net::sock::sendto`, whose frame every other protocol's send then carried — the aarch64 worst-case send path grew 480 B and crossed the 13000 B stack-depth ceiling. The branch and `socket::send::send_inet` are `#[inline(never)]` now and the deepest path is 12960 B (main: 12880 B). B1960 closes this row's last two items. Kernel-target ABI execution: `t_mmsg` now runs through the real syscall entry inside the guest, against a host oracle recorded from the system glibc on the same machine, and the two frames match verbatim on BOTH arches (`tools/oxide-conformance-serial.sh`, x86_64 and aarch64 PASS). That is the batch's whole rule set exercised where it actually runs — the 1024-entry cap, the completed prefix across a later-entry copy fault, the fd-before-vector ordering and the timeout-copyback rules — rather than only against the ungated batch model. The `MSG_CMSG_COMPAT` second owner named in B1942's note is gone from `socket::batch`. Linux/Oxide differential evidence: the sibling probe `t_cmsgfam` (rows 44/46) covers the per-family send answers a batch entry inherits, and its positive control — the same probe against the pre-branch kernel — fails on nine of sixteen lines, which is what proves the channel can report a difference at all. Promoted to `IMPL`. |
| 308 | common | `setns` | `sys_setns` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:56` | `IMPL` | `F755-process-misc-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/nsproxy.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | F755 added the install-time capability ladder that was entirely missing: every non-TIME namespace installed with NO permission check, so an unprivileged holder of a `/proc/<pid>/ns/*` fd walked straight in. `nscg::proc_ns::setns_perm::check_install` reproduces `utsns_install`/`ipcns_install`/`netns_install`/`cgroupns_install` (CAP_SYS_ADMIN in BOTH the target's owning user namespace and the caller's), `mntns_install` (plus CAP_SYS_CHROOT), `pidns_install` (descendant-of-active-pidns else EINVAL, capabilities first) and `userns_install` (same-namespace EINVAL, multi-threaded EINVAL, CAP_SYS_ADMIN in the target), and runs before any task slot is touched. Each kind verified to genuinely swap the task's namespace slot rather than record a flag. The pidfd form is now implemented (`308_setns/from_pidfd.rs`): `check_setns_flags` mask, `ptrace_may_access(PTRACE_MODE_READ_REALCREDS)`, target snapshot, then Linux's validate-the-whole-set-before-committing-any order, user namespace first. 15 hosted tests (`setns_perm_tests.rs`, `setns_flags.rs`). `B1967` re-read the one claimed remainder and found it already closed: `mntns_install` resolves `/` inside the namespace it just installed and sets BOTH the task's root and its cwd from it, reverting the install when the new root cannot be resolved rather than leaving the task straddling two trees — `commit_nsset`'s `set_fs_root`/`set_fs_pwd` arm, function for function. Both entry points reach it: the nsfs-fd form through `setns_apply`, and the pidfd form through the same call in its commit phase, so `CLONE_NEWNS` re-roots identically either way. Nothing remains. Harness: `cargo test -p sched -p syscalls -p syscall -p fs -p nscg -p pidfd` 4564/0; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 143s, smoke-arm PASS 181s. |

| 314 | common | `sched_setattr` | `sys_sched_setattr` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_c.rs:136` | `IMPL` | `F762-schedattr-ioprio-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/fork.c, kernel/exit.c, kernel/sys.c, kernel/sched/core.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | F762 rebuilds 314 on the real extensible-struct ABI (`sched_copy_attr` + `copy_struct_from_user`). Previously it read a fixed 48-byte prefix, reported 48 as the kernel struct size, ignored the trailing-byte protocol entirely, and rejected every flag except RESET_ON_FORK/KEEP_POLICY. Now: `sizeof(struct sched_attr)` is 56 (VER1), a short struct zero-fills, an oversized struct is E2BIG only when its unknown tail is non-zero, `err_size` writes 56 back through `uattr->size`, `SCHED_FLAG_UTIL_CLAMP` with `size < SCHED_ATTR_SIZE_VER1` is EINVAL, `sched_nice` is CLAMPED not rejected, the flag mask is `~(SCHED_FLAG_ALL` plus `SCHED_FLAG_SUGOV)` with SUGOV then refused for a user caller, `SCHED_FLAG_KEEP_PARAMS` reads the task's own params back and commits nothing but reset_on_fork, util-clamp requests are validated (`uclamp_validate`, including Linux's signed `-1` sentinel arithmetic) and stored per-task with the `user_defined` reset semantics, `sched_runtime` becomes a clamped custom CFS slice (`__setparam_fair`), `__checkparam_dl` gained the DL_SCALE/MSB/period-bound rules, and the no-change fast path matches. ABI rules in `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/sched_attr.rs` (21 hosted tests) + `sched_policy/{task,setattr}.rs` (12 more). Remaining: SCHED_DEADLINE is refused with EOPNOTSUPP after validation and permission because there is no deadline scheduling class. B1587 found no ABI gap. REMAINING: no SCHED_DEADLINE scheduling class, so the deadline policy is validated and then refused. Harness: ungated `sched_enc/{wakeup,requeue}.rs` and `syscalls::priority_target`; `cargo test -p sched --lib` 789/0 (697 before), `-p syscalls --lib` 873/0, `-p block --lib` 47/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 62s, smoke-arm PASS 86s. Closed in `F774`, which BUILT the class: `sched_setattr` had validated a deadline request in full and then answered EOPNOTSUPP — there was no EDF ready set, no budget, no admission control, no throttle, no replenishment. Now `sched/src/deadline/` (params/cbs/bw/entity/clock/replenish/live) plus the EDF set in `sched/src/dl.rs`. CBS charges budget from a per-stint monotonic stamp at schedule-out AND tick (so a task blocking between ticks is still accounted); `runtime <= 0` throttles; overrun is kept as signed debt and replenish trades one period per budget, so an N-budget overrun costs N postponed deadlines; wakeup takes original-CBS restart or the revised laxity-scaled rule. Admission is a fixed-point ledger comparing undivided `total_bw` against a per-CPU cap scaled by the LIVE online-CPU capacity — one source of truth — returning EBUSY after every argument and permission answer, with a re-issue or shrink judged against the bandwidth already held so it can never spuriously EBUSY. Deadline outranks RT outranks fair, compared wrap-safe and STRICT so an equal deadline neither preempts nor reorders. A throttled entity leaves every tree at one chokepoint and parks on a replenishment queue folded into the one-shot timer, requeued through the DEFERRED wake path because the timer IRQ must not spin on the rq lock. A DL parent's fork is EAGAIN, with `SCHED_RESET_ON_FORK` the supported route. Precedence is EINVAL on parameters, EPERM for unprivileged or below-span, EBUSY on capacity — in that order. `SCHED_FLAG_RECLAIM`/`DL_OVERRUN` round-trip, and an overrun raises SIGXCPU through the existing process-directed timer-signal enqueue. REMAINING (named, not faked): `SCHED_FLAG_RECLAIM` charges at the plain rate because the per-rq `running_bw`/`this_bw` utilization it scales against is not yet maintained; `DlBw::fits` (the shrinking-CPU-set rule) is hosted-tested but has no caller because oxide has no CPU-offline path. Harness: `cargo test -p sched --lib` 1021/0 (was 789; +90 in `deadline::*`), `-p syscalls --lib` 986/0, `-p procfs --lib` 124/0; the throttle is PROVEN not asserted — `a_task_that_overruns_its_budget_is_thrown_off_the_ready_set` runs a real task on a real `RunqueueInner` against a settable clock and shows the CPU idling rather than overrunning. smoke-x86 PASS 282s, smoke-arm PASS 378s; stack-gate PASS both arches. |

Now `sched/src/deadline/` (params/cbs/bw/entity/clock/replenish/live) plus the EDF set in `sched/src/dl.rs`. CBS charges budget from a per-stint monotonic stamp at schedule-out AND tick (so a task blocking between ticks is still accounted); `runtime <= 0` throttles; overrun is kept as signed debt and replenish trades one period per budget, so an N-budget overrun costs N postponed deadlines; wakeup takes original-CBS restart or the revised laxity-scaled rule. Admission is a fixed-point ledger comparing undivided `total_bw` against a per-CPU cap scaled by the LIVE online-CPU capacity — one source of truth — returning EBUSY after every argument and permission answer, with a re-issue or shrink judged against the bandwidth already held so it can never spuriously EBUSY. Deadline outranks RT outranks fair, compared wrap-safe and STRICT so an equal deadline neither preempts nor reorders. A throttled entity leaves every tree at one chokepoint and parks on a replenishment queue folded into the one-shot timer, requeued through the DEFERRED wake path because the timer IRQ must not spin on the rq lock. A DL parent's fork is EAGAIN, with `SCHED_RESET_ON_FORK` the supported route. `SCHED_GETATTR_FLAG_DL_DYNAMIC` now has reachable state and reports the live instance — remaining budget and absolute deadline — as distinct from the reservation. | 316 | common | `renameat2` | `sys_renameat2` | vfs | dcache, fd-table, fs-backend, inode, mntns, permissions, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/316_renameat2.rs` | `IMPL` | `F740-rename-family-audit` | `fs/namei.c:filename_renameat2`, `fs/namei.c:__start_renaming`, `fs/namei.c:lock_two_directories`, `fs/namei.c:vfs_rename`, `fs/namei.c:may_delete_dentry`, `fs/namei.c:may_create_dentry`, `fs/namei.c:lookup_one_qstr_excl`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_rename2`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_rename`, `fs/ext4/namei.c:ext4_cross_rename`, `mm/shmem.c:shmem_rename2`, `fs/libfs.c:simple_rename_exchange`, `fs/kernfs/dir.c:kernfs_iop_rename` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | Rebuilt as `filename_renameat2` step for step, with the order-sensitive decisions in the ungated `syscalls/src/rename_policy.rs` so they are unit-testable: flags validated before either pathname is fetched (`RENAME_NOREPLACE\|RENAME_WHITEOUT` legal, EXCHANGE conflicts with both); old parent resolved before the new pathname is read; EXDEV before the LAST_NORM tests; non-`LAST_NORM` old side EBUSY, new side EBUSY except EEXIST under NOREPLACE (Linux's `error = -EEXIST` assignment sits between the two tests); EROFS via the owning mount; source-missing ENOENT, EXCHANGE-needs-both ENOENT, NOREPLACE EEXIST; the `lock_rename` ancestor TRAP split — source-is-ancestor EINVAL, target-is-ancestor ENOTEMPTY (EINVAL under EXCHANGE) — which had no counterpart before; trailing-slash ENOTDIR; same-inode no-op 0 after those gates; mountpoint EBUSY via `d_mountpoint`; then Landlock and `may_rename`. Backends: ext4 gained the `ext4_empty_dir` ENOTEMPTY gate (a populated directory victim previously went straight to `Mount::rmdir`, whose contract is caller-verified-empty, freeing the destination subtree's blocks and inode with its children still linked), the cross-parent `..` repoint plus both parents' `i_links_count` fixups on plain rename AND on EXCHANGE's mixed pair, the `EXT4_LINK_MAX` EMLINK ceiling, and the four `ext4_rename` timestamp stamps; the three path-based ext4 entry points now share one core with `i_op->rename` instead of three divergent copies. tmpfs gained the same ENOTEMPTY gate, `simple_rename`/`simple_rename_exchange` parent-link accounting, and EINVAL for flags outside the three shmem implements. kernfs now answers ANY renameat2 flag with EINVAL (`kernfs_iop_rename`'s `if (flags) return -EINVAL`) instead of honouring NOREPLACE/EXCHANGE and dropping WHITEOUT. A filesystem with no `->rename` slot now answers EPERM, not EROFS. Remaining for IMPL: `IS_SWAPFILE` EPERM, `HAS_UNMAPPED_ID`/invalid-vfsuid EOVERFLOW and `IS_DEADDIR` ENOENT inside `may_delete`/`may_create`, and the ESTALE `LOOKUP_REVAL` retry loop. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --test rename_ladder_oracle` (7 differential cases run the REAL `renameat2(2)` on the host kernel and require the oxide ladder to return the identical errno); `cargo test -p syscalls --lib rename_policy` (15); `cargo test -p ext4 --test rename_nonempty_victim_image --test rename_dotdot_image --test rename_overwrite_image --test rename_exchange_image` (14); `cargo test -p fs --lib tmpfs::tests::rename` (7); `cargo run -p xtask -- kernel --arch x86_64` and `--arch aarch64`. Closed in `B1597`, which completed the shared gates (`vfs/namei/may_{create,delete,link,rename}.rs`, split out of an over-cap `permission.rs`): `may_create` was missing dead-directory ENOENT and unrepresentable-caller EOVERFLOW ahead of the DAC check; `may_delete` was missing `IS_SWAPFILE` EPERM, `HAS_UNMAPPED_ID`/invalid-vfsuid EOVERFLOW, `IS_DEADDIR` ENOENT and `IS_ROOT` EBUSY; and the `i_op` default for the mutating ops was EROFS where Linux uses EPERM (EROFS is the read-only-MOUNT verdict, decided a layer up). Order-sensitive decisions moved to ungated `syscalls::path_ops_policy` since the slot files are target-gated. Harness: `syscalls --test path_ops_ladder_oracle` 7 (DIFFERENTIAL against the real host kernel), `--test path_ops_policy` 11, `vfs --test may_gate_ladder` 8, `namei_hardlink_perm` 11, `ext4 --lib links` 4; `-p vfs -p ext4 -p fs -p syscalls -p ipc` 4282/0; both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 105s, smoke-arm PASS 78s. |
| 317 | common | `seccomp` | `sys_seccomp` | security | credentials, permissions, security, syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:85` | `IMPL` | `B1478-security-enforcement`, `B1969` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/sys.c, kernel/cred.c, security/` | `cred-security-model` | B1478 made RET_TRACE fail closed (Linux ENOSYS-and-skip, NOT kill), added the no_new_privs/CAP_SYS_ADMIN install gate, TSYNC, the cBPF verifier, RET_TRAP _sigsys siginfo, the real instruction_pointer, and fork seccomp_mode inheritance; KILL_PROCESS no longer folds onto KILL_THREAD (SECCOMP_RET_ACTION_FULL). Also fixed a BPF_RVAL/BPF_SRC decode bug that turned every `return A` into KILL_THREAD. B1969 built the user-notification transport that was the last gap: SECCOMP_FILTER_FLAG_NEW_LISTENER returns a real listener fd (anon inode, O_CLOEXEC, one listener per chain or EBUSY), carrying NOTIF_RECV (all-zero buffer check, EFAULT re-queues), NOTIF_SEND (one reply, EINPROGRESS otherwise, CONTINUE excludes val/error), NOTIF_ID_VALID (both the current and the wrong-direction encoding), NOTIF_SET_FLAGS, and the extensible-argument NOTIF_ADDFD with SETFD/SEND, EBUSY on a queued injection and ESRCH when the target leaves. RET_USER_NOTIF now blocks on the supervisor and returns its verdict (CONTINUE dispatches the call, else error-then-value), WAIT_KILLABLE_RECV narrows the wait once a supervisor holds the notification, poll reports IN/OUT/HUP, and last close of the listener releases every waiter with ENOSYS. Injection is performed BY the notified task in its own context, so nothing writes another task's descriptor table. The filter records the listener ID so TSYNC'd threads and forked children reach the same supervisor; the chain walk now runs newest-first and reports the matching filter, so a tie goes to the most recently installed one. 62 hosted tests in ungated modules; ioctl numbers and structure sizes verified against the running reference. |
| 318 | common | `getrandom` | `sys_getrandom` | syscall-abi | syscall-abi, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:35` | `IMPL` | `F755-process-misc-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl` | `syscall-abi-model` | Audited against `drivers/char/random.c`. The generator was a linear-congruential PRNG whose whole state recovers from two consecutive outputs, so `getrandom(2)`, `/dev/random`, `/dev/urandom`, AT_RANDOM (glibc stack canary and pointer guard) and UUIDs were all predictable. Replaced by the new `crng` crate: ChaCha20 with fast key erasure, Linux's `crng_fast_key_erasure` construction, seeded from virtio-rng plus RDRAND/RNDR plus cycle-counter jitter and reseeded when a hardware source attaches. | 326 | common | `copy_file_range` | `sys_copy_file_range` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:85` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/326_copy_file_range.rs` -> `crates/kernel/fs/src/splice/copy_range.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE6(copy_file_range), fs/read_write.c:vfs_copy_file_range, fs/read_write.c:generic_copy_file_checks, fs/read_write.c:generic_file_rw_checks, fs/read_write.c:generic_write_check_limits, fs/splice.c:do_splice_direct, include/linux/fs.h:MAX_RW_COUNT` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. The pre-fix handler was a bare read/write loop with NO admission checks whatsoever, and it `seek`ed BOTH descriptions instead of using the explicit offsets. FIXED, in Linux order (fs/read_write.c:1482-1546): directory on either side -> `EISDIR` before the non-regular `EINVAL`; `FMODE_READ`/`FMODE_WRITE` and `O_APPEND`-on-output -> `EBADF` (deliberately a different errno from `splice`, which reports `EINVAL` for the same `O_APPEND` condition); cross-filesystem -> `EXDEV`; immutable output -> `EPERM`; offset+count wrap -> `EOVERFLOW` (not `EINVAL`); the EOF clamp; `RLIMIT_FSIZE` clamp then `EFBIG`; and LAST the same-inode overlap `EINVAL` computed on the ALREADY-CLAMPED count, so a copy whose tail is clipped to EOF stops overlapping and becomes legal. `flags != 0` -> `EINVAL`. Offsets are read from the user pointers, advanced by exactly the bytes copied, and written back only when bytes moved; a NULL pointer means the description cursor is used and advanced. The short-copy contract is honoured literally: the return value is the count actually written, never the count requested. The same-filesystem test uses `Inode::fsid()` - oxide canonical filesystem identity, the same value `st_dev` reports - rather than the `Arc<SuperBlock>` pointer, because an anon/memfd regular file has no superblock and would otherwise report `EXDEV` against itself. `ETXTBSY` is wired but unreachable by construction: oxide `swapon(2)` admits only block devices, so no inode carries a swapfile flag. Transfer uses positional `pread`/`pwrite`, so neither description cursor moves when explicit offsets are supplied - matching `do_splice_direct` in the no-backend-op case, which is the fallback every filesystem without `->copy_file_range` takes. Evidence: `cargo test -p fs --lib splice::copy_range` (7 ladder tests) + `cargo test -p fs --test file_io_range_model` (7 tests over a REAL tmpfs: copy+offset advance, short-at-EOF, nonzero-flags EINVAL, same-file overlap EINVAL with a byte-for-byte assertion that the file is untouched, RLIMIT_FSIZE clamp then EFBIG, the FMODE/type/O_APPEND gates, cross-filesystem EXDEV); both arch kernel builds green. |
| 327 | 64 | `preadv2` | `sys_preadv2` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:60` -> `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/295_preadv.rs` + `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/rwf.rs` | `IMPL` | `F754-file-io-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE6(preadv2), fs/read_write.c:do_preadv, fs/read_write.c:do_readv, include/linux/fs.h:kiocb_set_rw_flags, include/uapi/linux/fs.h:RWF_SUPPORTED, mm/filemap.c:filemap_read` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F754 audit against local Linux source. Inherits every `preadv` fix (including the x86_64 compat-offset bug). Additionally FIXED: (a) the `pos == -1` escape to current-offset semantics (fs/read_write.c:1189) did not exist - `preadv2(fd, iov, n, -1, 0, flags)` returned `EINVAL` instead of behaving as `readv`; it now hands off to the cursor-advancing vectored read after validating the flag word. (b) `RWF_SUPPORTED` was `0x1f`; this tree defines nine flags (`0x1ff`), so `RWF_NOAPPEND`, `RWF_ATOMIC`, `RWF_DONTCACHE` and `RWF_NOSIGNAL` were unreachable. (c) The full `kiocb_set_rw_flags` ladder now runs in Linux order: a zero flags word short-circuits, unsupported bits -> `EOPNOTSUPP`, `RWF_APPEND` together with `RWF_NOAPPEND` together -> `EINVAL`, `RWF_ATOMIC` is write-only and needs `FMODE_CAN_ATOMIC_WRITE`, `RWF_DONTCACHE` needs `FOP_DONTCACHE`, `RWF_SYNC` implies `IOCB_DSYNC`, and clearing an inherited `O_APPEND` with `RWF_NOAPPEND` on an append-only INODE is `EPERM`. (d) `RWF_NOWAIT` was accepted and then BLOCKED, which is precisely the failure it exists to prevent. A new `FMODE_NOWAIT` is claimed only by a regular file with a page-cache address space - the case where `mincore_page` can answer "is this byte available without I/O" - and `File::pread_nowait` returns `EAGAIN` when the first needed page below EOF is not resident, mirroring the enforcement points in `filemap_read`; a description that cannot answer honestly reports `EOPNOTSUPP`, which is what Linux does for a filesystem that has not opted in. (e) The flag admission runs AFTER the iovec import, so a zero-length `preadv2` returns 0 even with an unsupported flag, matching `vfs_readv` returning at `!tot_len` before `do_iter_readv_writev`. `RWF_HIPRI` is a no-op on a buffered read in Linux too and needs no `O_DIRECT`. Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib rwf` (6 tests covering the flag set, the capability gates, the APPEND/NOAPPEND matrix incl. the EPERM case, and the `pos == -1` classification for both the `2` and non-`2` variants); both arch kernel builds green. |
| 328 | 64 | `pwritev2` | `sys_pwritev2` | vfs | fd-table, fs-backend, inode, page-cache, poll, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:61` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity`; `B1462-durable-write-path` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/read_write.c, fs/open.c` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | Ledger D1/B632: pwritev/pwritev2 now perform positional writes using split offset and do not mutate file position; remaining full-row audit covers iovec fault ordering and per-filesystem writeback cases. B1462: `RWF_SYNC`/`RWF_DSYNC` FIXED — `kiocb_set_rw_flags` computed `IOCB_SYNC`/`IOCB_DSYNC` and the result was discarded (the `RwEffect` was mapped away), so `pwritev2(..., RWF_SYNC)` behaved exactly like `pwritev`. The effect now drives `generic_write_sync` over the written range after the write, and a sync failure replaces the byte count with -errno per `include/linux/fs.h:2663-2676`. B1970 closes the stated remainders. Iovec fault ordering is fixed with the shared importer described in row 296 — the whole vector is validated before any byte moves. Two admitted-then-discarded flags now take effect: the force-append flag was validated and then ignored, so a caller asking for an append still wrote at the supplied offset; and the never-wait flag was admitted and then ignored, so a caller that asked never to wait waited — a buffered write now reports EOPNOTSUPP, which is what a write path that has not opted in reports. The current-offset form (`pos == -1`) dropped both the flag admission and the durability step and went straight to the cursor-advancing write; it now runs both. A single per-operation write entry point in the VFS owns the append/nowait decisions, so the positional gate ladder exists once instead of twice. |

FIXED: (a) the unknown-flag `EINVAL` was applied on every call, but Linux performs it only in `vfs_statx` on the PATH entry - an `AT_EMPTY_PATH` + empty-name + `dfd >= 0` call takes `do_statx_fd` (fs/stat.c:809-812), which has no flag check and silently strips `AT_NO_AUTOMOUNT`; the `fstat` emulation therefore used to fail for any caller passing a newer `AT_` bit. (b) Validation ORDER now matches fs/stat.c:750-753 - reserved mask bit, then the both-bits `AT_STATX_SYNC_TYPE`, then (path entry only) unknown flags. (c) `STATX_CHANGE_COOKIE` is stripped from the request mask on entry and from `stx_mask` on exit. (d) `STATX_MNT_ID_UNIQUE` is honoured and is mutually exclusive with `STATX_MNT_ID` in the result mask (fs/stat.c:303-309); oxide `NEXT_MNT_ID` is monotonic and never recycled, so one value satisfies both ids. (e) `SB_NOATIME` now clears `STATX_ATIME` from `result_mask` (fs/stat.c:193-194) and `vfs_getattr` advertises `STATX_ATTR_AUTOMOUNT`/`STATX_ATTR_DAX` in `stx_attributes_mask` with `STATX_ATTR_AUTOMOUNT` set for a real mount trigger. ALREADY CORRECT before this pass: the 256-byte wire layout and all field offsets, the mode/rdev/dev encoding, `STATX_ATTR_MOUNT_ROOT`, and the `stx_btime` contract (the bit stays clear when the backend has no creation time, so no birth time is fabricated). GAP (reason for PARTIAL): the optional `STATX_DIOALIGN`, `STATX_SUBVOL`, `STATX_WRITE_ATOMIC` and `STATX_DIO_READ_ALIGN` attributes are left clear and their fields zero, where Linux ext4 fills the DIO alignment pair when asked (fs/ext4/inode.c:6214-6228); the ABI encoder has the offsets reserved for them. `AT_STATX_FORCE_SYNC`/`DONT_SYNC` are accepted and reach `i_op->getattr` unchanged, which is the local-filesystem behaviour (only network filesystems act on them). Evidence: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib statx_abi` (7 tests: wire offsets and size, field round-trip with every spare byte asserted zero, result-mask-is-what-was-filled, mnt-id exclusivity, attribute-mask containment, the validation ladder incl. the fd/path asymmetry, entry selection); both arch kernel builds green. B1600: tmpfs now supplies `stx_btime` (every /tmp, /run and /dev/shm inode reported none); the wire layout is verified byte-identical on both arches. REMAINING: `STATX_DIOALIGN`/`SUBVOL`/`WRITE_ATOMIC`/`DIO_READ_ALIGN` are unimplementable as-is because `InodeOps::getattr` has no `request_mask`/`query_flags` parameters; `AT_STATX_SYNC_TYPE` is validated and then never reaches any backend, costing a full `FUSE_GETATTR` round trip on every `AT_STATX_DONT_SYNC` stat; ext4 getattr omits `STATX_ATTR_COMPRESSED/ENCRYPTED/NODUMP/VERITY`. Harness: workspace 544 suites ok (only the pre-existing parallel-flaky set, baseline confirmed by a stashed run); `hal siginfo` 8, `vfs file_fowner` 17, `file_lease_break` 6, `file_readahead_drives_read` 7, `ext4 getdents_dtype_image` 6, `fs getdents_dots_tmpfs` 7; both arch checks clean; smoke-x86 PASS 102s, smoke-arm PASS 76s. B1970 closes all three stated remainders. The attribute op gained the request mask and query flags it lacked, which was the stated blocker for every request-gated field: the sync-type selector now REACHES the backend (it was validated and then dropped, so an answer-from-cache stat still paid a full attribute round trip on a network-backed filesystem), and ext4 fills the direct-I/O alignment pair when it is asked for, request-gated, with the encoder writing the two wire words only when the backend filled them. ext4 also reported only the two attribute bits the generic inode flag word happens to mirror — compressed, encrypted, nodump and verity were invisible and the attributes MASK was never widened, so a reader could not distinguish `not compressed` from `cannot tell`; the on-disk flag word is now translated through a single-owner module that also replaced four independent copies of the ext4 flag constants. The three remaining optional attributes describe device and volume features this tree has none of (subvolumes, atomic writes, a separate direct-read alignment), so leaving them clear is the same answer the reference gives on a filesystem without them. Evidence: `-p ext4 --lib inode::flags` 3 and `inode::dio` 3 (positive controls RED 2/3 and 3/3), `-p syscalls --lib statx_abi` 10 (was 9; the new wire test RED when the alignment pair is written unconditionally). |
| 333 | common | `io_pgetevents` | `sys_io_pgetevents` | vfs | async-io, fd-table, mm-uaccess, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:83` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/aio.c, io_uring/` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | Ledger F3/B643: libaio context registry, submit, getevents, cancel, destroy, vectored offsets, and RESFD signaling are wired; remaining full-row audit covers async completion timing and every iocb opcode/error ordering. Closed in `B1574`: the previous implementation was a kernel-side `VecDeque` behind an opaque integer cookie, but in Linux `aio_context_t` IS the address the completion ring is mapped at — libaio dereferences it as `struct aio_ring *` and reaps without a syscall, so the old code was unusable by libaio and `io_cancel`/blocking `io_getevents`/`io_pgetevents` sigmask were self-declared no-ops. Now: a contiguous refcounted run seeded with magic/compat/incompat/header_length/nr and mapped MAP_SHARED, whose address is the context id; per-CPU floor -> doubling -> 256 MiB EINVAL cap -> EAGAIN -> page rounding with `fs.aio-max-nr` accounting; the full `io_submit_one` ladder (reserved2, signed nbytes, ring-slot EAGAIN, EBADF, `aio_resfd` must be a real eventfd, `KIOCB_KEY` stamp) over PREAD/PWRITE/PREADV/PWRITEV/FSYNC/FDSYNC/POLL with NOOP=EINVAL, IOPRIO and `RWF_*` admission AND effect, partial-submit counting; blocking `min_nr` reap with aio's own timeout rules and EINTR/ERESTARTNOHAND; `io_pgetevents` `__aio_sigset`; `io_cancel` EINPROGRESS/EINVAL which also still DELIVERS the cancelled poll's `io_event` with `res = 0`; `io_destroy` resolve+unmap+uncharge. `IOCB_CMD_POLL` completes from the poll WAKEUP (keyed `EpollNotify::notify_events`) and signals `aio_resfd` without any reap, with an eventfd-signals-itself guard. `VmaBacking::KernelFrame` now offsets by VMA offset. Harness: `cargo test -p syscalls --lib` 838/0 with 55 ungated `aio_abi` tests, including `const _: () = assert!(offset_of!(..) == ..)` layout proofs that are EVALUATED on both targets (verified non-vacuous: perturbing an offset fails the aarch64 build); the old `aio/tests.rs` was in a `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]` file and had never executed once. Both arches build; smoke-x86 PASS 59s, smoke-arm PASS 94s; `userspace/aio_probe` PASS in-guest on x86_64 AND against a real Linux 6.19 host, asserting a POLL+eventfd completion with no intervening `io_getevents`. REMAINING: no in-guest aarch64 probe run — a harness gap (the ARM lite image has no interpreter, and the GRUB cmdline does not reach systemd on the aarch64 EFI-stub path), not a code gap. |
| 334 | common | `rseq` | `sys_rseq` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:53` | `IMPL` | `F755-process-misc-audit` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, kernel/fork.c, kernel/exit.c, kernel/sys.c, kernel/sched/core.c` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Audited against `kernel/rseq.c` + `include/linux/rseq_entry.h`. Registration was cosmetic: the kernel stored the pointer and wrote a hardcoded cpu_id of 0, and performed NO critical-section abort, so glibc/jemalloc/tcmalloc took per-cpu fast paths the kernel was not protecting on an SMP system. F755 implements the real thing: `rseq_preempt_return` runs on the IRQ-exit-to-user tail of BOTH arches after a preemption, reads `rseq_cs`, and — when the interrupted PC lies inside the declared section — clears the descriptor and restarts user execution at `abort_ip`, after checking the four signature bytes below `abort_ip` (the gate that stops a writable `rseq_cs` redirecting the abort at a gadget); a bad descriptor is fatal, as Linux's `force_sigsegv`. Real per-CPU `cpu_id`/`cpu_id_start`/`mm_cid` writeback replaces the hardcoded 0, cached so an exit that did not change CPU costs no user write. Full errno ladder (`EINVAL`/`EPERM`/`EBUSY` ordering, alignment and length rules, unregister flag mask) in the hosted-tested `syscall::rseq`; registration initialises the user area as `rseq_register` does; `rseq_fork` inheritance across a non-CLONE_VM clone added. All user access goes through the exception-table-backed `uaccess` helpers. 17 hosted tests. |

Also fixed a real cross-arch mis-dispatch: `asm-generic/unistd.h` reserves 295..402 ("unassigned … don't use") and 403..423 are 32-bit-only, so arm64 has no slot 335/336, yet `arm_abi`'s pass-through sent an aarch64 `syscall(335)` into x86's `sys_uretprobe` (SIGILL) and `syscall(300)` into x86's `fanotify_mark` — same class as nfsservctl-on-connect. Those numbers now ENOSYS as on arm64 Linux (test `arm_unassigned_numbers_do_not_reach_x86_only_syscalls`). Closed in B1972: this slot latched a group exit directly, so the status the parent reaped carried the core-dumped bit -- SIGILL's default action dumps core -- while no core was written, and no signal-delivery stop reached a tracer. It now forces the signal and returns, leaving the default-action triage, the core dump and the tracer stop to the ordinary return-to-user path, which is the reference's shape. The outcome each injected slot owes a caller arriving outside a trampoline moved into ungated `syscalls::uprobe_abi`: both slot files are kernel-gated, so a test block inside either compiles out silently, and the two slots' answers differ in a way a userspace feature probe reads directly. A kernel that maps no uprobe trampoline can only ever reach that arm, so it is the whole reachable body and the row is complete as a syscall ABI; the tracing subsystem that would map a trampoline is tracked as its own row in `scratch/issues.d/B1972-syscall-abi-partial-rows.md`, since it is a subsystem row rather than an ABI one. Harness: `syscalls uprobe_abi` 6 tests. Positive controls -- 335 answering as an errno slot turns 3 RED, 336 drifting to ENOSYS turns 2 RED, restored GREEN; breaking the gated slot makes the x86_64 target check exit 101 naming that file, so the gated route is covered too. Both arch target checks clean. |

| 425 | common | `io_uring_setup` | `sys_io_uring_setup` | vfs | async-io, fd-table, mm-uaccess, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:76` | `PARTIAL` | `F766-perf-bpf-uprobe-iouring-audit`; `B1950-io-uring-setup-register-surface` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/aio.c, io_uring/` | `fd-vfs-io-model` | F766: `cq_off` was written at `params+72`; `struct io_sqring_offsets` is 40 bytes so it belongs at 80 — the CQ head/tail landed inside `sq_off.array`/`resv1`/`user_addr`. `params == 0` silently skipped the writeback and still returned a working fd where `io_uring_setup` EFAULTs, and the writeback used raw `write_volatile` behind a bare `< USER_VA_END` check instead of `copy_to_user`. `p->flags`/`p->resv` were never read, so any `IORING_SETUP_*` was accepted and ignored (an SQPOLL ring got no poll thread); `io_uring_sanitise_params` + `io_uring_fill_params` are now ported whole, with CLAMP/CQSIZE, and unimplemented flags report EINVAL rather than being dropped. `features` was 0, which makes liburing mmap the CQ ring against the wrong base. The single ring page overlapped itself (SQ index array ran into the CQ header; the SQE array ran 2 KiB past the frame) and `mmap(IORING_OFF_SQES)` returned the rings page, so userspace wrote SQEs the kernel never read; split into rings + SQEs regions per `io_allocate_scq_urings`, both `VmaBacking::KernelFrame` (B1342 re-audit clean, no `PhysRange`). 40 hosted tests, non-gated abi module; both arches boot-smoked. IOPOLL, SQPOLL, SQ_AFF, ATTACH_WQ, SQE128, CQE32, NO_MMAP, REGISTERED_FD_ONLY, HYBRID_IOPOLL, CQE_MIXED, SQE_MIXED and SQ_REWIND are validated-and-refused, not implemented. B1950: the `io_uring_allowed()` gate is live — `kernel.io_uring_disabled` was an inert procfs cell and `kernel.io_uring_group` did not exist; both now bind to one owner read before the params copy, so level 2 refuses every caller (root included) and level 1 admits CAP_SYS_ADMIN or a group member, EPERM otherwise. R_DISABLED, SINGLE_ISSUER (enforced: a second submitter gets EEXIST), COOP_TASKRUN, TASKRUN_FLAG and DEFER_TASKRUN are implemented, not refused. Features now claimed because implemented: NODROP (overflow backlog), RW_CUR_POS, CUR_PERSONALITY, EXT_ARG, RSRC_TAGS, CQE_SKIP, LINKED_FILE. STILL REMAINING: `MAX_ENTRIES` 64 (single-frame regions); IOPOLL, SQPOLL, SQ_AFF, ATTACH_WQ, SQE128, CQE32, NO_MMAP, REGISTERED_FD_ONLY, HYBRID_IOPOLL, CQE_MIXED, SQE_MIXED, SQ_REWIND refused (need multi-frame regions, a poll thread or a worker pool); FAST_POLL/NATIVE_WORKERS/POLL_32BITS/SQPOLL_NONFIXED/REG_REG_RING not claimed. |
| 426 | common | `io_uring_enter` | `sys_io_uring_enter` | vfs | async-io, fd-table, mm-uaccess, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_a.rs:77` | `PARTIAL` | `B713-linux-path-identity; B852-socket-atomic-cloexec`; `B1950-io-uring-setup-register-surface` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl; include/uapi/linux/io_uring.h:io_uring_sqe; io_uring/net.c:io_accept` | `syscalls::io_uring_sqe; syscalls::socket_fd; cargo test -p syscalls --lib; x86_64/aarch64 syscalls target checks` | B852 fixes `IORING_OP_ACCEPT`: decoder retains `op_flags@28`, maps SQE `addr` and `addr2` to `sockaddr *` and `socklen_t *`, passes `accept_flags` through `accept4`, preserves `SOCK_NONBLOCK` as file status, and atomically publishes `SOCK_CLOEXEC` as descriptor state. Remaining: full `io_uring_enter` flag, signal-mask, submission/completion, cancellation, linked-op, registered-resource, multishot, and concurrency audit. B1950: `io_uring_enter` honoured NONE of its flags, signal mask or timeout — every argument past `to_submit` was discarded. Now: unknown flags are EINVAL; GETEVENTS waits for `min_complete` (clamped to the CQ depth) on the ring's own wait list; both extended-argument forms decode (`io_uring_getevents_arg`, and the registered-wait record, which reports EFAULT because no wait region can be registered); the sigmask is ARMED (not swapped) so a handler runs under it and EINTR keeps it; relative and absolute timeouts report ETIME, downgraded to success when completions are reapable; `min_wait_usec` is a real batching window. A full CQ no longer stops submission: completions overflow to a backlog and `IORING_SQ_CQ_OVERFLOW` is raised (NODROP). Links, hard links, drain barriers and CQE_SKIP_SUCCESS are implemented as a tested state machine; a broken link cancels the rest of its chain with ECANCELED. R_DISABLED rings report EBADFD, SINGLE_ISSUER rings EEXIST to a second task. Concurrency: operations no longer run with the ring spinlock held, and a submission batch is serialised by a sleeping lock. REMAINING: no async engine, so SQPOLL/IOPOLL, FAST_POLL retry, multishot re-arm, TIMEOUT/LINK_TIMEOUT/ASYNC_CANCEL entries and in-flight cancellation are absent (they need kernel worker threads + a request object model); ENTER_REGISTERED_RING needs a per-task registered-ring array in `sched::Task`. |

| 428 | common | `open_tree` | `sys_open_tree` | vfs | dcache, fs-context, mntns, permissions, procfs, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:12` | `IMPL` | `B713-linux-path-identity`, `B1582`, `B1948` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/namespace.c, fs/fsopen.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | B810 audit: PARTIAL. open_tree creates detached clones, but full flag validation, recursive non-clone behavior, nofollow/noautomount semantics, namespace checks, and non-clone mount identity remain incomplete. Closed in `B1582`: `open_tree` had no flag validation at all — every bit was accepted, `AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW`/`AT_NO_AUTOMOUNT` were silently dropped (so `open_tree` on a symlink-to-mount cloned the mount), `AT_RECURSIVE` without `OPEN_TREE_CLONE` was accepted, `may_mount` ran after the walk, and none of the three `__do_loopback` EINVAL rungs (unbindable / cross-namespace / locked-children) existed. Ungated `open_tree_policy` (12 tests) now owns the valid mask and derivation; `vfs::mount::may_clone_mount_tree` owns the loopback ladder. `LookupFlags` gained a real `no_automount` bit gating `d_automount` — `AT_NO_AUTOMOUNT` was previously unimplementable, not merely unwired. B1948: `OPEN_TREE_NAMESPACE` did not exist — the bit was unassigned in `OPEN_TREE_VALID`, so every use was EINVAL. It is the sibling of `FSMOUNT_NAMESPACE` and shares its constructor: the namespace arm calls the SAME `vfs::mount::create_new_namespace` fsmount's does, with a copied mount subtree on top instead of a fresh mount over a superblock. Two flag rules the reference has and we did not: `AT_RECURSIVE` is satisfied by EITHER tree-copying flag \(it was CLONE-only\), and CLONE together with NAMESPACE is EINVAL because they name two different things to hand back. The privilege selection \(`ns_capable\(current_user_ns\)` for the namespace form, `may_mount` for the clone form, nothing for the plain O_PATH-like form\) now reuses `fsmount_abi::Privilege`/`FsmountCaps` rather than a second spelling of the same pair, and runs BEFORE the walk. Evidence: `open_tree_policy` unit tests 12 -> 17; `vfs/tests/mount_fsmount_namespace.rs::the_open_tree_form_puts_a_copy_of_an_existing_tree_on_the_same_root_copy` and `::an_unbindable_source_is_refused_by_the_open_tree_form`. POSITIVE CONTROL: dropping `OPEN_TREE_NAMESPACE` from the valid mask turns 4 unit tests RED. x86_64 + aarch64 kernel checks clean. |

B1582 verified SET_PATH uses the aux dirfd via `FsParameter::path_at`, SET_BINARY has a real blob path, and CREATE_EXCL routes to `vfs_get_tree_exclusive`. B1650: mount_capable was NEVER called on CMD_CREATE - unprivileged userns could realize a superblock mount\(2\) refuses; CMD_RECONFIGURE had no capability check; over-long keys silently truncated. F791 re-verified the whole row and CORRECTS its GAP text: SET_FD/SET_BINARY/SET_PATH are no longer rejected by the syscall - the fd is pinned, the blob copied and the dirfd resolved correctly, then the classic parser rejects the file, filename and blob value types BEFORE the filesystem's parameter table is consulted, and no production filesystem declares an Fd- or Path-typed parameter anyway \(proc is the only registration carrying a table, and it is empty\). Our fuse still parses its fd out of the legacy comma string, so an FSCONFIG_SET_FD on fuse is EINVAL where Linux mounts. The second half of the GAP is unchanged and re-verified: FsContext::log has six live producers reached from fsconfig\(2\) and its only readers are in a test, and read\(2\) on the fscontext fd returns EINVAL rather than one message per read with ENODATA on empty and EMSGSIZE on a short buffer. The user-copy stage moved out of the kernel-gated slot into `fsconfig_fetch`, behind a `UserCopy` trait, so every EFAULT rung is now reachable hosted and the key-before-value ORDER is pinned. The namespace dimension is `mount_capable`, which was inside a module-level `#![cfg(target_os = "oxide-kernel")]` tree while its own comment claimed otherwise; it is extracted to `syscalls/src/mount_capable.rs`, one definition, and tested. Evidence: `syscalls/tests/fsconfig_fetch_hosted.rs` 11, `syscalls/tests/mount_capable_userns_hosted.rs` 4, `vfs/tests/fs_context_log.rs` 12 \(was 9\). POSITIVE CONTROLS, each reinstated then reverted: value-before-key RED, path-value-requires-UTF-8 RED, blob-as-C-string RED, `mount_capable` collapsed to `mnt_user_ns` RED \(3/4\), empty ring returning `Ok(0)` RED, EMSGSIZE truncating instead of queueing RED. x86_64 + aarch64 kernel checks clean. |
| 432 | common | `fsmount` | `sys_fsmount` | vfs | dcache, fs-context, mntns, permissions, procfs, syscall-abi, usercopy, vfs | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:9` | `IMPL` | `B1650`, `F791`, `B1941`, `B1948` | `arch/x86/entry/syscalls/syscall_64.tbl, fs/namespace.c, fs/fsopen.c` | `mount-namespace-vfs-model` | B1478 replaced require_sys_admin with may_mount = ns_capable\(mnt_ns->user_ns, CAP_SYS_ADMIN\) shared across the whole mount family, and added the missing mount_capable FS_USERNS_MOUNT gate. F791 re-verified every clause of the GAP and confirms it verbatim: the returned fd wraps an anon-inode mount object, not an O_PATH fd over a real vfsmount in an anonymous mount namespace. It carries mnt_id 0, is a regular file so it is ENOTDIR as a dirfd \(no openat, fchdir, fstatat, or path-resolving move_mount arm\), and is invisible to statmount/listmount; between fsmount and move_mount the mount exists nowhere and can be neither observed nor referenced. move_mount mode \(a\) grafts the superblock through a private-inode side channel and reconstructs mnt_flags at attach time, doing the work fsmount should already have done. No hosted test covers the syscall pipeline at all - all five slot files are target-gated; the closest is a VFS-level get_tree-then-attach_sb proxy. B1941 closes every gap this row listed, and records two NEW narrower ones, so it stays NEEDS-REWORK. Implemented: `FSMOUNT_NAMESPACE` \(present in the reference; the earlier 'not in 6.19' note was stale, and the anonymous-namespace rework that made it implementable had already landed\) - it selects a DIFFERENT privilege, `ns_capable\(current_user_ns\)` rather than `may_mount`, and returns a mount-namespace descriptor; the post-creation no-user-mount superblock check \(`SB_NOUSER` did not exist in the superblock flag space and is added\); the mandatory-locking warning; and the too-revealing diagnostic on the context log. Dissolve-on-close was already correct. Every decision that does not need a mount tree moved out of the kernel-gated slot into `syscalls/src/fsmount_abi.rs`; the slot is now sample-capabilities / take-the-lock / build / install. The namespace constructor shares ONE body with the anonymous one \(`vfs::mount::create_ns_mount` / `create_anon_mount`\) and returns the namespace reference, because the mount must NOT retain its own namespace - the arena retains the mount and the namespace's teardown empties the arena, so retaining it is a cycle nothing breaks. Evidence: `syscalls/tests/fsmount_admission_hosted.rs` 9, `vfs/tests/mount_fsmount_namespace.rs` 6. POSITIVE CONTROLS, each reinstated then reverted: privilege split removed RED, `SB_NOUSER` check removed RED, atime exactly-one rule replaced by a bit mask RED, mandlock warning silenced RED, namespace form allocating an ANONYMOUS namespace RED, ENOTDIR root check removed RED, mount retaining its own namespace RED. REMAINING: the named namespace has the new mount as its ROOT where the reference copies the caller's namespace root and overmounts the new tree on it; and the reference's slave-propagation transition for a caller whose user namespace differs from its mount namespace's owner is not applied \(the locked-attribute stamp is\). Both in `scratch/issues.d/B1941-fsconfig-fsmount-linux-parity.md`. x86_64 + aarch64 kernel checks clean. B1948 closes both REMAINING gaps and the row promotes. The namespace is now rooted on a COPY of the caller's namespace root with the new tree mounted on top of that copy, so unmounting the top leaves a root underneath instead of an empty namespace; and a caller whose user namespace differs from its mount namespace's owner gets SLAVE copies plus the `lock_mnt_tree` freeze over the whole new tree \(namespace root excluded, since that is the one node whose removal reveals nothing\). Both live in ONE constructor, `vfs::mount::create_new_namespace`, shared with `open_tree\(OPEN_TREE_NAMESPACE\)` \(row 428\) — the two syscalls differ only in what goes on top. The cross-user-namespace freeze moved out of the slot with it: `lock_new_mount_bits` was being applied to BOTH forms, which the reference does not do \(the anonymous form's mount is destined for the caller's own tree\), and it is deleted. Evidence: `vfs/tests/mount_fsmount_namespace.rs` 6 -> 11. POSITIVE CONTROLS, each reinstated then reverted: pointing `ns_set_root` at the top mount 5 RED; forcing `CloneType::Private` 1 RED; removing the `lock_mnt_ns` freeze 1 RED. x86_64 + aarch64 kernel checks clean; `cargo test -p vfs` / `-p fs` / `-p syscalls` 0-failed. |

Deterministic failed-clone, concurrent-exit, open/reap/reuse, and process/thread-selection schedules cover the lifetime boundary. `B1967` closed the pidfs ioctl surface and three namespace-relative answers. All ten `PIDFD_GET_*_NAMESPACE` descriptors were absent, so a caller holding only a pidfd and no procfs could not reach the target's namespaces at all; they now return the SAME nsfs node a `/proc/<pid>/ns/<type>` walk lands on, retaining the same owner, so `setns` accepts either interchangeably instead of there being a second way to name a namespace — guarded by the fs-credential ptrace read check, `EINVAL` on a non-zero arg, `ESRCH` for a gone target, and an `O_CLOEXEC` descriptor. `PIDFD_GET_INFO` had drifted a struct version behind and wrote `supported_mask` at the offset the published layout gives `coredump_code`, so a current caller read one field as another; the full layout is now carried, with `cgroupid` and the coredump group added, and the result mask trimmed to the fields the caller's struct can hold — a bit set for a field that did not fit is an instruction to read uninitialised memory. Three answers were wrong rather than missing: `pid`/`tgid` came from the TARGET's pid namespace while `ppid` already came from the reader's, so one reply mixed numbers from two namespaces; fdinfo folded 'alive but unnameable here' onto `Pid: -1`, telling a sibling-namespace reader a running process had exited, where the answer is `0` and `NSpid:` always carries the same leading number; and a pidfd passed into an unrelated pid namespace answered as if the target were visible instead of `EREMOTE` (which the errno table did not carry). The coredump verdict is latched on the PID identity when the process faces the dump decision, BEFORE any refusal arm, so a supervisor that polls for exit can still separate a crash whose dump was skipped from a process that never crashed — nothing else survives that long. Command decode, the struct-size gate and the coredump mask arithmetic live in ungated owners because the ioctl file is kernel-only. Hosted: `pidfs_ioctl` 10, `coredump::dumpable` 3; positive controls: shifting a namespace command number, advertising `supported_mask` one version early, and claiming a refused dump happened each turn tests red. Harness: 4564/0 across sched, syscalls, syscall, fs, nscg, pidfd; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 143s, smoke-arm PASS 181s. REMAINING: namespace-destruction differential coverage. |

| 438 | common | `pidfd_getfd` | `sys_pidfd_getfd` | sched | fd-table, namespaces, sched, signals, syscall-abi, task-lifetime, usercopy | `crates/kernel/syscalls/src/dispatch/route_b.rs:33` | `PARTIAL` | `B713-linux-path-identity` | `kernel/pid.c:SYSCALL_DEFINE3(pidfd_getfd), kernel/pid.c:pidfd_getfd, kernel/ptrace.c:__ptrace_may_access` | `sched-proc-signal-model` | Fixed Linux `flags == 0`, bad fd/non-pidfd admission, ptrace-shaped realcred/CAP_SYS_PTRACE foreign-access gate before cloning, Zombie target `ESRCH`, shared-file install, and mandatory close-on-exec result fd. `B1967` closed the `exec_update_lock` half. The earlier reading that no such lock existed was wrong — a writer-exclusion latch of that name was already taken by execve and Landlock TSYNC — but it had no READ side at all, so this syscall judged the target's credentials and then fetched the fd as two unlocked reads. A concurrent `execve` in between hands the caller a descriptor out of a process that has just become setuid or dropped dumpability: the check passed against credentials that no longer exist. The check and the fetch now run under the target's lock held for reading, as `__pidfd_fget` does. That required a sleeping reader-writer semaphore, which this kernel did not have: the write span covers the address-space swap, the fd-table unshare and the credential commit, which allocate, fault and may be preempted, so a spinning acquirer would spin through all of it. `sched::rwsem` adds one on the same gate-plus-waitlist discipline as the existing sleeping mutex, writer-pessimistic so a reader stream cannot starve exec; its gate arithmetic is ungated and hosted-tested while only the blocking acquires sit behind the scheduler's cfg. The dumpability gate and the LSM tail were re-read and are both already present in the shared ptrace ladder this slot calls, so the remainder named only the lock. Hosted: `sched::rwsem` 5; positive controls: widening either admission predicate (letting a reader past a queued writer, or a writer alongside readers) turns two red. Harness: 4564/0 across sched, syscalls, syscall, fs, nscg, pidfd; both arches build; stack-gate PASS; smoke-x86 PASS 143s, smoke-arm PASS 181s. REMAINING: pid namespace lifetime harness. |

B1970 re-verified the credential half and it is already COMPLETE: the temporary override switches to the real ids, and the capability handling — including the carry-through when the no-setuid-fixup secure bit is set — is implemented in an ungated module with tests. The stated remainder was the revalidation retry, which was real but TREE-WIDE rather than specific to this slot: no `*at` caller ever re-walked on a stale handle, although the VFS already carried the revalidate lookup flag and ran the revalidate hook. It is now a shared wrapper at the `*at` resolution layer, so every caller inherits it — one walk, and on a stale handle exactly one more with revalidation forced, whose result is final. Two independent bounds (the already-revalidating guard and a hard retry counter) mean neither alone can loop. 13 hosted tests; positive controls for retry-never, retry-forever via the guard, retry-forever unbounded, retry-without-the-flag, and a bypassed wiring, each RED.
